**1.1.1**  Agni I laud, the divine household-priest of the sacrifice, the seasonal officiant, the Hotṛ, the most generous bestower of treasures.

**1.1.2**  Agni, lauded by the ancient seers and by those of today — may he convey the gods hither.

**1.1.3**  Through Agni may one attain wealth and prosperity day by day — wealth glorious and abounding in heroes.

**1.1.4**  O Agni, whatever unblemished sacrifice you encompass on all sides — that one truly reaches the gods.

**1.1.5**  May Agni the Hotṛ, of poet-wisdom, true, most rich in varied fame, come hither as a god among the gods.

**1.1.6**  Whatever good, O Agni, you will bestow upon the worshipper — that is indeed your truth, O Aṅgiras.

**1.1.7**  To you, O Agni, day by day, at the dusk and at the dawn, we come with prayer bearing homage.

**1.1.8**  To him who reigns over the sacrifices, the guardian of ṛta who shines bright, who grows mighty in his own house —

**1.1.9**  Be gracious to us, O Agni, as a father to his son. Be our companion for our well-being.

**1.2.1**  O Vāyu, come here — these Soma-draughts are made ready for you, who are beautiful to behold. Drink of them; hear our invocation.

**1.2.2**  O Vāyu, the praisers extol you with hymns; those praisers whose Soma is pressed, who know the days of the sacrifice.

**1.2.3**  O Vāyu, your abundant stream of inspiration goes forth to the worshipper, wide-ranging, for the drinking of Soma.

**1.2.4**  O Indra and Vāyu, these Soma-juices are pressed; come with your delights, for the Soma-drops long for you both.

**1.2.5**  O Vāyu and Indra, you two are keen-minded for the pressed juices, O lords rich in vigour. Come swiftly, both of you.

**1.2.6**  O Vāyu and Indra, come to the pressing of one who extracts the Soma; come quickly hither with your thought, O heroes.

**1.2.7**  I invoke Mitra of pure power and Varuṇa the consumer of enemies, as they accomplish the inspiration shining with ghee.

**1.2.8**  Mitra and Varuṇa, who grow great by ṛta and touch ṛta — through ṛta you two attain lofty wisdom.

**1.2.9**  Mitra and Varuṇa, who are seers, born of great might, dwelling in broad spaces — they two sustain active power and skill.

**1.3.1**  O Aśvins, lords of beauty with swift hands, lords of sacrificial boons, who enjoy much — find pleasure in these offerings.

**1.3.2**  O Aśvins, heroes of many wondrous deeds, with powerful thought, with minds attentive — take delight in our hymns.

**1.3.3**  O marvellous Nāsatyas, with your soma-draughts pressed, the sacred grass strewn — you whose chariot-tracks are those of Rudra — come hither.

**1.3.4**  O Indra, come hither, O you of radiant splendour; these Soma-juices, pressed for you, are purified through fine filters with the Soma plant.

**1.3.5**  O Indra, come hither, impelled by prayer, urged by the inspired singer, toward the one who has pressed the Soma, toward the sacred formulas of the sacrificer.

**1.3.6**  O Indra, come hither swiftly, O lord of the tawny steeds, toward our sacred formulas. At the pressing, establish your favour upon us.

**1.3.7**  O Viśvedevas — all-protecting, sustaining men — come here; grant boons to the worshipper's pressed Soma.

**1.3.8**  O all-gods, tireless and swift, come to the pressed Soma — as the shining ones come to their own dawns.

**1.3.9**  O Viśvedevas, unfailing, possessed of magical power, guileless — may the bearers delight in the oblation.

**1.3.10**  May Sarasvatī, the purifying one, rich in refreshments and victuals, she who is wealthy in insight — desire our sacrifice.

**1.3.11**  Sarasvatī, who impels the truthful words and awakens benevolent thoughts — she upholds the sacrifice.

**1.3.12**  Sarasvatī illumines with her ray the great flood of light; she shines brilliantly over all insights.

**1.4.1**  We invoke day by day, for help, Indra who fashions beautiful forms — even as one milks a good milch-cow.

**1.4.2**  Come to our pressings, O drinker of Soma, and drink the Soma. Truly, that exhilaration grants cows and riches.

**1.4.3**  May we know those who are nearest to you, your most gracious intentions. Do not pass us by — come hither.

**1.4.4**  Approach Indra the unconquered, the strong one; ask of him who is full of wisdom — he who has the most excellent gift for his friends.

**1.4.5**  And let our adversaries speak of us only with respect, no matter where they come from — holding Indra alone as their lord.

**1.4.6**  And may even the enemy call us fortunate, O marvellous one; may the peoples call us so. May we dwell in Indra's shelter.

**1.4.7**  Come, bring the swift one to the swift, the glory of the sacrifice, the joy of men, the friend who exhilarates as he speeds along.

**1.4.8**  Having drunk of this, O Śatakratu, you became the slayer of the Vṛtras; you aided the vigorous one in battles for booty.

**1.4.9**  You, O Śatakratu, vigorous in the battles, we impel to victory — O Indra, for the winning of riches.

**1.4.10**  Sing to that Indra — who is the great protector of wealth, of easy passage, the friend of the Soma-presser.

**1.5.1**  Come, sit down here; sing forth to Indra, O companions who carry the hymns of praise.

**1.5.2**  Indra, the most bountiful of the bountiful, lord of desirable goods — him we honour together with Soma when pressed.

**1.5.3**  May he be present at our yoking, at our wealth, at our full abundance; may he come to us with vigours.

**1.5.4**  Sing to that Indra — in whose presence his two tawny steeds are not restrained even in battles by enemies.

**1.5.5**  For the drinker of the pressed Soma, these bright juices, pressed and mingled with curds, go forth for his enjoyment.

**1.5.6**  You, O Indra, grew mighty instantly for the drinking of the pressed Soma — for greatness and supremacy, O Sukratu.

**1.5.7**  May the swift Soma-draughts enter into you, O Indra, who art praised by song; may they bring you joy, O wise one.

**1.5.8**  The hymns of praise have magnified you, the recitations have magnified you, O Śatakratu. May our songs magnify you.

**1.5.9**  Indra, with his inexhaustible aid, shall win this thousandfold booty in which all heroic powers reside.

**1.5.10**  Let no mortal man do harm to our persons, O Indra, praised by songs. As master, ward off the weapon.

**1.6.1**  They harness the tawny, ruddy one as he moves around those who stand firm; the luminous lights blaze in the sky.

**1.6.2**  They harness his two lovely tawny steeds, wide-winged, to his chariot — the two ruddy, bold ones, the man-carriers.

**1.6.3**  Making a beacon for the beaconless, form for the formless, O hero — you were born together with the dawns.

**1.6.4**  Then, following their inherent power, they were set in motion toward renewed germination — bearing the name worthy of sacrifice.

**1.6.5**  Even what was hard to break you split, even what was hidden, O Indra, with your carriers; you found the cows that were in the cave.

**1.6.6**  As those who worship the gods send their hymns toward the one who discovers wealth — so have they sung of the great, far-famed one.

**1.6.7**  For you are seen together with Indra — united, without fear, both exhilarating and of equal glory.

**1.6.8**  The liberal one sings brilliantly with his blameless, heavenly ones, with the lovely troops of Indra.

**1.6.9**  Come hither, O all-pervading one, from this place or from the shining vault of heaven. The hymns meet in him.

**1.6.10**  From here or from the heaven or from the earthly realm, or from the vast expanse — we implore Indra for his bounty.

**1.7.1**  The singers with their great songs, the chanters with their chants, the melodists with their melodies — all have sung of Indra.

**1.7.2**  Indra is together with his two tawny steeds, yoked by the word, all-commingled. Indra wields the vajra and is golden.

**1.7.3**  For far-reaching sight, Indra set the sun to rise in the sky; he released the light-cows from the rock.

**1.7.4**  O Indra, protect us in the battles and in the contests for a thousandfold prize — O mighty one, with your mighty aids.

**1.7.5**  We invoke Indra in the great prize-contest and in the small — the vajra-wielder as our ally in the battles against Vṛtras.

**1.7.6**  O mighty bull, give us that beautiful offering; grant it completely, the all-giver who is never thwarted.

**1.7.7**  At each pressing after pressing, the praises of Indra the vajra-wielder grow greater; I find no end to his laudation.

**1.7.8**  Like a bull leading the herd to the stall, he sets the peoples in motion by his power — the lord, who is never thwarted.

**1.7.9**  He who alone rules over men and over riches — Indra, lord of the five peoples.

**1.7.10**  We invoke Indra from all sides, beyond all peoples; may he be ours alone.

**1.8.1**  O Indra, bring us wealth that wins, that ever conquers, ever prevails — the most excellent wealth for our aid.

**1.8.2**  By which, with a fist-blow, we may utterly check the Vṛtras — we who are aided by you, with your chariot-horse.

**1.8.3**  O Indra, aided by you, we would take the vajra as a weapon of smiting; may we conquer our rivals in battle.

**1.8.4**  We, with our heroic archers, with you, O Indra, as our ally — we would overpower those who strive against us in battle.

**1.8.5**  Great is Indra now and beyond, and great let his greatness be for the vajra-wielder — his power as broad as the sky.

**1.8.6**  Whether in battle or in the gaining of offspring, those heroes who are inspired and intent on prayer — they attain him.

**1.8.7**  His belly, which contains the most Soma, swells like the ocean — like the broad waters at their crest.

**1.8.8**  For such is his bounty, joyful, mighty, rich in cows — like a ripe branch bending down to the worshipper.

**1.8.9**  For thus, O Indra, your manifold aids are present even now, immediately, for me your devoted worshipper.

**1.8.10**  For such is his desirable hymn of praise and the recitation worthy to be proclaimed — for Indra, for the drinking of Soma.

**1.9.1**  Come, O Indra, rejoice in the Soma-drink with all its Soma-portions; the great invocatory power comes to you with might.

**1.9.2**  Release him — the exhilarating one — toward Indra at the pressing, for Indra who delights in exhilaration, the performer of all deeds.

**1.9.3**  Be exhilarated, O fair-jawed one, by the exhilarating praises, O you who look upon all men; be present at these pressings.

**1.9.4**  They have poured forth your praises, O Indra; they have raised themselves up toward you — eagerly, toward the bull-lord.

**1.9.5**  Stir hither toward us a wondrous, excellent bounty, O Indra; may it be yours, pervasive and mighty.

**1.9.6**  O Indra, of great brilliance, of great fame — impel us there, toward wealth, toward the eager and the glorious.

**1.9.7**  O Indra, grant us broad, mighty fame together with cows and vigour — lasting for all ages, inexhaustible.

**1.9.8**  Grant us great fame, O Indra, and the brilliance most fruitful of thousands — and those chariot-borne refreshments.

**1.9.9**  Let us praise with songs Indra, lord of goods, master of riches, worthy of the Ṛgveda-hymns — the one who comes to offer his aid.

**1.9.10**  At each and every pressing, the praiser who abides at home sings for Indra a great, great song of heroic power.

**1.10.1**  The Gāyatrī-singers sing to you; the Arka-chanters hymn the Arka to you; the Brāhmaṇas, O Śatakratu, have raised you up like a bamboo pole.

**1.10.2**  As he climbs from ridge to ridge, discerning the many deeds to be done — Indra perceives the purpose and goes forth with his troop, the bull.

**1.10.3**  Harness your two maned, tawny stallions, vigorous, filling the girth-strap; then come, O Indra, drinker of Soma, within hearing of our songs.

**1.10.4**  Come, resound toward the praises, roar forth toward us; attend to our sacred utterance, O Indra, O master, and strengthen our sacrifice.

**1.10.5**  A recitation praiseworthy and magnifying for Indra who dwells in many places — so that Śakra may rejoice in our pressings and in our friendships.

**1.10.6**  Him we implore for friendship, him for wealth, him for valour of heroes; may Śakra — and Indra while sharing his riches — be able to help us.

**1.10.7**  Well-spread and well-brightened — truly the glory given by you, O Indra; open the stall of the cows; O boulder-wielder, bestow your bounty.

**1.10.8**  Neither of the two worlds can constrain you as you thunder; win the sun-bright waters; shake the cows together for us.

**1.10.9**  O you whose ears listen to our cry — hear now my invocation; receive my songs, O Indra; make this my hymn the most intimate of allies.

**1.10.10**  For we know you as the most virile bull in the battles, heard when invoked; of this most virile one we implore the aid most fruitful of thousands.

**1.10.11**  Come now, O Indra of the Kuśika clan, exhilarated, and drink the pressed Soma; prolong a new life for us; make the seer win a thousandfold.

**1.10.12**  May these songs encompass you on all sides, O lover of praise; as the grown ones follow the elder, may the beloved praises be pleasing.

**1.11.1**  All hymns, vast as the ocean, have magnified Indra — the best charioteer among charioteers, the true lord of prizes, the sovereign of all.

**1.11.2**  In friendship with you, O Indra, in your companionship of strength-bestowing bounty, may we not fear, O lord of might. We laud you who are the unconquered conqueror.

**1.11.3**  Indra's many gifts and his aids do not fail, whenever he bestows the wealth of cattle along with prizes upon those who praise him.

**1.11.4**  He was born as the breaker of fortresses, young and wise, of immeasurable might — Indra, the upholder of every action, the wielder of the vajra, lauded by many.

**1.11.5**  You, O stone-armed one, uncovered the cave of Vala filled with cattle. The gods, themselves fearless, revealed you as you were being urged forward.

**1.11.6**  By your bounties, O hero, I have approached, singing aloud, the river. Those who delight in your praises, O praise-lover, have drawn near, knowing your might.

**1.11.7**  By your wiles, O Indra, you have overthrown Śuṣṇa the wily. The wise who know this deed of yours — lift up their glories.

**1.11.8**  The praises have sung forth to Indra who rules by his might — he whose gifts are a thousand, or even more.

**1.12.1**  We choose Agni as our messenger, the hotar of all wisdom, the one of fine insight for this sacrifice.

**1.12.2**  They ever invoke Agni upon Agni with offerings — the lord of the house, the carrier of oblations, the one greatly beloved.

**1.12.3**  O Agni, being born, bring hither the gods for him who has strewn the sacred grass. You are the hotar worthy of our praise.

**1.12.4**  Awaken those eager gods when you go, O Agni, on your mission as messenger. Sit down on the sacred grass with the gods.

**1.12.5**  O Agni, you who blaze and are invoked with ghee — burn away those who assail us. Protect us, O Agni.

**1.12.6**  Agni is kindled by Agni — the seer, the young lord of the household, the carrier of oblations, he whose mouth is the ladle.

**1.12.7**  Praise Agni the seer, who upholds the true ordinance at the sacrifice — the god who drives away disease.

**1.12.8**  Whosoever, as lord of offerings, honours you, O Agni, as his divine messenger — be his protector and helper.

**1.12.9**  He who, bearing oblations, attends upon Agni for the enjoyment of the gods — show grace to him, O Pāvaka.

**1.12.10**  So blaze for us, O Pāvaka Agni, and bring the gods hither to our sacrifice and our oblation.

**1.12.11**  Being praised by us, bring to us with the ever-new gāyatra hymn wealth rich in heroes, and nourishment.

**1.12.12**  O Agni, with your bright flame and with all the invocations of the gods, accept this hymn of praise from us.

**1.13.1**  Well kindled, O Agni, bring the gods hither to the one who offers oblations. O hotar, O Pāvaka, worship them too.

**1.13.2**  O Tanūnapāt, O seer, make our sweet sacrifice a delight for the gods today.

**1.13.3**  I invoke here the beloved Narāśaṁsa at this sacrifice — he of honeyed tongue, formed by oblations.

**1.13.4**  O Agni, on your most pleasant chariot bring hither the gods who are praised. You are the hotar established by Manu.

**1.13.5**  Let the wise strew the sacred grass in succession, ghee-backed, where is the seat of the immortal.

**1.13.6**  Let the divine doors, those that augment ṛta, ever-flowing, swing open — for the worship offered today and henceforth.

**1.13.7**  Night and Dawn, beautifully adorned — I invoke them both at this sacrifice to sit upon our sacred grass.

**1.13.8**  I invoke the two eloquent divine hotars, seers both — may they worship this sacrifice of ours.

**1.13.9**  Iḷā, Sarasvatī, and Mahī — three goddesses bestowing bliss — let them, unfailing, take their seats on the sacred grass.

**1.13.10**  I invoke here Tvaṣṭar, the foremost, of all forms — may he belong to us alone.

**1.13.11**  O divine Vanaspati, release the oblation toward the gods. May there be discernment for the giver.

**1.13.12**  With svāhā, make the sacrifice in the house of the worshipper for Indra. There I invoke the gods.

**1.14.1**  O Agni, with these ministrations and hymns, come with all the gods for the drinking of Soma, and worship.

**1.14.2**  The Kaṇvas have called to you; the inspired ones declare your praises. Come, O Agni, with the gods.

**1.14.3**  Indra and Vāyu, Bṛhaspati, Mitra and Agni, Pūṣan and Bhaga, the Ādityas, and the host of Maruts.

**1.14.4**  The Soma drops are being pressed for you — exhilarating, causing rapture, drops of sweetness that settle in the cups.

**1.14.5**  The Kaṇvas, seeking your aid, who have strewn the sacred grass, bearing offerings, fully prepared — they praise you.

**1.14.6**  The carriers whose backs are anointed with ghee, yoked by the mind, who convey you — they bring the gods for the drinking of Soma.

**1.14.7**  Make those worthy of sacrifice, those augmenters of ṛta, come with their consorts, O Agni. Give them to drink of the sweet Soma, O you of good tongue.

**1.14.8**  Those who are worthy of sacrifice, those who are praiseworthy — let them drink by means of your tongue, O Agni, with the call of vaṣaṭ, from the sweet draught.

**1.14.9**  The inspired hotar will bring hither all the gods who awake at dawn from the luminous realm of the sun.

**1.14.10**  O Agni, drink the sweet Soma with all the gods, with Indra, with Vāyu, with the established ordinances of Mitra.

**1.14.11**  You, O Agni, are the hotar established by Manu; you take your seat at the sacrifices. Worship this our non-violent sacrifice.

**1.14.12**  Yoke the ruddy mares to the chariot and the bay steeds, O divine Rohita. With them bring the gods hither.

**1.15.1**  Drink the Soma in due order, O Indra; let the drops enter you — those exhilarating ones, those that are at home in that.

**1.15.2**  O Maruts, drink in due order from the purifier's vessel; purify the sacrifice. You are, indeed, great givers.

**1.15.3**  Celebrate our sacrifice, O Neṣṭar; drink in due order with the wives of the gods. For you are indeed the one who holds treasure.

**1.15.4**  O Agni, bring the gods hither and seat them in the three stations. Attend on them, and drink in due order.

**1.15.5**  From the brāhmaṇa's bounty, O Indra, drink the Soma through the succession of seasons. Truly, your friendship is undiminished.

**1.15.6**  You two, O Mitra and Varuṇa, who hold firm your vows, who guide insight that is hard to attain — you two obtain the sacrifice in due season.

**1.15.7**  Those who are wealthy and give wealth, pressing-stone in hand at the sacrifice, they praise the god at the sacrifices.

**1.15.8**  May Draviṇodas give us the treasures which are renowned. We shall win those among the gods.

**1.15.9**  Draviṇodas desires to drink; pour the oblation and advance. Be sent forth through the seasons from the Neṣṭar's cup.

**1.15.10**  When we worship you, O Draviṇodas, as the fourth through the seasons — then indeed become a giver to us.

**1.15.11**  O Aśvins, drink the honey — you whose fire blazes, of pure vows, who carry the sacrifice in due season.

**1.15.12**  By the fire of the gārhapatya, O truthful one, you are the guide of the sacrifice in due season. Worship the gods for the god-devoted worshipper.

**1.16.1**  May the bay steeds bring you, O bull among gods, for the drinking of Soma, O Indra — you who are seen by the sun.

**1.16.2**  These grain offerings, dripping with ghee — the two bays shall bring Indra hither on his most pleasant chariot.

**1.16.3**  We invoke Indra at dawn, we invoke Indra at the advance of the sacrifice, we invoke Indra for the drinking of Soma.

**1.16.4**  Come to our pressed Soma, O Indra, with your flowing-maned bay steeds. For at the pressing we invoke you.

**1.16.5**  Come to this our hymn of praise, and to this pressing of the Soma. Drink like a thirsty bull.

**1.16.6**  These are the Soma drops, pressed and set out upon the sacred grass. Drink them, O Indra, for your strength.

**1.16.7**  May this hymn, foremost of all, touch your heart and be most pleasing to you. Then drink the pressed Soma.

**1.16.8**  Indra, the slayer of Vṛtra, goes to every pressing of the Soma that has been pressed, for exhilaration and for drinking.

**1.16.9**  Fill up this our desire with cattle and horses, O you of a hundred powers. We who are devoted to deep thought praise you.

**1.17.1**  I take refuge with Indra and Varuṇa, the two universal sovereigns. May they two show us grace in such need as this.

**1.17.2**  For you two come to the aid of the call of the inspired poet who praises you. You are the upholders of the peoples.

**1.17.3**  O Indra and Varuṇa, gratify us to our heart's desire with wealth. We approach you two most closely.

**1.17.4**  May we be among those whose mighty deeds belong to you two, whose benevolent thoughts belong to you two, among those who receive gifts of prize.

**1.17.5**  Indra becomes the inspiration worthy of hymn among those who give a thousandfold; Varuṇa becomes so among those worthy of praise.

**1.17.6**  By the protection of those two alone may we gain victory and lay low our foes. May there be an overflowing gift.

**1.17.7**  O Indra and Varuṇa, I invoke you two for brilliant bounty. Make us thoroughly victorious.

**1.17.8**  O Indra and Varuṇa, now at this moment in the thoughts that are striving to reach you — grant shelter to us.

**1.17.9**  O Indra and Varuṇa, may the perfect praise which I call upon reach you — the joint hymn of praise at which you two rejoice.

**1.18.1**  O Bṛhaspati, make the Soma drinker resound — Kakṣīvat, the son of Uśij.

**1.18.2**  He who is wealthy, who drives away disease, who finds prosperity, who augments well-being — may that swift one attend upon us.

**1.18.3**  May the harmful intent of a malicious mortal not overcome us. Protect us, O Bṛhaspati.

**1.18.4**  That hero truly does not come to harm whom Indra and Bṛhaspati and Soma urge forward.

**1.18.5**  You, O Bṛhaspati, and Soma, and Indra — together with Dakṣiṇā guard that mortal from distress.

**1.18.6**  I have approached the wondrous lord of the assembly, the beloved and dear to Indra, seeking from him wisdom and intelligence.

**1.18.7**  Without whom the sacrifice does not succeed, not even that of the wise man — he, Bṛhaspati, controls the yoking of inspired thoughts.

**1.18.8**  He brings to completion the preparation of offerings, makes the sacrifice proceed forward; the hotrā rite goes to the gods.

**1.18.9**  I have beheld Narāśaṁsa most bold and most widely famed, shining like the dwelling-place of heaven.

**1.19.1**  You are invoked for the protection of this dear sacrifice. Come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.19.2**  No god, no mortal, surpasses your great might. Come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.19.3**  Who know the great firmament, all the gods without deceit — come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.19.4**  Who in their might have reached toward the sun's hymn, irresistible in power — come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.19.5**  Who are brilliant, of terrible forms, lords of fine dominion, destroyers of enemies — come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.19.6**  Who sit as gods in heaven upon the shining vault of the sky — come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.19.7**  Who cause the mountains to tremble, across the surging ocean's flood — come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.19.8**  Who stretch their rays of light with might across the ocean — come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.19.9**  I release the sweet Soma drink to you for the first drinking. Come, O Agni, with the Maruts.

**1.20.1**  This hymn of praise has been fashioned with devotion by the inspired seers for the divine birth — the most treasure-laden of hymns.

**1.20.2**  Those who, for Indra, fashioned by mind the two bays joined by speech — the Ṛbhus — they attained the sacrifice with their labours.

**1.20.3**  They fashioned for the two Nāsatyas a chariot that travels everywhere and is pleasant. They fashioned the cow that yields milk in abundance.

**1.20.4**  The Ṛbhus, with true incantations and straight-going, made the two parents youthful again; they accomplished it through their skill.

**1.20.5**  Your intoxicating Soma-draughts have come together, along with Indra accompanied by the Maruts, and with the royal Ādityas.

**1.20.6**  And they fashioned again into four that single cup made by the divine Tvaṣṭar — making one into four.

**1.20.7**  Do you set treasures upon us — thrice seven in number — for the one who presses Soma, one by one with your perfect praises.

**1.20.8**  The Ṛbhus, as divine bearers, sustained all; by their excellent deeds they obtained a share worthy of sacrifice among the gods.

**1.21.1**  Here I invoke Indra and Agni together; it is their praise that we desire. Those two, who are the most avid drinkers of Soma, come drink the Soma.

**1.21.2**  Praise those two, Indra and Agni, at the sacrifices; adorn them, O men. Sing of those two in the Gāyatrī metres.

**1.21.3**  For the glory of Mitra, we invoke those two, Indra and Agni — those Soma-drinkers — to drink the Soma.

**1.21.4**  We invoke the two mighty ones for this pressing where the Soma is expressed. Let Indra and Agni come hither.

**1.21.5**  Those two great lords of the assembly, Indra and Agni, crush the demons. May those who devour without offspring remain childless.

**1.21.6**  By that truth, keep vigilant watch over the highly luminous station. Indra and Agni, grant us shelter.

**1.22.1**  Arouse the two who yoke their chariot at dawn — let the Aśvins come hither to drink of this Soma.

**1.22.2**  Those two gods with the excellent chariot, the most chariot-skilled of all, touching heaven — those two Aśvins we invoke.

**1.22.3**  That whip of yours, O Aśvins, honey-sweet and full of true, joyful speech — with it refresh the sacrifice.

**1.22.4**  There is no far distance for you, wherever you go by chariot — O Aśvins, the Soma-presser's house is not remote to you.

**1.22.5**  For our aid I invoke golden-handed Savitar; he it is who points out the station of the gods.

**1.22.6**  Praise Savitar, the child of the waters, for our protection; his ordinances we desire to follow.

**1.22.7**  We invoke the distributor of brilliant and various treasures, Savitar who looks upon all men.

**1.22.8**  Friends, be seated here — Savitar is now worthy of our praise; the giver adorns bountiful gifts.

**1.22.9**  O Agni, bring hither the willing consorts of the gods, and Tvaṣṭṛ, to drink the Soma.

**1.22.10**  O youngest Agni, bring the divine wives hither for our protection — Hotrā, Bhāratī, Varūtrī, and Dhiṣaṇā.

**1.22.11**  May the divine wives of heroes, with their unbroken wings, accompany us with great shelter and protection.

**1.22.12**  Here I invoke Indrāṇī and Varuṇānī for well-being, and Agnāyī to drink the Soma.

**1.22.13**  Let great Heaven and Earth refresh this our sacrifice; let them fill us with their nourishing powers.

**1.22.14**  The inspired sages, with their thoughts, lick the ghee-rich milk of those two at the firm station of the Gandharva.

**1.22.15**  Be gentle, O Earth, thornless and good for settling; grant us wide shelter.

**1.22.16**  From that quarter whence Viṣṇu strode, through the seven stations of the earth, may the gods protect us.

**1.22.17**  Viṣṇu strode across this universe; three times he planted his step; it is gathered together in his dust-filled footprint.

**1.22.18**  Viṣṇu, the undeceivable guardian, strode his three steps, upholding the cosmic laws from that foundation.

**1.22.19**  Behold the deeds of Viṣṇu, from whom he set in motion his ordinances — the worthy ally and friend of Indra.

**1.22.20**  That highest step of Viṣṇu the wise ones always behold, spread out like an eye in the sky.

**1.22.21**  The inspired and eloquent poets, ever wakeful, kindle together that highest step of Viṣṇu.

**1.23.1**  Come to the potent, mixed Soma juices, these that are pressed and ready. O Vāyu, drink of those that are set forth.

**1.23.2**  We invoke both gods who touch the sky, Indra and Vāyu, to drink of this Soma.

**1.23.3**  The inspired poets invoke for protection Indra and Vāyu, swift as thought, the thousand-eyed lords of wisdom.

**1.23.4**  We invoke Mitra and Varuṇa to drink the Soma — the two born with pure discernment.

**1.23.5**  Those two who by ṛta increase ṛta, the lords of the light of ṛta — those two, Mitra and Varuṇa, I invoke.

**1.23.6**  May Varuṇa be our promoter and Mitra with all his aids; may those two of excellent bounty act on our behalf.

**1.23.7**  We invoke Indra, accompanied by the Maruts, to drink the Soma; let him be satisfied together with the host.

**1.23.8**  The troops of Maruts with Indra as chief, the gods who give gifts like Pūṣan — all of you, hear my call.

**1.23.9**  You generous ones, slay Vṛtra along with Indra your mighty ally; let no evil-speaker rule over us.

**1.23.10**  We invoke all the gods, the Maruts, to drink the Soma — mighty they are, born of the dappled cow Pṛśni.

**1.23.11**  Like the thunder of conquerors, the rumble of the Maruts advances boldly — whatever splendor you possess, O heroes, as you go.

**1.23.12**  Born from the ha-sound and the lightning flash, may they protect us from that. May the Maruts be gracious to us.

**1.23.13**  O shining Pūṣan, bring back the brilliant sacrificial seat, the pillar of heaven, as a goat-herd leads back a lost animal.

**1.23.14**  The shining Pūṣan, the king, found the one hidden away, laid in a secret place — he found the one with the brilliant sacred grass.

**1.23.15**  And he has closely tracked it for me with the Soma-drops, with six yoked ones, as if ploughing barley with cattle.

**1.23.16**  The mothers go along the sacrificial paths, the sisters of the ritual performers, mixing their milk with honey.

**1.23.17**  Those waters yonder that are beside the sun, and those with whom the sun goes — let them speed our sacrifice.

**1.23.18**  I invoke the divine waters where our cattle drink; the oblation is to be made to the rivers.

**1.23.19**  Within the waters is ambrosia, in the waters is medicine; for the praise of the waters, O gods, be endowed with vigor.

**1.23.20**  Soma has told me that within the waters are all medicines, and Agni who is universally beneficent, and the waters who are universally healing.

**1.23.21**  O Waters, provide medicine and protection for my body, that I may long see the sun.

**1.23.22**  O Waters, carry away from me whatever evil there is in me, whether I have committed treachery, or cursed, or spoken falsehood.

**1.23.23**  Today I have moved through the waters; we have merged with their essence. O Agni, come full of milk; unite me with vital radiance.

**1.23.24**  O Agni, unite me with vital radiance, with offspring, with long life. May the gods know me for this; may Indra know it together with the seers.

**1.24.1**  Whose dear name among the immortals do we now celebrate? Who will give us back to great Aditi, so that I may see my father and my mother?

**1.24.2**  We celebrate the dear name of Agni, the first among the immortals. May he give us back to great Aditi, that I may see my father and my mother.

**1.24.3**  O god Savitar, master of all desirable things, always beneficent one, we beg our share.

**1.24.4**  For he who thus has diligently served you, Bhaga, is kept free from censure and without enmity in his hands.

**1.24.5**  We, who are partakers of your portion, Bhaga, would rise up through your aid; we take hold of the summit of wealth.

**1.24.6**  Not your dominion, nor your might, nor your anger — not even birds in flight have reached them. Not these unblinking, ever-flowing waters, nor those who do not diminish the force of the wind.

**1.24.7**  In the bottomless space, King Varuṇa of pure discernment holds up the crown of the tree upright; the rays are set downward, their roots are above; may their beams be implanted within us.

**1.24.8**  King Varuṇa has made wide the path for the sun to travel; he has placed feet for the footless to walk by, and also removed the heart-piercers.

**1.24.9**  O King, you have a hundred healers, a thousand; wide and deep be your benevolence. Drive away Nirṛti far from us; release us even from the sin we have committed.

**1.24.10**  Those stars set on high — seen by night — where do they go in the daytime? The inviolable ordinances of Varuṇa are displayed as the moon travels by night.

**1.24.11**  Praising you with sacred prayer I approach you; the sacrificer commissions you with oblations. Without offense, Varuṇa, be aware here; widely praised one, do not steal away our life.

**1.24.12**  That they say to me by night, that by day — this same message my heart discerns from within. He whom Śunaḥśepa called when bound — may that King Varuṇa release us.

**1.24.13**  For Śunaḥśepa called out when seized, when bound to three posts, upon the son of Aditi. May King Varuṇa release him; knowing, undeceived, may he loose the bonds.

**1.24.14**  With obeisances we seek to remove your wrath, O Varuṇa; with sacrifices and oblations we implore. Ruling over us, O Asura of deep wisdom, O King, you loosen the sins we have committed.

**1.24.15**  O Varuṇa, release from us the uppermost bond, loosen the lowermost and slacken the middle. Then, O son of Aditi, under your ordinance, may we be free of sin before Aditi.

**1.25.1**  Whatever ordinance of yours, O god Varuṇa, we transgress day by day, as men do —

**1.25.2**  Do not deliver us, when you are provoked, to the blow of death; do not deliver us to the anger of the one who is angry.

**1.25.3**  We would stretch out your mind toward granting grace, as a charioteer pulls in a horse — O Varuṇa, with songs of praise.

**1.25.4**  For my errant longings fly away in search of wealth, like birds toward their nests.

**1.25.5**  When shall we win Varuṇa the man of heroic glory, wide-seeing, who grants grace?

**1.25.6**  Both of them seek the same thing, longing for it without failing — for the steadfast-vowed one, to the worshipper who offers.

**1.25.7**  He knows the track of birds flying through the atmosphere; he knows the course of the ships on the sea.

**1.25.8**  Varuṇa of steadfast ordinances knows the twelve months with their offspring; he knows what is born in addition.

**1.25.9**  He knows the path of the wide, exalted, and great wind; he knows those who dwell on high.

**1.25.10**  Varuṇa of steadfast ordinances, the wise-minded one, has taken his seat in the dwellings for sovereign kingship.

**1.25.11**  From there, all-knowing, he looks down upon all marvels — what has been done and what is to be done.

**1.25.12**  May the wise-minded Āditya set us on good paths every day; may he prolong our lives.

**1.25.13**  Varuṇa wears a golden cloak and a shining garment; his spies have settled all around.

**1.25.14**  The deceivers do not deceive him; those who injure people, and the demons who assail him, cannot harm that god.

**1.25.15**  And whoever among men has spread his fame without limit — even within our midst.

**1.25.16**  My thoughts go forth seeking him, like cattle following the pasture path, seeking the wide-seeing one.

**1.25.17**  Let us speak together again, from whom honey has been brought for me; you enjoy the dear offering, like a priest at the rite.

**1.25.18**  Now let me behold the one visible to all, behold the chariot upon the earth; let these my songs find favor.

**1.25.19**  Hear this my call, O Varuṇa, and be gracious to me today; I come to you desiring your help.

**1.25.20**  You, the wise, reign over all of heaven and earth; hear me in your course of travel.

**1.25.21**  Release the uppermost bond from us, loosen the middle one; unloose the lowest ones, that we may live.

**1.26.1**  Put on the sacrificial garments, O Lord of food and strength, and perform this sacrifice of ours.

**1.26.2**  Let the excellent Hotṛ-priest be seated for us always, youngest Agni, with our hymns — with speech that reaches heaven.

**1.26.3**  For indeed the father sacrifices as a friend for his son, and for his family — a worthy friend to the friend.

**1.26.4**  Let Varuṇa, Mitra, and Aryaman — devourers of the wicked — take their seats on our sacred grass, as befits Manu's descendants.

**1.26.5**  O primal Hotṛ, rejoice in this rite of ours and in our friendship; hear well now these our songs.

**1.26.6**  However unceasingly we sacrifice to each god in turn, the oblation is indeed poured into you, O Agni.

**1.26.7**  May the lord of the clan, the joyful and excellent Hotṛ, be dear to us; may we who tend Agni well be dear to him.

**1.26.8**  For the gods have given us desirable boons because we tend Agni well; as tenders of Agni we take thought.

**1.26.9**  And so, O immortal one, let there be mutual praises between us mortals and you — between both parties.

**1.26.10**  O Agni, with all the fires, sustain this sacrifice and this speech; O son of strength, bestow delight.

**1.27.1**  We would praise you, O Agni, with reverences, as a horse adorned with a mane — sovereign king of all sacrificial rites.

**1.27.2**  May he — the son who goes forth broadly with strength, most beneficent — become our bountiful one, showering gifts.

**1.27.3**  May he protect us always, he who lives for all — from the mortal who would harm us from near and from far.

**1.27.4**  Now do announce this prize-song of ours, this newest Gāyatra hymn, O Agni, among the gods.

**1.27.5**  Grant us a share in the highest prizes, in the middle prizes; let us win a share of the innermost wealth.

**1.27.6**  You are the distributor, O brilliant-shining one, near at hand like the wave of a river; you flow immediately to the worshipper.

**1.27.7**  The mortal whom you aid in battles, O Agni, whom you speed in contests — he becomes the perpetual ruler of refreshments.

**1.27.8**  No one at all can overcome his prize — none can encompass it from any side; his vigor is worthy of fame.

**1.27.9**  May he, the all-pervading one, be the winner of the prize with swift steeds; may he be the winner of it with inspired singers.

**1.27.10**  O Agni, awakened by the elder's praise, kindle that visible hymn of praise to Rudra, fitting for each people at the sacrifice.

**1.27.11**  May the great, blameless, smoke-bannered, many-radiant one impel us toward wisdom and prize.

**1.27.12**  Like a wealthy lord of the clan, the divine banner, great-rayed Agni — let him hear us with our hymns.

**1.27.13**  Homage to the great, homage to the small, homage to the young, and homage to the old. We sacrifice to the gods if we are able; O gods, do not cut us off from the praise of the greater one.

**1.28.1**  Where the broad-based pressing-stone stands upright for pressing — Indra's gaping throat knows well of those who press in the mortar.

**1.28.2**  Where the two pressing boards are made, set up like two thighs — Indra's gaping throat knows well of those who press in the mortar.

**1.28.3**  Where a woman learns to rock the mortar back and forth — Indra's gaping throat knows well of those who press in the mortar.

**1.28.4**  Where they fasten the churning stick with cords as if to rein in horses — Indra's gaping throat knows well of those who press in the mortar.

**1.28.5**  O little mortar, however you are employed in house after house, here sound out the most brilliant noise, like the battle-drum of conquerors.

**1.28.6**  And the wind blows across your front, O lord of the wood; therefore, mortar, press out the Soma for Indra to drink.

**1.28.7**  The two most devoted to sacrifice and winning prizes — they resound aloft, like Indra's two bay steeds devouring the oblations.

**1.28.8**  Today may those two lordly ones of the forest, with exalted pressers, press out the honey-sweet Soma for Indra.

**1.28.9**  Bring the remnant from the two bowls; pour the Soma through the filter; lay it down upon the cow-hide.

**1.29.1**  Though we seem, O true Soma-drinker, like men without praise — do bring us fame, O Indra, in cattle, in bright horses, in thousands, O greatly bountiful one.

**1.29.2**  O cheek-flanged lord of prizes, O Śacī-powered one, great are your wondrous deeds — do bring us fame, O Indra, in cattle, in bright horses, in thousands, O greatly bountiful one.

**1.29.3**  Lull to sleep those two, gazing in opposite directions, who lie not waking — do bring us fame, O Indra, in cattle, in bright horses, in thousands, O greatly bountiful one.

**1.29.4**  Let those hostile ones sleep, let the generous gifts awake, O hero — do bring us fame, O Indra, in cattle, in bright horses, in thousands, O greatly bountiful one.

**1.29.5**  O Indra, crush together the braying donkey with that wicked device — do bring us fame, O Indra, in cattle, in bright horses, in thousands, O greatly bountiful one.

**1.29.6**  The Kuṇḍṛṇā bird flies far away above the forest on the wind — do bring us fame, O Indra, in cattle, in bright horses, in thousands, O greatly bountiful one.

**1.29.7**  Slay with your jaws all-encompassing clamor and the Kṛkadāśva — do bring us fame, O Indra, in cattle, in bright horses, in thousands, O greatly bountiful one.

**1.30.1**  Seeking the prize, pour for him who has a hundred powers, great Indra, with the Soma drops — pour for the most bountiful one, as for a Krivi.

**1.30.2**  Whether a hundred of the pure ones or a thousand of the mixed ones — still it flows down like water to the hollow.

**1.30.3**  When the potent one is gathered for intoxication — for it is within his belly — it spreads out like an ocean in the expanse.

**1.30.4**  This Soma too has sped to you like a pigeon to its nest; yet you do not heed even that speech.

**1.30.5**  May the praise of the lord of bounties, you O hero who carry hymns, whose abundance is real and joyful, be proclaimed.

**1.30.6**  Stand upright for our aid in this prize-contest, O you of a hundred powers; let us speak together about the others too.

**1.30.7**  In each and every yoking, in each and every prize-race, as friends we invoke Indra for our aid, the ever more powerful one.

**1.30.8**  Surely he will come if he hears — with his thousandfold aids and prizes — to our call.

**1.30.9**  In the track of the ancient house I invoke the man of great counter-thrust, whom your father previously invoked.

**1.30.10**  You who are all-desirable, O much-invoked one, we beseech you — O friend and lord, bestow on the singers.

**1.30.11**  O Soma-drinker, friend of those who drink Soma, O wielder of the thunderbolt, be the friend of our cheek-flanged warriors.

**1.30.12**  Let it be so, O Soma-drinker, O friend with the thunderbolt, make it so — as we desire for your favor.

**1.30.13**  May our companions in joy at Indra's feast be rich and full of many prizes, the sustaining ones with which we would exult.

**1.30.14**  Truly one such as you, self-achieved, proceeding boldly to the singers, unlocks the wheel-axle as it were from its wheels.

**1.30.15**  When, O you of a hundred powers, you fulfill the desire of the singers, you loosen the wheel-axle with your deeds of might.

**1.30.16**  Indra has ever conquered wealth with snorting, roaring, triumphant steeds. May he, the wonder-working one with the golden chariot, be our winner and give to us.

**1.30.17**  O Aśvins, come on this horse-rich path with your mighty power; come, O wondrous ones, with cattle and with gold.

**1.30.18**  For your chariot, O wondrous immortal Aśvins, is yoked by the same yoking; it courses over the sea.

**1.30.19**  You have set the wheel of your chariot upon the head of the inviolable cow; the other one revolves around the sky.

**1.30.20**  Who of the mortals enjoys you, O Uṣas, O beloved of Kadhapri, O immortal one? Whom do you seek, O you of many lights?

**1.30.21**  For we have pondered you, O ruddy one bright as a horse, from near and from far away.

**1.30.22**  Come with those prizes, O daughter of heaven; bestow wealth upon us.

**1.31.1**  You, O Agni, became the first among the Aṅgirases — a seer, a god, an auspicious friend of the gods. In your ordinance the poets, skillful in sacred knowledge, were born — the Maruts with gleaming spears.

**1.31.2**  You, O Agni, most foremost of the Aṅgirases, as a seer you adorn the ordinance of the gods. All-pervading, wise for all the worlds, born of two mothers, you rest in many ways for man's benefit.

**1.31.3**  You, O Agni, were first revealed by Mātariśvan to Vivasvat through your power of right action. Heaven and Earth trembled at the heroic deed of the Hotṛ; great One, you bore the burden [of sacrifice] and duly worshipped.

**1.31.4**  You, O Agni, made heaven resound for Manu; for righteous Purūravas you were even more beneficent. When by a dog's [Śyāva's] means you were taken away from your parents, they brought you forth first and then again a second time.

**1.31.5**  You, O Agni, are the bull who increases nourishment; you become praiseworthy for him who lifts the ladle. As the sole life-force you who know the oblation and the vaṣaṭ-call attend upon the clans at the head.

**1.31.6**  You, O Agni, on the crooked path carry the man [safely] in your company, all-seeing one, at the assembly. He who, in a contest of heroes, in a contest for stakes, slays even many arrayed opponents with [his] few.

**1.31.7**  You, O Agni, establish that mortal man in the highest immortality for fame day by day — him who striving for both worlds you gladden, O Agni, and give refreshment to the pious one.

**1.31.8**  O Agni, being praised, make our singer renowned for the winning of wealth. May we prosper through new action and work; may Heaven and Earth, together with the gods, protect us.

**1.31.9**  You, O Agni, in the lap of our parents, a god blameless among the gods, keep vigil. Be the body-fashioner and the guiding wisdom for the singer; you, O auspicious one, protect all good wealth.

**1.31.10**  O Agni, you are our guiding wisdom, you are our father; you are the maker of [our] strength — we are your kindred. May hundreds of riches, may thousands flow to you together, O guardian of ordinances, undeceivable one, along with brave heroes.

**1.31.11**  You, O Agni, the gods made the first living one for Āyu, the lord of the clan of Nahuṣa. They made Iḷā as the instructor of human beings, as when a son is born to his own father.

**1.31.12**  O Agni, O god worthy of homage, protect the magnanimous man and his body with your watchers. You are the protector of offspring and of progeny with cattle, guarding without blinking, within your ordinance.

**1.31.13**  You, O Agni, four-eyed one, are kindled as the inner guardian for the worshipper who is free from injury. You who are invoked with oblations tend the wolf-free one; even the bard's prayer you gain by mind.

**1.31.14**  O Agni, for the widely praised singer you obtain that which is the most desirable supreme wealth. Even for the helpless one you are called the guiding wisdom and father; you instruct the simple, knowing the directions better than any.

**1.31.15**  You, O Agni, protect from all sides the man who extends his right hand like one sewn in armour. He who eats sweet food, who makes a comfortable dwelling, who sacrifices the living sacrifice — he is the equal of heaven.

**1.31.16**  Do not, O Agni, disregard this road of ours, this path along which we have come from afar. You are kinsman, father, and guide of the Soma-pressing ones; you are the active maker of seers among mortals.

**1.31.17**  O Agni, O Aṅgiras, as [you dwelt] with Manu, as with the Aṅgirases, as with Yayāti in the seat of old, O bright one — come hither, bring the divine folk, seat them on the sacred grass, and worship what is dear.

**1.31.18**  O Agni, strengthen yourself with this sacred prayer, or with whatever power we have performed, or with wisdom. Also lead us forward to greater wealth; unite us with a well-disposed, vigorous thought.

**1.32.1**  Now I will proclaim the heroic deeds of Indra — the first deeds which the vajra-wielder performed. He slew the serpent [Vṛtra], and then he bored through the waters; he split open the bellies of the mountains.

**1.32.2**  He slew the serpent lying on the mountain; Tvaṣṭṛ fashioned for him the sky-roaring thunderbolt. Like lowing cows rushing swiftly, the waters flowed straight down to the ocean.

**1.32.3**  Acting like a bull he chose Soma and drank of the pressed juice at the three Tri-kadrukas. Maghavan took up the lightning weapon, the missile, and slew Vṛtra, the first-born among serpents.

**1.32.4**  When, O Indra, you slew the first-born of serpents and outwitted the māyā-tricks of the tricksters, generating the sun, the sky, and the dawn at that very moment — then indeed you found no enemy to withstand you.

**1.32.5**  Indra slew Vṛtra, the more formidable hinderer, with his great weapon, the thunderbolt. Like the trunk of a tree split by an axe, the serpent lay prostrate, touching the earth.

**1.32.6**  Like a man drunk and unfit for battle, Vṛtra challenged the great hero, the mighty assailer, the soma-drinker. He did not withstand the onslaught of his weapons; shattered, with broken joints, lay Indra's enemy.

**1.32.7**  Footless and handless, Vṛtra still fought against Indra; Indra smote him on the back with his thunderbolt. The castrated one, seeking to match the bull [Indra], lay shattered in many places.

**1.32.8**  Like a broken reed, Vṛtra lay there, and over him the waters flowed, riding on his mind [i.e. flooding his body]. Those [waters] which Vṛtra in his greatness had encompassed — the serpent now lay with his head at their feet.

**1.32.9**  Vṛtra's mother, Dānu, became one who sinks downward; Indra hurled his weapon upon her. Above lay the mother, below lay the son; Dānu lay there like a cow with her calf.

**1.32.10**  In the midst of the channels that stand motionless and never rest, the body of Vṛtra lay hid; the waters flow over his secret parts — Indra's enemy lay in long darkness.

**1.32.11**  The waters stood still, guarded by the Dāsa's rule, guarded by the serpent — dammed up like cows held by a Paṇi. When Indra had slain Vṛtra, he opened the blocked opening of the waters.

**1.32.12**  You became, O Indra, like a horse's tail at that moment when the one god [Vṛtra] struck back at you. You conquered the cattle, O hero, you conquered Soma; you released the seven rivers to flow.

**1.32.13**  Neither lightning nor thunder could stop him; nor the mist he scattered, nor the hail. When Indra and the serpent fought, Maghavan indeed conquered, now and for the future.

**1.32.14**  Whom did you see as the avenger of the serpent, O Indra, when fear entered your heart after the slaying? Then like a frightened eagle you crossed the ninety-nine rivers.

**1.32.15**  Indra the king of that which is exhausted and of that which is quiet, the horned one, the vajra-armed — he indeed rules as king over all peoples. Like a wheel's rim he encircled them all.

**1.33.1**  Let us go on this journey to Indra, seeking cattle; let him promote our good understanding. Unstricken by debts — will he now turn to us the innermost desire of his wealth of cattle?

**1.33.2**  I fly toward the unchallenged wealth-giver, toward a favorable abode, like a hawk. Reverencing Indra with my highest hymns — he who is invocable by singers along the way.

**1.33.3**  The noble man whose quiver is stuck full [with arrows] drives together the cattle of whoever desires them. O Indra, you who roar aloud with abundant wealth — do not be a Paṇi, superior to us in might.

**1.33.4**  You slew the Dasyu who is rich in possessions, O Indra, roaming alone with your supporters. Spreading in different directions from your bow, the ancients who did not sacrifice went to their doom.

**1.33.5**  Those non-sacrificers competing with sacrificers even turned their heads away — when, O Indra, steady driver of bay horses, terrible one, you pressed the lawless ones out from both worlds.

**1.33.6**  They could not fight against the blameless one's army; the Navagva clans went over to the opposite side. Armed with bull-like weapons, the castrated ones, driven out, came advancing toward Indra from the streams.

**1.33.7**  These weeping and shouting ones, O Indra, you fought beyond the dust of battle. You burned the Dasyu down from the high sky; you furthered the glory of those who press Soma and those who sing praises.

**1.33.8**  Adorning themselves with gold jewels [spanning] the girth of the earth, those who were not urging [the sacrifice] could not cross over Indra; he stationed his spies around them with the sun.

**1.33.9**  When, O Indra, you encompassed both worlds everywhere with your greatness — you thrust out the Dasyu with your sacred prayers, [the gods] who think clearly against those who think [wrongly], O Indra.

**1.33.10**  They did not reach the end of sky or earth, nor did they surpass the wealth-giver with their māyā. Indra the bull made his thunderbolt his ally; with light he milked the cattle out of the darkness.

**1.33.11**  The waters flowed according to his own nature; he grew in the midst of the rivers. With concentrated mind, O Indra, with your mightiest blow you struck the gambler down.

**1.33.12**  He pierced through the strongholds of Ilībiśa; Indra split open the horned Śuṣṇa. As far as your sharpness, O Maghavan, as far as your might — with the thunderbolt you slew the attacking enemy.

**1.33.13**  His force swept over his enemies; with his sharp bull-might he broke open the strongholds. Indra drove Vṛtra at the thunderbolt, surpassing his own intention as he conquers.

**1.33.14**  O Indra, you helped Kutsa in whom you took pleasure; you aided the fighting bull Daśadyu. The dust raised by hooves touched the sky; the son of Śvitra stood up for the defeat of enemies.

**1.33.15**  O Maghavan, in the battle for fields you helped Śama the bull and Śvitryā the cow among the Tugryas. Even those who had stood here for long — the reward fell to those who acted as enemies.

**1.34.1**  Be here with us three times today with new gifts, O Aśvins; your journey is vast and so is your bestowal. For your harnessing, like putting on a winter garment, should be eagerly done by the wise.

**1.34.2**  Three hubs are on the honey-bearing chariot in the wake of the Soma's longing — all know it. Three are the supports, set up to be grasped; three times by night you travel, O Aśvins, and three times by day.

**1.34.3**  On the same day, three times drive away blame; three times today fill the sacrifice with honey. Three times, O Aśvins, you two who have vigorous nourishment — fill us with bounty by night and at dawn.

**1.34.4**  Come three times for the oblation, three times to the obedient man; three times to the worthy one give succour, as if threefold. Three times bring the jubilation, O Aśvins; three times pour the nourishment into us like water through an axle.

**1.34.5**  Three times bring us riches, O Aśvins you two; three times the divine favor, and three times support our thoughts. Three times good fortune and three times renown for us — may the sun's daughter mount your three-stepped chariot.

**1.34.6**  Three times, O Aśvins, give us the heavenly medicines; three times the earthly, and three times those from the waters. Bring protection and well-being to my son who desires it — O lords of beauty, bring three-partite shelter.

**1.34.7**  Three times a day, O Aśvins, worthy of worship, you two have lain around the earth with your three-partite resting. O Nāsatyas, you traverse three distant routes in your chariot, like the wind going to its own haunts.

**1.34.8**  Three times, O Aśvins, with the rivers of seven mothers; three invocations; the oblation prepared in three ways. You protect the three earths and the vault of heaven above the streams, fixed with days and nights.

**1.34.9**  Where are the three wheels of the triple chariot? Where are the three yoke-poles that share a pole together? When is the yoking of the vigorous ass by which, O Nāsatyas, you come to the sacrifice?

**1.34.10**  Come here, O Nāsatyas — the oblation is being offered; drink of the honey with your honey-drinking lips. For Savitṛ first impels for Ṛta the wondrous, ghee-rich chariot of you two, ahead of the Usas.

**1.34.11**  Come here, O Nāsatyas, with three times eleven gods for the honey-drinking, O Aśvins. Preserve our life uninjured; wipe away our sins; ward off enmity; be ever present.

**1.34.12**  O Aśvins, bring us on your triple chariot riches turned toward us, [rich] in brave heroes. Hearing [our call] I invoke you two again and again for protection; be for our growth in the conquest of prizes.

**1.35.1**  I invoke Agni first for well-being; I invoke Mitra and Varuṇa here for aid. I invoke Night, the settler of the world; I invoke the god Savitṛ for protection.

**1.35.2**  Traveling through the dark realm, settling the immortal and the mortal in their places, Savitṛ the god travels through the worlds seeing all on his golden chariot.

**1.35.3**  The god travels on the downward slope, travels on the upward slope, travels with his two bright, adorable bay steeds. The god Savitṛ comes from afar, driving away all difficulties.

**1.35.4**  Covered with pearls, of all forms, with golden bolts, adorable, and vast — Savitṛ of brilliant radiance has mounted his chariot, bearing strength through the dark realms.

**1.35.5**  The dark, white-footed [horses] displayed the peoples, drawing the chariot with the golden yoke-pole. All peoples, all worlds have always stood in the lap of divine Savitṛ.

**1.35.6**  Three heavens belong to Savitṛ — two are his lap, one belongs in the world of Yama, the all-conquering. Upon the immortal as upon a chariot axle they are established — let him who knows this speak it here.

**1.35.7**  The fair-winged one [Savitṛ] surveyed the atmospheres, the Asura of deep inspiration and good guidance. Where is the sun now? Who knows it? To which heaven has his ray stretched forth?

**1.35.8**  He surveyed the eight peaks of the earth, the three desert-distances, the seven rivers. Golden-eyed Savitṛ the god has come, granting desirable treasures to the pious worshipper.

**1.35.9**  Golden-handed Savitṛ, all-seeing one, moves between both Heaven and Earth. He drives away disease and goes to meet the sun; he presses the sky downward with the dark realm.

**1.35.10**  Golden-handed, the Asura of good guidance, most gracious, self-dependent — may he come here toward us. Repelling demons and sorcerers, the god has stood at evening, receiving praises.

**1.35.11**  O Savitṛ, those ancient paths of yours, dustless and well-made in the atmosphere — by those easily traveled paths protect us today and speak over us, O god.

**1.36.1**  For you many peoples longing for the gods — we approach Agni the young, the great, with well-spoken words and praises; him whom another also implores.

**1.36.2**  The peoples who possess oblations have established Agni, who grows with might; we would serve you, O truly existent one. Be you today favorably disposed and a helper here to us in contests.

**1.36.3**  We choose you as our messenger, as the priest who calls, the all-knowing. Your flames spread abroad in all directions as you are great; your radiances touch the sky.

**1.36.4**  The gods, Varuṇa, Mitra, Aryaman kindle you, the ancient messenger, together. O Agni, that mortal wins all wealth with you who has pleased you.

**1.36.5**  O Agni, you are the gladsome Hotṛ, the householder, the messenger of the clans. In you all the fixed ordinances are gathered together which the gods have established.

**1.36.6**  In you, O Agni, most blessed, most youthful one, all the oblation is offered. Be well-disposed to us today and again worship the gods for brave heroism.

**1.36.7**  Thus, bowing in homage, men approach him, the self-king. Men kindle Agni with the Hotṛ-calls, desiring to cross over failures.

**1.36.8**  Slaying Vṛtra they crossed Heaven and Earth; they made wide space for dwelling. The bull [Agni], full of brilliance, invoked among the Kaṇvas — the horse will neigh in the cattle raids.

**1.36.9**  Settle yourself down — you are great; blaze forth, most desired of gods. O Agni, let the ruddy smoke stream forth, splendid one, visible and praiseworthy.

**1.36.10**  You whom the gods placed here for Manu, most worthy of worship, O carrier of oblations — you whom Kaṇva, Medhyātithi, Dhanasṛt, Vṛṣā, and Upastuta [have praised].

**1.36.11**  The Agni which Medhyātithi, the Kaṇva, kindled upon the [altar of] Ṛta — his flames have shone; these hymns magnify that Agni.

**1.36.12**  Fill [us] with wealth, O self-strong one — for you have something to obtain among the gods, O Agni. You rule over renowned prize. Have mercy on us — you are great.

**1.36.13**  Rise up now for our protection, like the god Savitṛ. Rise up as the winner of the prize when we invoke you with the anointed singers.

**1.36.14**  Rising, protect us from distress; burn up all things that devour, with your banner. Make us rise up to wander and to live; find our service among the gods.

**1.36.15**  O Agni, protect us from the demon, protect us from the deceitful ungiver. Protect from the injurer and even from him who would slay us, O you of great brilliance, most youthful one.

**1.36.16**  Strike in all directions, like a hammer, those ungiver enemies who harm us, O you with fiery jaws. Let not that mortal enemy who sharpens himself against us in the night hours hold power over us.

**1.36.17**  Agni has gained [for us] brave heroism; Agni has gained good fortune for the Kaṇva. Agni helped Mitra and also Medhyātithi; Agni in the winning [helped] Upastuta.

**1.36.18**  With Agni we invoke Turvaśa and Yadu from afar, and Ugrādeva. Agni leading Navavāstava [and] Bṛhadratha — Turvīti [received] strength against the Dasyu.

**1.36.19**  Manu set you down, O Agni, as the light for the people for ever. You shone forth, born of Ṛta, O Kaṇva, when strengthened — you whom all peoples revere.

**1.36.20**  Resplendent and mighty are the flames of Agni, terrible to confront. Protective always against sorcerers, burn up everything that devours.

**1.37.1**  O Kaṇvas, sing forth in praise of the sport-loving Marut troop — not to be checked, glorious in their chariot.

**1.37.2**  They who were born with their own radiance, with speckled deer, with spears, together with their axes, and their [ointment-]staves.

**1.37.3**  Here, as it were, I hear the whips in their hands, as they speak. Along their course they adorn themselves brilliantly.

**1.37.4**  Sing for your excited troop, for those of fearful splendor, the vigorous ones — sing the god-given sacred prayer.

**1.37.5**  Proclaim the inviolable [might] in the cows, the sport of the Marut troop — it has grown strong in the jaws of taste.

**1.37.6**  Who among you is the greatest, O men, O shakers of heaven and earth? Since you shake [even] what is at the boundary.

**1.37.7**  At your onslaught, at your fierce fury, the mountain, the rock trembles for mortal man.

**1.37.8**  At whose rushing even the earth, like an aged lord of the clan, trembles in fear at their courses.

**1.37.9**  For strong is their birth, [sprung] from their mother's womb to go forth — when their power follows them as a second force.

**1.37.10**  Up have those sons [the Maruts] stretched their shouts like courses at their rushings — like lowing [cows] going knee-deep.

**1.37.11**  Even that long, broad, irresistible child of mist [i.e. the rain cloud] they cause to rush forward with their courses.

**1.37.12**  O Maruts, when with your strength you have caused peoples to tremble, you have caused mountains to tremble.

**1.37.13**  When the Maruts go forth and speak together on the way — does anyone hear them?

**1.37.14**  Go swiftly with your swift ones — your services are among the Kaṇvas. There indeed rejoice.

**1.37.15**  For there is indeed [something] for your joy, and we are indeed theirs. May we live out our whole lifespan.

**1.38.1**  How now, O you whose delight is [in being asked] 'how?' — do you hold [us] in your hands like a father his son? O you for whom the sacred grass has been strewn.

**1.38.2**  Where now, and to what purpose will you go — as if from the sky to the earth? Where do your cows not delight?

**1.38.3**  Where, O Maruts, are your new favors? Where your well-being? Where are all your blessings?

**1.38.4**  If you, O sons of Pṛśni, were mortal men, your praiser would be immortal.

**1.38.5**  May your praiser not become like a beast in the pasture, unwanted — may he not go along Yama's path.

**1.38.6**  Let not Nirṛti, the hard-hitting one, slay us again and again; may she perish with thirst.

**1.38.7**  Truly the sons of Rudra are formidable and mighty; even across the desert they make moisture without wind.

**1.38.8**  The lightning bellows like a lowing [cow]; the mother clings to the calf — when their rain was let loose.

**1.38.9**  Even by day they make darkness with Parjanya laden with waters, when they flood the earth.

**1.38.10**  Then at the roaring of the Maruts all earthly dwellings shook; men trembled and quaked.

**1.38.11**  O Maruts, with firm-gripping hands travel along the bright, obstructed [roads], with untiring courses.

**1.38.12**  May your fellies be firm; may their chariots and horses, their reins well-fashioned [be ready].

**1.38.13**  Speak with continuous praise toward Brahmaṇaspati, whom one desires to see, like Agni, like Mitra.

**1.38.14**  Form the chant in [your] mouth, you who have spread [it] like Parjanya; sing the Gāyatric verse worthy of recitation.

**1.38.15**  Praise the Marut host, formidable, wonderful, provided with songs — may the magnified ones be here with us.

**1.39.1**  When, just so, from afar you display your glory like a flame — by whose will, O Maruts? In whose form? To whom do you go? Whom indeed do you shake?

**1.39.2**  May your weapons be firm for driving away [enemies], and strong for withstanding them. May your might be the more wonderful — not that of any deceitful mortal.

**1.39.3**  When, O heroes, you strike away what is firmly fixed and roll away what is heavy — you cleave through the forests of the earth, through the sides of the mountains.

**1.39.4**  No enemy of yours has been found in the sky above, nor on the earth, O eaters of enemies. May your might, O sons of Rudra, be — with your power as an ally — unconquerable even now.

**1.39.5**  They cause the mountains to tremble; they split open the lords of the forest. The Maruts have rushed forth, like gods intoxicated, with their whole host.

**1.39.6**  You have yoked the speckled deer to your chariots; the reddish horse draws as the leader. For your course even the earth cried out; mortal men were afraid.

**1.39.7**  We swiftly choose your protection, O sons of Rudra, for our well-being. Come now to us with aid, as of old you came to the frightened Kaṇva.

**1.39.8**  Sent by you, O Maruts, or sent by mortals — whoever attacks us with monstrous force, drive him away with your might, with your power, with your aids.

**1.39.9**  For you are worthy of prior worship, O far-seeing ones; grant [your aid] to Kaṇva with your boundless [gifts]. Come to us, O Maruts, with your boundless aids — like lightning with rain.

**1.39.10**  You carry boundless force, O generous givers; your shaking [imparts] boundless vigor. O Maruts, incensed against the sage-haters, let fly your arrow upon the enemy.

**1.40.1**  Rise up, O Brahmaṇaspati — we the god-seeking ones implore you. Let the Maruts, the generous givers, approach; O Indra, be swift, be our companion.

**1.40.2**  You indeed, O son of strength, the mortal calls upon when stakes are set. He who has desired you — may he obtain from the Maruts brave heroism and fine horses.

**1.40.3**  Let Brahmaṇaspati go forth; let the divine Sūnṛtā go forth. Toward the heroic man of manly might who fulfills fivefold — may the gods lead our sacrifice.

**1.40.4**  He who gives the singer noble wealth — he acquires undying fame. For him we worship Iḷā, rich in brave men, swift in aid, faultless.

**1.40.5**  Now indeed Brahmaṇaspati speaks the mantra worthy of recitation — in which Indra, Varuṇa, Mitra, and Aryaman, the gods, have made their dwelling places.

**1.40.6**  That beneficent, faultless mantra, O gods, we would speak at the gatherings. And may you, O heroes, like this speech well — may he [the worshipper] obtain all your good things.

**1.40.7**  Who would reach the god-seeking man? Who the man with strewn sacred grass? The generous giver pressed ever forward with his households from within, establishing [his] dwelling.

**1.40.8**  He should attach himself to kingly power; he establishes good dwelling even in fear, smiting with kings. There is no turner-back, no overcomer for the vajra-wielder [Indra] in the great contest or the small.

**1.41.1**  The man whom the far-sighted ones protect — Varuṇa, Mitra, and Aryamā — that man can never be deceived.

**1.41.2**  They guard the mortal man from harm as if with arms upraised; unscathed in every way he prospers.

**1.41.3**  The kings of these — those sovereign lords — break down the foes' strongholds and lead them safely through all difficulties.

**1.41.4**  Easy and thornless is the path, O Ādityas, for him who walks by ṛta; there is no stumbling-block for you there.

**1.41.5**  The sacrifice that you, O lords, O Ādityas, lead along the straight path — may it reach forward unto your contemplation.

**1.41.6**  That mortal, unassailed, moves toward the jewel, toward all wealth, toward offspring, and himself goes forward.

**1.41.7**  How, O friends, shall we worthily achieve the hymn of Mitra and Aryamā, and the great magnificence of Varuṇa?

**1.41.8**  May I not speak back to you — who smite and who curse — when I am seeking the gods; with your gracious favors alone do I desire to win you.

**1.41.9**  Even a generous giver should fear up to the moment of deposit; let him not covet evil speech.

**1.42.1**  O Pūṣan, cross us safely over the road; O offspring of the Deliverer, drive away adversity; lead us forward, O god.

**1.42.2**  The evil wolf, O Pūṣan, that ill-omened one who menaces us — drive him away from the road.

**1.42.3**  Drive far away that lurking robber, the ambusher who lies crooked in the path, away from the track.

**1.42.4**  Tread down with your foot the burning force of that double-tongued one, whosoever he may be who utters evil.

**1.42.5**  That aid of yours, O wondrous Pūṣan, we choose by our prayer — that with which you once urged forward the Fathers.

**1.42.6**  So now, O lord of all good fortune, you who possess the finest golden axe, make our riches easy to win.

**1.42.7**  Lead us past those who follow after us; make the roads good and the paths easy for us; O Pūṣan, find the resolve for us here.

**1.42.8**  Lead us to good pasture; let there be no fresh sting of distress upon the road; O Pūṣan, find the resolve for us here.

**1.42.9**  Strengthen us, fill us, send us forth, and whet our front; O Pūṣan, find the resolve for us here.

**1.42.10**  We do not contend against Pūṣan; we praise him with hymns well-spoken; we implore the wondrous one for wealth.

**1.43.1**  What most heartening word shall we speak to the far-seeing Rudra, the most bounteous, the mighty one?

**1.43.2**  As Aditi has brought it about for our cattle, for our men, for our cows — so may the Rudrian grace be ours for our offspring.

**1.43.3**  As Mitra and Varuṇa know it for us, as Rudra discerns it, as all the gods who share one pleasure know it.

**1.43.4**  Of Rudra, lord of songs, lord of sacrifice, healer with cooling balm — we implore that gracious benevolence and peace.

**1.43.5**  He who shines bright as the blazing sun, who gleams like gold — he is the best treasure among the gods.

**1.43.6**  He grants well-being to our horse, easy passage to ram and ewe, to our men, our women, and our cow.

**1.43.7**  O Soma, bestow upon us splendor, upon a hundred men; great fame and mighty power.

**1.43.8**  May no oppressors hem us in around the Soma, may no hostilities lead us astray; O Indu, grant us a share in the prize.

**1.43.9**  Those progeny of yours, O Soma — of the immortal one, in the further abode of ṛta, at the crown and navel, O Soma who are longed for — knowing those who adorn themselves, O Soma, you know them.

**1.44.1**  O Agni, bright like the shining Dawn, O immortal Jātavedas, bring today the wondrous bounty to the worshipper; bring hither the gods who wake at dawn.

**1.44.2**  For you are the beloved messenger, O Agni, the carrier of oblations, the charioteer of sacrifices; in concert with the Aśvins and with Dawn bestow upon us great renown and heroic strength.

**1.44.3**  Today we choose as messenger the good Agni, beloved of many, whose banner is smoke, who blazes bright, the glory of sacrifices at the breaking of dawn.

**1.44.4**  The best and youngest, the guest, well-offered, beloved by the giving folk — I praise Agni Jātavedas, to travel toward the gods at the breaking of dawn.

**1.44.5**  I shall praise you, O immortal enjoyer of all; O Agni, immortal protector, worthy of worship, most deserving of the sacrifice, O carrier of oblations.

**1.44.6**  Be well-praised to the singer, O youngest, honey-tongued, well-fueled; prolonging the life of Praskaṇva so that he may live, be reverenced by the divine folk.

**1.44.7**  For the peoples kindle you, all-knowing hotar; so bring them here swiftly, O Agni, far-sighted one, much-invoked, bring the gods hither.

**1.44.8**  Savitṛ, Dawn, the Aśvins, Bhaga — the Kaṇvas with pressed Soma kindle you, O Agni, at the night's breaking, O carrier of oblations, you who render the sacrifice auspicious.

**1.44.9**  For you are the lord of sacrifices, O Agni, the messenger of the peoples; O you who wake at dawn, bring hither today the heaven-beholding gods for the drinking of Soma.

**1.44.10**  O Agni, O radiant one, you have shone through the dawns of old, seen by all; you are the protector and purohita in the villages, you are the human priest at the sacrifices.

**1.44.11**  We set you in place, O Agni, as the accomplisher of the sacrifice, the hotar, the seasonal priest — O far-sighted, swift, immortal messenger, as Manu did, O god.

**1.44.12**  When, O Agni, revered by Mitra's friends, you go on your mission as purohita among the gods, your flames blaze bright like the roaring waves of a river.

**1.44.13**  Hear, O Agni, you of the listening ear, together with the god-bearers, your companions; may Mitra and Aryamā, who journey in the morning, sit down upon the barhis at the sacrifice.

**1.44.14**  May the Maruts, bounteous ones, whose tongue is Agni, increasers of ṛta, hear the hymn; may Varuṇa of steadfast vow drink the Soma, together with the Aśvins and with Dawn.

**1.45.1**  O Agni, sacrifice here to the Vasus, the Rudras, and the Ādityas; sacrifice to the people born of Manu, the Manu-born folk who sprinkle ghee, making the rite auspicious.

**1.45.2**  For the gods, O Agni, are prompt to heed the worshipper, they are discerning; bring them here, O you praised with songs, O you whose horses are red — all thirty-three.

**1.45.3**  As for Priyamedha, as for Atri, O Jātavedas, as for Virūpa, as for the Aṅgirases — O you of great vows, hear the call of Praskaṇva.

**1.45.4**  The Priyamedhas called on the great-horned one for aid — Agni, the king of sacrifices, blazing with brilliant splendor.

**1.45.5**  O you who are invoked with ghee, these words are real and true — hear them; with these songs the sons of Kaṇva call upon you for protection.

**1.45.6**  You, of most wondrous fame, O Agni, O hair of flame, O most beloved — the beings among the clans call upon you to carry their oblations.

**1.45.7**  The inspired seers have set you in place, O Agni, as hotar and seasonal priest, most rich in goods, of listening ear, most widely famed, at the celestial rites.

**1.45.8**  The inspired poets with pressed Soma have stirred you toward the offering; bearing a great light, O Agni, bring the oblation to the mortal worshipper.

**1.45.9**  O powerful one, true one, you who travel in the morning — for the drinking of Soma, seat the divine company here today upon the sacred grass, O Vasu.

**1.45.10**  O Agni, honor with joined invocations the divine company that faces toward us; this Soma is present, O bounteous ones — drink it throughout the day.

**1.46.1**  This unprecedented Dawn is shining forth, beloved of the sky; I praise you two, O Aśvins, with a great hymn.

**1.46.2**  You two wondrous ones whose mother is the river, who fly swifter than thought among riches — divine ones who find wealth through insight.

**1.46.3**  Your praises resound, O mighty ones, in the heaven on high in the ancient world; when your chariot flies with its wings abroad.

**1.46.4**  The lover of the waters, who has drunk deep, fills with oblation, O heroes — the father of the kuṭa, the observer of men.

**1.46.5**  Ready is our reception of your thoughts, O Nāsatyās, you who speak with inspired words; drink the Soma boldly.

**1.46.6**  That which shall carry us across the darkness, O Aśvins, that light-bearing gift — bestow that refreshment upon us.

**1.46.7**  Come to us on the ship of thought, to travel to the far shore; yoke your chariot, O Aśvins.

**1.46.8**  Your rudder is the broad sky; your chariot stands at the ford of rivers; the Soma drops have been yoked by thought.

**1.46.9**  The Kaṇvan Soma drops from heaven, the wealth at the place of the rivers — where do you two wish to deposit your own veil?

**1.46.10**  There arose a radiance for the Soma stalk; golden, the Sun turned toward it, and dark-tongued fire perceived it clearly.

**1.46.11**  There arose a path to cross over, on the right course of ṛta; the flowing path of heaven was clearly seen.

**1.46.12**  That — that very aid of the Aśvins does the singer adorn, in the exhilaration of the flowing Soma.

**1.46.13**  Desiring, you two radiant ones — come, as Manu did, with the drinking of Soma and with the song, you two who bring well-being.

**1.46.14**  Dawn attended upon the glory of you two who circle round; you two win through ṛta, with the night-beams.

**1.46.15**  Both of you drink; both of you grant us shelter, O Aśvins, with aids that drive away what is alien.

**1.47.1**  This sweetest pressed Soma, O increasers of ṛta — drink of it throughout the day, O Aśvins, and bestow jewels upon the worshipper.

**1.47.2**  Come, O Aśvins, on the triple-yoked, triple-twisted, beautiful chariot; the Kaṇvas fashion the sacred prayer for you at the sacrifice — hear well their call.
