**1.47.3**  O Aśvins, O increasers of ṛta, drink the sweetest Soma; and then today, O wondrous ones, bearing wealth in your chariot, approach the worshipping man.

**1.47.4**  O all-knowing ones, sprinkle the sacrifice with sweetness at the three-seated sacred grass; the Kaṇvas with pressed Soma, the brilliant ones, call upon you two, O Aśvins.

**1.47.5**  With those helps with which you two, O Aśvins, formerly aided Kaṇva, with those very same aids protect us now, O lords of beauty; drink the Soma, O increasers of ṛta.

**1.47.6**  O wondrous Aśvins, bearing wealth in your chariot, bring nourishment to Sudās; place upon us from the ocean or from heaven riches much to be coveted.

**1.47.7**  Whether you two Nāsatyās are in the far distance, or whether you abide with Turvaśa — from there come to us on your well-rolling chariot, together with the rays of the sun.

**1.47.8**  May your horses, glorious at the sacrifice, bear you hitherward swiftly; mixing refreshment for the well-doer, O bounteous ones, sit down upon the sacred grass, O heroes.

**1.47.9**  Come, O Nāsatyās, on that sun-skinned chariot with which you have always carried goods to the worshipper, for the drinking of the sweet Soma.

**1.47.10**  With recitations and with songs we call upon you two, rich in many goods, to come hither for our protection; for, O Aśvins, you have ever drunk Soma joyfully in the dear assembly of the Kaṇvas.

**1.48.1**  Shine forth for us with wealth, O Dawn, O daughter of the sky; with great splendor, O radiant one — grant us riches, O goddess.

**1.48.2**  Rich in horses, rich in cows, bearing all gifts — they stir forth in abundance to awaken; rouse toward me the true and joyful words, O Dawn, and urge on the bounty of the liberal ones.

**1.48.3**  Dawn has shone and will shine yet again, the goddess who is swift with chariots; those who have been set in her courses, like men who covet fame in the sea.

**1.48.4**  O Dawn, those wise men who, at your departing journeys, yoke their minds toward giving — here among these, Kaṇva, most Kaṇva-like of them, proclaims the name of heroes.

**1.48.5**  Dawn comes like a lovely woman, gracious, consuming all before her; wearing away the settled folk she moves on foot, and sends the birds flying up.

**1.48.6**  She who sends forth the assembly and the seekers of goals, not knowing rest as she swells up — no bird sits still at your shining, O Dawn who bear the prize.

**1.48.7**  This auspicious Dawn has yoked her team from the far distance, above the rising place of the Sun; with a hundred chariots she moves forth across toward humankind.

**1.48.8**  The whole world bows to her that it may see; the gracious one makes the moving world light; the daughter of heaven, the bounteous Dawn, has shone forth, driving away hatred and foes.

**1.48.9**  O Dawn, shine forth with your bright and brilliant radiance, O daughter of the sky; bringing great good fortune to us, shining in your celestial courses.

**1.48.10**  For when you shine forth, O gracious one, the breath and life of all the world is in you; so hear our call on your great radiant chariot, O wondrous-wealthy one.

**1.48.11**  O Dawn, win the prize that is conspicuous among the human folk; with that bring the good-doers toward the rites, those who praise you, those who carry the offering.

**1.48.12**  Bring all the gods from the mid-air for the drinking of Soma, O Dawn; and place upon us that which is worthy of hymns — wealth in cows and horses, O Dawn, and heroic strength.

**1.48.13**  She whose gleaming, propitious rays have been seen coming forward — may that Dawn grant us wealth of all desirable things, of beautiful form, easy to obtain.

**1.48.14**  Even the ancient seers who called upon you, O great one, for aid and protection — answer our hymns, O Dawn, and grant your bounty with your brilliant splendor.

**1.48.15**  O Dawn, when today with your radiance you throw open the two doors of heaven, grant us a broad and wolf-free shelter, O goddess, and food rich in cattle.

**1.48.16**  Blend for us great wealth of all beautiful forms, blend for us with refreshments, O Dawn; blend for us, O Dawn who shines in all directions, with great splendor; and with prizes, O you who bear the prize.

**1.49.1**  Come, O Dawn, with auspicious gifts, from above the bright shining of the sky; may the reddish-tinged steeds carry those who press Soma toward your dwelling.

**1.49.2**  The beautiful, pleasant chariot upon which you have mounted, O Dawn — with that protect today the folk of great renown, O daughter of the sky.

**1.49.3**  Even the birds that have wings, O bright Dawn, two-footed and four-footed — they all start forth at your coming, following the seasons from the ends of heaven.

**1.49.4**  Shining forth with your rays, you illumine all the bright world; desiring wealth, the Kaṇvas have called upon you, O Dawn, with songs.

**1.50.1**  The bright rays bear aloft that god, Jātavedas, so that all may see — they bear up Sūrya.

**1.50.2**  Like thieves the stars slink away with the night, before Sūrya the all-seeing one.

**1.50.3**  His bright rays have been seen, spreading out among the peoples, blazing like fires.

**1.50.4**  You are swift, seen by all, O Sūrya, maker of light; you shine upon the entire bright world.

**1.50.5**  You rise facing toward the peoples of the gods, facing toward mankind, facing toward all the world to see the sun-light.

**1.50.6**  With that pure eye of yours, as it moves busily among the peoples, you, Varuṇa, look upon them.

**1.50.7**  You traverse the broad, wide expanse of the sky, measuring out the days by nights, O Sūrya, looking upon all creatures.

**1.50.8**  Seven bay mares carry you in your chariot, O god Sūrya, O flame-haired, far-sighted one.

**1.50.9**  The Sun has yoked the seven bright mares, the daughters of his chariot; with these he travels, self-yoked.

**1.50.10**  Rising up out of the darkness, beholding the higher light, we have gone to Sūrya, the god among the gods — to the highest light.

**1.50.11**  Rising today, O you revered by Mitra, mounting the higher sky — O Sūrya, destroy my heart-sickness and my jaundice.

**1.50.12**  Into the parrots and the yellow-tinged mynah birds we blow off my jaundice; and into the haritāla-yellow plants we press down my jaundice.

**1.50.13**  This Āditya has risen, with all his might; may he subdue my enemy for me — let me not be subject to him who hates me.

**1.51.1**  Sing forth with hymns to him, that ram much-invoked, lauded in song — Indra, the ocean of wealth — whose heavens men do not traverse to the end; praise the most bounteous one, the inspired, for our enjoyment.

**1.51.2**  His own aids did homage to him of self-might, him who fills mid-air, encompassed by his powers; the wise Ṛbhus exalted Indra, the intoxication-pouring, the hundred-thoughted — and swift Truth mounted up.

**1.51.3**  You cleft the cow-pen for the Aṅgiras; you found the path for Atri amid the hundred fortresses; even arrayed with battle-host you brought wealth to Vimada in the contest, and made the rock leap for him who pressed the Soma.

**1.51.4**  You uncovered the covering of the waters and set flowing on the mountain the dripping treasure; when, O Indra, you slew with your might Vṛtra the serpent, you then raised the sun in the sky for all to see.

**1.51.5**  You cast down the sorcerers with your own magic powers — those who poured oblations on the couch of sleep; you, O hero-minded, broke open the fortresses of Pipru; and you aided Ṛjiśvan in the slayings of the Dasyus.

**1.51.6**  You aided Kutsa in the slayings of Śuṣṇa; you gave Śambara into the hand of Atithigva; you trod down with your foot even the great Arbuda — from of old you were born for the slaughter of the Dasyus.

**1.51.7**  All strength is placed in you, directed to one aim; your bounty rejoices for the drinking of Soma; your thunderbolt held in both arms is well-known — cut away all the virile powers of the foe.

**1.51.8**  Distinguish the Āryas from the Dasyus; subdue for the Soma-strewer those who transgress the ordinances; be the mighty impeller of the sacrificer — all these things of yours please at the communal joys.

**1.51.9**  Subduing the lawless for him who follows the law, Indra with his own presences smites down those who grasp at nothing; being praised while still growing he reaches toward heaven even as the great grows greater — the ant Vamra struck apart the ones who coil.

**1.51.10**  Uśanā fashioned for you with might your mighty force; your power with its greatness pushes apart the two worlds; the coursers harnessed by thought and driven by the wind brought you, O hero-minded one, as you were being filled, toward fame.

**1.51.11**  Indra was gladdened when keeping company with the sage Uśanā Kāvya; the Vṛtra-destroying one stands even above the cunning; the fierce one let loose the waters with their streams and shook apart the established fortresses of Śuṣṇa.

**1.51.12**  You take your stand in your chariot at the bull-handed ones, at the offerings of Śāryāta where you are gladdened; as, O Indra, you delight in pressed-Soma rites, you mount up to unimpeded glory in the sky.

**1.51.13**  You gave the young Vṛcayā to the great eloquent Kakṣīvat who pressed the Soma, O Indra; you became the ally of Vṛṣaṇaśva, O good-powered one — all these deeds of yours are to be proclaimed at the pressings.

**1.51.14**  Indra of good thought was celebrated alone at the Pajras; the praise-hymn like the sacrificial post at the house-door stands firm; seeking horses, desiring cattle, seeking chariots, desiring wealth — Indra alone rules over riches as their bestower.

**1.51.15**  This homage has been spoken to the bull, the self-sovereign, him of true vigor, the powerful one; in this enclosed space, O Indra, together with all heroes, may we together with our patrons be in your shelter.

**1.52.1**  Magnify that ram, the finder of heaven, at whose side a hundred of his fine ones move; like a prize steed I would turn Indra toward us for aid with well-fashioned hymns — him who hears the call and swiftly comes like a chariot to the race.

**1.52.2**  He, unshaken as a mountain in his foundations, of thousandfold help, has waxed great in his powers; when Indra slew Vṛtra who had dammed the rivers, he pressed the floods forth, exulting in the Soma-draught.

**1.52.3**  He is the door-opener at doors, at the udder-like fountain of bright clarity, grown great in rapture by the wise ones; I call that Indra with well-working thought — the most bounteous giver, for he is the filler with the Soma-draught.

**1.52.4**  Him whom, on the heavenly seat with Soma-strewn grass, his own fine aids fill like an ocean; him in the slaying of Vṛtra the vigorous aids followed — Indra's fierce, wind-swift, unerring helpers.

**1.52.5**  Toward his own rain-shower in the rapture of him fighting, the aids coursed swiftly as swift ones down a slope; when Indra the thunderbolt-bearer, bold in the Soma-draught, split apart the enclosures of Vala, even as Trita does.

**1.52.6**  His force moves around amid heat and gleaming splendor; having encompassed the waters it lay down at the base of the atmosphere; when in the downward slope of Vṛtra, hard to seize and roaring, O Indra, you smote the thunder between his two jaws.

**1.52.7**  As into a lake, the waves do not overwhelm you; the sacred formulas, O Indra, those that increase you — even Tvaṣṭṛ's matched power has grown great for you; he fashioned the thunderbolt of overwhelming force.

**1.52.8**  Having slain Vṛtra with your bay steeds, your will gathered, O Indra, you opened the waters' path for Manu; you stretched out the iron thunderbolt from your two arms and fixed the sun in the sky for all to see.

**1.52.9**  Vast and self-shining with force, what is hymn-worthy — they made the ascent of heaven tremble in fear; when the human-battle aids of Indra, the man-rousing Maruts, followed him exulting toward the sun.

**1.52.10**  Even Heaven, which is forceful, shook away in fear from the roaring of the serpent; your thunderbolt, O Indra, quivered; when with the force of the pressed Soma you smashed the head of Vṛtra who had pressed the two worlds together.

**1.52.11**  When, O Indra, the earth of ten degrees stretched out all the days, the peoples spread forth — in that, O Maghavan, your widely heard power became in its force a great thing co-extensive with heaven.

**1.52.12**  You, O bold-minded one, are on the far shore of this aerial realm, your own being's force ready to help; you have made the earth as the measure of your power; you who encompass the waters and the sun come to heaven.

**1.52.13**  You have become the measure of the earth; you have become lord of the great one of lofty heroism; you have filled the entire mid-air with your greatness — truly indeed no other is like you.

**1.52.14**  Neither Heaven and Earth have reached the breadth of him, nor have the rivers reached the end of his realm; not even his own rain in the rapture of him fighting — he alone has made all things successive, one after another.

**1.52.15**  Here the Maruts were singing praises in this very battle; all the gods were gladdened following you; when with the pointed weapon, O Indra, you struck down Vṛtra face to face, smiting him down.

**1.53.1**  Now we bring forth speech, carry hymns to the great Indra at the seat of Vivasvat; even now one finds a jewel as if of sleepers — ill praise is not proclaimed at the giving of wealth.

**1.53.2**  You are the door of the horse, the door of the cow, O Indra; the door of barley, the lord and master of wealth; you who from long ago instruct men, who never diminishes those who desire — that friend to friends we now praise.

**1.53.3**  O Indra of mighty deeds, doer of many acts, most brilliant — this wealth all around is understood to be yours; therefore seize it, O all-conquering one, and bring it here; do not diminish the desire of the singer who yearns toward you.

**1.53.4**  With these days, well-minded, with these Soma-drops, keeping darkness away with rays of light, with the two bay-horses; splitting the Dasyu with Indra, with the Soma-drops — may we of active hatred grasp at the impulse together.

**1.53.5**  May we grasp together with Indra's wealth, together with impulse, with prizes of many splendors overwhelming the sky; together with the divine great-thought, the heroic-vigored one — may we seize together that which is led by kine and furnished with horses.

**1.53.6**  Those raptures intoxicated you, those virile powers were yours; those Soma-draughts in the slayings of Vṛtra, O lord of good men; when for the singer Kāra you cut down ten Vṛtra-like ones unresisting and thousands for the Soma-strewer.

**1.53.7**  Bold you come to battle from battle, fortress by fortress you smash them with force; when in the friendship of Namya in the far distance, O Indra, you slew Namuci, the sorcerer by name.

**1.53.8**  You slew Karañja and Parṇaya with the sharpest weapon, on the track of Atithigva; you split the hundred fortresses of Vaṅgṛda — irresistible, urged on all sides by Ṛjiśvan.

**1.53.9**  You pinned down those twenty kings of men who had come together against Suśravas, the kinless one; sixty thousand and ninety-nine thousands, O celebrated one, you rolled down with the chariot-wheel — those hard to tread upon.

**1.53.10**  You aided Suśravas with your helps, with your protections, O Indra — and Tūrvayāṇa; you gave as allies Kutsa, Atithigva, and Āyu to this young great king.

**1.53.11**  May we be your most auspicious, god-guarded friends, O Indra, surpassing others; we shall praise you, and with you as our support, full of good heroes, may we hold a longer, more extended life.

**1.54.1**  Do not leave us in distress in these battles, O Maghavan — for there is no end to the encompassing of your might; you made the rivers cry out, you made the forests roar — how would the earthly regions not come together in fear?

**1.54.2**  Sing to Śakra, the powerful one, the deed-mighty; honoring the hearing Indra, sing praises; who as a bull, exercising bull-power with bold might, anoints and adorns the two worlds — the bull of bulls.

**1.54.3**  Sing a vigorous word fit for the great heaven — his whose bold mind is self-sovereign and bold; the Asura of great fame, made mighty by his greatness — for his is the bull-chariot driven forward by the bay steeds.

**1.54.4**  You set the ridge of great heaven shaking; with bold selfhood you split apart Śambara; when, bold in exhilaration, you attack the sorcerers and the braggers, hurling the sharpened thunderbolt-ray.

**1.54.5**  When you press down on the head of the snorter, and even the forests roar at the bragging Śuṣṇa; with the forward-moving mind that has its greatness — whatever you do even today, who encompasses you?

**1.54.6**  You aided the manly Turvaśa and Yadu; you aided Turvīti and Vayya, O hundred-thoughted one; you placed the chariot of Etaśa in the contested prize; you beguiled ninety-nine fortresses.

**1.54.7**  That king, the lord of good men, has grown great — the people whose oblations are given, who furthers the command; who responds to the hymns with bounty — for him the upper heavenly stream swells with gifts.

**1.54.8**  Matchless be the lordship, matchless the thought — these Soma-drinkers, let them be foremost with their deed; those who, O Indra, increase the great, firm, and virile sovereignty of the giver.

**1.54.9**  For you these many cups, milked by the pressing-stone, sitting in the cups made for Indra to drink; enjoy them fully, satiate their desire, and then turn your mind toward giving wealth.

**1.54.10**  The darkness that calls its supporting frame, the mountain lodged within the belly of Vṛtra, blocked the waters; Indra strikes them downward — all the rivers that were shut in by Vṛtra's coil — along the slopes.

**1.54.11**  O Indra, the most powerful, place upon us the strengthening brightness and the great lordship, the people-conquering; protect us, O Maghavan, guard our patrons, and establish for us wealth, good offspring, and food.

**1.55.1**  Even the breadth of heaven spread wide — even Earth does not match Indra in greatness; fearful and powerful, he blazes against the peoples; he whets the thunderbolt for brilliance like a forest-bird whets its beak.

**1.55.2**  Like ocean-going rivers, the broad ones, he receives the scattered ones with his expanses; Indra rages like a bull for the drinking of Soma; from of old he, the warrior, is celebrated for his strength.

**1.55.3**  You, O Indra, for enjoyment — as it were a mountain — put in motion the laws of great manly power; you are radiant through your heroism beyond the divine measure; fierce, you are the foremost priest for every deed.

**1.55.4**  He is honored by those who do reverence in the forest, proclaiming Indra's nature beautifully among the peoples; a bull of pleasure, he becomes desirable as a bull; Maghavan is a bull when he impels the cow of prayer in peace.

**1.55.5**  He performs great battles with his greatness, with his warrior's strength, for the peoples; and then they place faith in the brilliant one — in Indra who ever and again smites with the weapon, the thunderbolt.

**1.55.6**  For he is fame-seeking; growing great on the earth in strength, he destroys the artificial abodes; creating the lights safe from wolves for the worshipper, the wise-purposed one let loose the waters to flow down.

**1.55.7**  O Soma-drinker, let your mind be for giving; turn your two bay steeds hitherward, hearing the greeting; your most well-reined charioteers, O Indra — the passionate ones do not deceive you, who perceives them.

**1.55.8**  You bear in your two hands the inexhaustible wealth; invincible strength is lodged in your body, O celebrated one; those who turn about you like makers turning about wells — in your persons, O Indra, your powers are manifold.

**1.56.1**  He pushes forth, pressing down many a one of that active-force, active like a steed raising up a woman — he who is agitated; he causes the golden, chariot-laden resource to be yoked with the Hari-horses, richly wrought.

**1.56.2**  To him go the agreeable ones, the full of oblations, the abundant — those about to win, like the meeting-place of the ocean; mount with brilliance over the lord of counsel and might, the ruler of the wide folk.

**1.56.3**  He is great in conquest, dustless in his manhood, his force gleams like the spike of a mountain; he who by his sorcery-power in exhilaration caused impetuous Śuṣṇa to rest, setting him in bonds though fierce.

**1.56.4**  If the divine strengthening aid of him who grows through you, O Indra, follows like the sun follows dawn — the one who with bold might pushes away the darkness, who stirs the great dust in the loud resounding.

**1.56.5**  When he stood over the unshaken, supporting realm, traversing the atmospheric space athwart it in his greatness at the stations of heaven; in the intoxication striving for the sun, O Indra, you drove off Vṛtra and pressed out the flood of the waters.

**1.56.6**  You support the foundation of heaven with your power, O Indra, great in the seats of the earth; in the exhilaration of the pressed Soma you released the waters; you broke the banks of Vṛtra all together.

**1.57.1**  I bring a thought to the most bounteous, the great one of great wealth, of true vigor, the powerful; whose bounty, all-sustaining, opened for strength and hard to hold back like waters down a slope.

**1.57.2**  Then all the world shall rejoice following your will, the pressings of the oblation-bearer like waters following the downward slope; when the golden thunderbolt of Indra, the desirable one, that pierces and strikes, alights on the mountain.

**1.57.3**  Bring with homage at the sacrifice to this fearful, admirable one — bright as the Dawn; whose name-station of renown is Indriya-power — the light was made, the bay steeds are driven onward.

**1.57.4**  These are we, O Indra, much praised — we who move about taking hold of you, O lord of abundant wealth; for no other, O lover of songs, can receive songs as you can — like the earthly regions, accept our speech.

**1.57.5**  Manifold is your heroic power, O Indra — we belong to it; fill the desire of this singer, O Maghavan; the vast heaven follows your heroic power, and this earth bows down to you for your strength.

**1.57.6**  You, O Indra, O thunderbolt-bearer, cut that great and broad mountain joint by joint with your thunderbolt; you released the waters, held back, to flow; you hold all strength utterly alone.

**1.58.1**  Even now the immortal, born of strength, stirs up — the Hotṛ who became the messenger of Vivasvat; he seeks to gain the divine companionship with oblation, traversing the realm with most direct paths.

**1.58.2**  The ageless one, ever youthful, eating his own food, eagerly active, stands in the dry places; his back gleams like a steed sprinkled with drops; thundering he has cried out like the ridge of heaven.

**1.58.3**  Active together with the Rudras and Vasus, installed as the Purohita, the immortal Hotṛ is seated, the wealth-conquering one; like a chariot adorning itself among the peoples, the god among the living sends forth desirable things one after another.

**1.58.4**  Wind-impelled, he stands in the dry places, freely spreading his tongues like a scythe, loud-roaring; when, O Agni, you make love to the dry trees, your black track shines brightly, O ageless one of bright waves.

**1.58.5**  Hot-jawed, in the forest, impelled by the wind, blowing down like one calling in a herd, the reed-striker; attacking the unwasting firmament with his might, even the stationary and the moving fear his wings.

**1.58.6**  The Bhṛgus established you among men, beautiful like wealth, easy to invoke for the peoples; as Hotṛ, O Agni, a welcome guest, auspicious as a friend, for the heavenly birth.

**1.58.7**  I worship Agni the Hotṛ, the most sacrificial of seven tongues of flame, whom the inspired singers choose at the rites; Agni the intermediary of all the Vasus — I approach with delight and seek the jewel.

**1.58.8**  O son of Strength, today extend to us, to the singers, an unbroken shelter, O great as Mitra; O Agni, O offspring of Strength, protect the singer from distress with iron fortresses.

**1.58.9**  Be a refuge to the singer, O brilliant one; be a shelter for the generous to the generous, O Maghavan; O Agni, protect the singer from distress — may the thought-rich one come quickly at dawn.

**1.59.1**  The other fires are but branches of you, O Agni; all the immortals rejoice in you; you, O Vaiśvānara, are the navel of the peoples — like a pillar you have pervaded the peoples.

**1.59.2**  Agni is the head of heaven, the navel of the earth, and then became the energizing messenger between the two worlds; the gods begot you as a god, O Vaiśvānara — truly a light for the Ārya.

**1.59.3**  Like firm rays in the sun, riches are deposited in Vaiśvānara Agni; those that are in mountains, in plants, in waters — of those riches in men you are king.

**1.59.4**  Like the vast heaven and earth to their son, the hymns — the Hotṛ of humans, skilled as a sage; many and young hymns for the sun-possessing, truly vigorous Vaiśvānara, the most manly one.

**1.59.5**  Even from great heaven, O Jātavedas, your greatness surpasses, O Vaiśvānara; you are king of the human peoples; in battle you have made wide space for the gods.

**1.59.6**  Now I shall proclaim the greatness of the bull whom the Pūrus accompany as the Vṛtra-slayer; Vaiśvānara Agni, having slain the Dasyu, shook loose the firesticks, split apart Śambara.

**1.59.7**  Vaiśvānara, of all peoples by his greatness, the adorable, self-brilliant one, is to be worshipped among the Bharadvājas; Agni, the truth-speaking one, is praised with hundredfold songs at the Śātavaneyī, at the many-named one.

**1.60.1**  The carrier, the glorious, the banner of the assembly, the easily invocable, the messenger who brings immediate purpose; the double-born one, praised like a treasure — Mātariśvan brought this gift to Bhṛgu.

**1.60.2**  Both kinds attend on his command — those who bear oblations, both the inspired poets and the mortals; even foremost among those of heaven he was set down as the Hotṛ, the worthy of question, the lord of the folk, the ordainer among the settlements.

**1.60.3**  May our fair praise reach him ever newly born in the heart — the honey-tongued one; whom the priests, the human Āyus pressing the Soma in the enclosed space, begetting, brought to birth.

**1.60.4**  The sage, the purifier, the good one among humans, the choiceworthy Hotṛ was placed among the settlements; the dweller in the house, the householder at home — may Agni become master of riches, lord of wealth.

**1.60.5**  You as master of riches, O Agni — we the Gotamas praise you with our thoughts; grooming you like a swift prize-winner — may the thought-rich one come quickly at dawn.

**1.61.1**  To him alone, the mighty and swift one, I carry praise as an offering — a hymn to the great one; to Ṛcīṣama, to Adhrigava, to Indra I bring forth the most generous prayers.

**1.61.2**  To him alone I now advance, as to an offering, I bring the hymn of praise and noble word; to Indra, with heart and mind and wisdom, the ancient lord — they polish their devotions.

**1.61.3**  To him alone I bring that supreme, sun-winning praise in my mouth; the most glorious one, with the swift outpourings of thoughts, with noble words, to exalt the inspired lord.

**1.61.4**  To him alone I drive together the hymn of praise, as a craftsman fashions a chariot for its fitter; and songs with noble word to the praise-borne Indra, the all-stirring, the wise.

**1.61.5**  To him alone, seeking renown as one grooms a swift horse, I anoint the hymn with my tongue for Indra; to honor the hero who is home for generous gifts, of joyous fame, the breaker of citadels.

**1.61.6**  For him alone Tvaṣṭr fashioned the vajra, most excellently wrought, heavenly, for battle; with it he found the vital spot even of Vṛtra — the sovereign one, striking, with his striking force of purpose.

**1.61.7**  He it was who, at his mother's pressings, straightway, the great one, having drunk of sweet food and fair nourishment — Viṣṇu, the more powerful, despoiled what was being cooked; the archer pierced the boar through the mountain.

**1.61.8**  To him alone even the divine wives, the consorts of the gods, have sung the hymn to Indra at the slaying of the serpent; he upheld the wide heaven and earth — no one encompasses his greatness.

**1.61.9**  He indeed has surpassed in greatness heaven and earth and the atmosphere; the self-ruling Indra, praised by all in the house, the heaven-goer, the irresistible, has swelled himself for battle.

**1.61.10**  He it was who with his power clove Vṛtra withering with the vajra, Indra; like cows seeking the streams, he released the waters — the mindful one for the giving of renown.

**1.61.11**  By his very fierceness the rivers rejoiced when he extended them with the vajra; he who makes sovereignty for the worshipper, honoring Turvīta — who is it that made a ford for Turvaṇi?

**1.61.12**  To him alone, rushing eagerly, wielding sovereign force of will, he sent forward the vajra against Vṛtra; like the joints of a cow he split them asunder, shooting across, that the floods of the waters might flow.

**1.61.13**  Proclaim anew his ancient deeds of the swift one with fresh hymns: when he, hurtling his weapons to the fight, raging, drives away the foes.

**1.61.14**  Even the firm mountains and heaven and earth tremble in fear at his birth; Nodhā, approaching the shelter of the beloved one, crying loudly, instantly became vigorous for heroic deeds.

**1.61.15**  To him it is given in consequence — the one who, sole, as sovereign, has gained much: Indra aided Praetaśa, contending with the sun, pressing soma well for the sake of good horses.

**1.61.16**  Thus, O Indra, yoke of the tawny steeds, the Gotamas have fashioned these prayers of noble word for you; fix on us the all-adorning vision — may he come swiftly at dawn, rich in wisdom.

**1.62.1**  We are preparing praise, a roaring hymn, for the mighty one who loves songs, in the manner of the Aṅgirases; with noble hymns, for the hymn-lord who is being praised, let us sing the sacred verse to the far-renowned hero.

**1.62.2**  Carry forth great reverence to the great one; sing the hymn worthy of praise to the mighty one — that by which our ancient forefathers, the track-knowing Aṅgirases, singing, found the cows.

**1.62.3**  At the bidding of Indra and the Aṅgirases, Saramā found the sustenance for her offspring; Bṛhaspati split the rock, found the cows — men bellowed together with the morning rays.

**1.62.4**  He with the excellent shout, with praise, with the seven sages, the heavenly one, with the Navagvas, with his voice split the rock; O Indra, O mighty one, with the swift ones and the Daśagvas you shattered Vala with a roar.

**1.62.5**  Being praised by the Aṅgirases, O wondrous one, you opened the darkness with the dawn, the sun, the cows of light; you extended the ridge of the earth, O Indra, and propped up the highest realm of the sky.

**1.62.6**  That is indeed his most praiseworthy deed, the most wonderful exploit of the wondrous one: that in the depths, the upper waters swelled the four honey-flooded rivers.

**1.62.7**  Ayāsya opened the two who were born together, nesting together, with the songs of those who praise; the wondrously mighty one sustained the two world-halves, considering himself like Bhaga in the highest vault.

**1.62.8**  From of old, the two of different form move around heaven and earth, the two youthful ones who are reborn by their own paths; Night with dark forms and Dawn with shining forms move alternately, each one after the other.

**1.62.9**  Working with skill he always maintains the friendship — the son, the wondrously mighty one, upheld with power; even in the raw you place the cooked within — bright milk in the dark cows and in the ruddy ones.

**1.62.10**  From of old the nestmates protect the windless streams, their ordinances with their powers as immortals; many thousands, like wives for their husbands, the sisters minister to the one who fears no shame.

**1.62.11**  Seeking life's continuance with reverence and fresh hymns, the thoughts of those who desire wealth, O wondrous one, have run to you; like wives, eager, touching an eager husband, the inspired thoughts touch you, O powerful one.

**1.62.12**  From of old the riches in your hands do not diminish, do not fail, O wondrous one; you are glorious, O Indra, rich in will, steadfast — help us, O mighty one, with your powers.

**1.62.13**  Gotama has fashioned for Indra, yoke of the tawny steeds, a new prayer for one who is ancient; O mighty one, be our guide, O Nodhā — may he come swiftly at dawn, rich in wisdom.

**1.63.1**  You are the great one, O Indra, who at birth by your powers shook heaven and earth; when even all mountains, the mighty ones, firm, trembling in fear, shed their rays before you.

**1.63.2**  When, O Indra, the two tawny steeds were yoked and the worshipper placed the vajra in your two arms — with it, O Indra of unerring will, O much-invoked, you drive away the enemies' many citadels.

**1.63.3**  You are true, O Indra, bold — these things are yours; you are the Ṛbhukṣa, the manly one, you the conqueror; you, O Indra, overthrew Śuṣṇa in the enclosure at the battle of food, accompanying young Kutsa, the gleaming one.

**1.63.4**  You, O Indra, as a friend urged on that deed, when O vajra-bearer, O bull of bull-deeds, you held Vṛtra fast; when, O hero, O bull-minded one, turning the dasyus away, you scattered them in their lair, the unvanquished conqueror.

**1.63.5**  You, O Indra, protected that — unharmed, even when the firm ones were unwelcome to mortals; the limit is far from us for the horse — O vajra-bearer, strike the enemies like a mace.

**1.63.6**  You it is, O Indra, whom the men call upon in battle where floods are won, where the heavenly prize is coveted; this help of yours, O self-empowered one, shall be here in the encounter, inexhaustible in the prizes.

**1.63.7**  You, O Indra, fighting, crushed the seven citadels for Purukutsa, O vajra-bearer; when, as the barhis unrolled for Sudās without resistance, you made wide space from the narrow pass for Pūru, O king.

**1.63.8**  You, O Indra, O god, have swelled for us that wondrous refreshment like waters, O far-roamer; with which, O hero, you bestow upon us self-sustaining strength, to flow for us as nourishment everywhere.

**1.63.9**  These prayers have been made for you, O Indra, by the Gotamas, spoken with reverence for the two tawny steeds; bring us a richly ornamented prize — may he come swiftly at dawn, rich in wisdom.

**1.64.1**  O Nodhā, carry forth a noble hymn to the mighty host, the fine-jawed, the wise, to the Maruts; like water, serene in mind, nimble of hand, I anoint the songs present at the assemblies.

**1.64.2**  They were born from heaven, the tall bulls, the youths of Rudra, the lordly ones, the stainless; purifying, bright as suns, valiant like drops, of terrible form.

**1.64.3**  Young, the Rudras, ageless, smashing, they have grown up irresistible like mountains; even the firm, all the earthly beings they set in motion, and the heavenly ones, by their greatness.

**1.64.4**  With bright ornaments they anoint themselves for beauty; they have placed golden ornaments on their chests for splendor; spears cling to their shoulders — together the men of heaven were born by their own power.

**1.64.5**  Creating sovereignty, roaring, consuming enemies, they have made the winds and lightnings with their powers; the shakers milk the heavenly udder, the far-runners swell the earth with milk.

**1.64.6**  The Maruts, good givers, swell the waters at the assemblies, milk that is like ghee; they lead away the prize-winning one like a stallion through the mist, they milk the thundering, inexhaustible spring.

**1.64.7**  Buffalo-like, magical, of wondrous brilliance, self-mighty like mountains, swift in descent; like wild elephants you devour the forests, when you harnessed your powers in the ruddy ones.

**1.64.8**  They roar like lions, the wise ones; beautifully adorned like Piśācas, all-knowing; quickening the nights with their dappled mares and spears, truly formidable in their rage and strength.

**1.64.9**  The two world-halves speak of the splendor of the host — the heroes who accompany men, formidable in strength and wrath; on your chariot poles visible like a prayer, like lightning stood the lightning in your chariots, O Maruts.

**1.64.10**  All-knowing, dwelling together with riches, mingling with powers, broad-bodied; the men who devour bulls, of boundless might, have placed their arrow in their hands as shooters.

**1.64.11**  With golden wheel-rims, swelling with milk, lifting up the mountains as if off the road; high-spirited, untiring, self-flowing, shaking what is firm, the Maruts of shining spears.

**1.64.12**  The impetuous, purifying, resplendent, far-seeing son of Rudra we praise with our call; follow the dust-crossing, mighty troop of Maruts, those who press soma, the bulls, for beauty.

**1.64.13**  Now indeed that mortal has surpassed men in might, whom you, O Maruts, have protected with aid; he bears the prize with his steeds, wins wealth with men of noble will — he dwells and prospers.

**1.64.14**  O Maruts, place among the generous ones an energy celebrated everywhere, irresistible in battles, glorious; the prize-winning, hymn-worthy, all-beholding — may we foster offspring and progeny for a hundred winters.

**1.64.15**  Now, O Maruts, place among us a firm, hero-accompanied wealth, victorious in every onset — thousandfold, hundredfold, ever increasing; may he come swiftly at dawn, rich in wisdom.

**1.65.1**  Like a thief hiding in secret among the cattle — harnessing reverence, bearing reverence.

**1.65.2**  The wise ones, of one mind, followed with steps; all the adorable ones sat down near you.

**1.65.3**  The gods went in accordance with the ordinances of Ṛta; may there be encompassment like heaven over the earth.

**1.65.4**  The swelling waters with admiration, the well-born in the womb of Ṛta — the young calf beautifully born.

**1.65.5**  Like nourishment that is joyful, like a broad settlement, like a mountain that bends, like a benign wave.

**1.65.6**  Like a swift horse poured out at a race, like a river's wave — who shall hold him back?

**1.65.7**  Kinsman of the rivers, like a brother to his sisters, like a king having elephants, he devours the forests.

**1.65.8**  When, urged by the wind, he stands apart in the forests — Agni indeed gives the hair of the earth.

**1.65.9**  He breathes in the waters like a swan sitting — most conspicuous in will, the arouser of the settlements at dawn.

**1.65.10**  Like Soma the ordainer, born from Ṛta, like a young animal, the all-pervading one shining from afar.

**1.66.1**  Like a wonderful treasure, like the sight of the sun, like the breath of life, like an eternal son.

**1.66.2**  Like a swift one blazing he clings to the forests; like milk in a cow, the pure one, the bright-shining.

**1.66.3**  He upholds welfare, delightful like a home, like ripened barley, the conqueror of peoples.

**1.66.4**  Like a ṛṣi praised with songs among the clans, like a contented prize-winner, he establishes vigor.

**1.66.5**  Blazing in a hard-to-leave abode, like an eternal will, like a wife in the womb, fit for all.

**1.66.6**  When the wondrous one shines without clouds, like a white one among the clans, like a golden chariot, formidable in battles.

**1.66.7**  Like an army let loose he maintains the fastest; like the lightning of the archer, of terrible face.

**1.66.8**  He is indeed Yama when born, Yama in birthhood, the lover of maidens, the lord of wives.

**1.66.9**  Him we approach moving as your dwelling; like cows going home they seek the kindled one.

**1.66.10**  Like a river's wave pressing the downward courses onward, the cows have rejoiced at the sight of the sun.

**1.67.1**  Born in the forests, Mitra among mortals, he chooses the obedience of one not growing old, like a king.

**1.67.2**  Good like welfare, auspicious like will — may the Hotā, the carrier of offerings, be independent in thought.

**1.67.3**  Holding all heroic powers in his hand, unconquered, he placed the gods while sitting in secret.

**1.67.4**  Men knowing this — those who fix their minds — when they proclaimed the mantra-words carved with the heart.

**1.67.5**  Like an unborn one he upheld the earth, propped up the sky with true mantras.

**1.67.6**  Protect the dear footsteps of the animal; O Agni, all-life, go from hiding place to hiding place.

**1.67.7**  He who knows him dwelling in secret, who has sat down at the stream of Ṛta —

**1.67.8**  Those who part the rites and cherish them — indeed the goods have been proclaimed for him.

**1.67.9**  He who, by greatness, grew among the plants — and also progeny, and within the seeds.

**1.67.10**  Intelligence in the house of the waters, all-life — the wise ones, as if at a dwelling, united and acted.

**1.68.1**  Glowing he stood near heaven, the eager one; he uncovered the nights of the standing and the moving.

**1.68.2**  When he, one among them all, became the god of all gods by his greatness.

**1.68.3**  Then all of them enjoyed your will, when, O god, you were born living from the dry.

**1.68.4**  All shared in the name divinity, cherishing Ṛta and the immortal with their ways.

**1.68.5**  By the impulse of Ṛta, by the thought of Ṛta, the all-life — all performed the works.

**1.68.6**  Who sacrifices to you, or who serves you — to him, the knowing one, assign wealth.

**1.68.7**  The Hotā seated in the offspring of Manu — he indeed is the lord of these riches.

**1.68.8**  Seeking seed mutually in their bodies, they came to know each other by their own capacities, the wise ones.

**1.68.9**  Like sons their father's will, they enjoyed it — those swift ones who heeded his command.

**1.68.10**  For wealth the much-nourishing one opened the doors, adorned the firmament with stars — the house-friend.

**1.69.1**  Bright, blazing, like the lover of Dawn, filling both regions like the light of heaven.

**1.69.2**  Born around, you became by your will; being the son, you became the father of the gods.

**1.69.3**  Agni the ordainer, unweakened, discerning — like the udder of cows, the sweet tasting of nourishments.

**1.69.4**  Dear to the folk, being one to be invoked, seated in the middle, delightful in the house.

**1.69.5**  Like a son born, delightful in the house — like a pleased prize-winner, he has penetrated the clans.

**1.69.6**  When he, dwelling together with men and the clans, called out — Agni through divinity obtained all.

**1.69.7**  No one diminishes these ordinances of yours, since you have made readiness for these men.

**1.69.8**  This indeed is your wondrous deed, that you struck with equals, that when yoked with men you warded off blemishes.

**1.69.9**  Like the lover of Dawn, the bright one, the dawn-colored — let him of the well-known form shine for him.

**1.69.10**  Bearing by themselves, they opened the doors; all rejoiced at the sight of the sun.

**1.70.1**  May we win the many gifts of the lord with wisdom; Agni, brilliantly glowing, has obtained all things.

**1.70.2**  The knowing one, he has filled the divine ordinances, has filled the birth of human folk.

**1.70.3**  He who is the embryo of the waters, the embryo of the forests, the embryo of the standing things, the embryo of the moving things.

**1.70.4**  Even in the rock, within the house, like the all-encompassing one of the clans, the immortal, independent in thought.

**1.70.5**  For he is the possessor of nights — Agni of riches — he gives to him who rightly serves with well-sung hymns.

**1.70.6**  O knowing one, protect these existences; O knower of the birth of gods and of mortals.

**1.70.7**  Him whom the many nights of varied form increased — both the standing and the moving, the chariot led by Ṛta.

**1.70.8**  The Hotā has succeeded, seated in heaven, performing all true works.

**1.70.9**  You are set for fame among the cows, among the forests; may all bring tribute — bring us the sun.

**1.70.10**  Men honor you in many places; like that of a living father, they carry the knowledge to you.

**1.70.11**  Like a good one, eager like a thrower — a hero; like a fierce one on the march, formidable in battles.

**1.71.1**  Gladdening the eager ones, the eager lord, coming near — the clans dwelling together like an eternal husband; the sisters enjoyed the dark one and the ruddy one, like cows the wondrous shining Dawn.

**1.71.2**  With hymns our fathers, the Aṅgirases, smashed even the firm and solid rock with their shout; they made a path for us in the great heaven — they found the day, the sun, the light of the morning rays.

**1.71.3**  Establishing Ṛta, enriching his thought — thus the lord's eager ones, the maintained ones; the unweary works go toward the gods, nourishing their birth with delight.

**1.71.4**  When Mātariśvan, the maintained one, churned him and the white one became the conqueror house by house; then the Bhṛgu-born one, accompanying as with a powerful king, has distributed the office of messenger.

**1.71.5**  When the dappled knowing one wrought his sap for the great father and heaven, the calf ran forward; the bold archer let the lightning fly for him — the god placed his brilliance in his own daughter.

**1.71.6**  He who shines for you in your own house, or gives homage day after day eagerly — he is your growth, O Agni; doubly great the vigor of that one; with riches may he go in the same chariot whom you urge forward.

**1.71.7**  All nourishments attend upon Agni, like the seven great rivers flowing to the ocean; his strength has not been distinguished by kinswomen — the knowing one, find wisdom for us among the gods.

**1.71.8**  When the brilliance reached the lord of men for impulse, the pure seed was poured in — heaven directly; Agni begot and purified the young, blameless host, independent in thought.

**1.71.9**  He who, like the mind, goes instantly along the paths — alone, together, the sun is lord of goods; the two kings Mitra and Varuṇa of beautiful hands, guarding the dear immortal one among the cows.

**1.71.10**  O Agni, do not forget the ancestral friendships, being a knowing poet; old age diminishes the form as the cloud does — before that danger, take thought for us.

**1.72.1**  The ordainer has continually laid down the poetic powers, holding many heroic things in his hand; Agni has become the lord of riches, the maker together of all immortal things.

**1.72.2**  The immortals, the wise ones, seeking, did not find the calf present among us; the wearied, track-following, mind-setting ones stood at the highest beautiful station of Agni.

**1.72.3**  For three autumns, O Agni, the pure ones worshipping you the pure one with ghee — even the sacred names they established; the well-born ones quickened their bodies.

**1.72.4**  Continuously filling the two great world-halves, the adorable Rudrian ones bore themselves; a mortal, thoughtful, knowing, found Agni standing at the highest station.

**1.72.5**  Recognizing, they sat down kneeling, those with wives paying homage to the adorable one; they made their own bodies grow thin — friend protecting friend in an instant.

**1.72.6**  The three-times-seven secrets that are in you — the adorable ones, depositing them, found the footsteps; with these they guard the immortal, of one accord — protect both the cattle, the standing ones, and the moving.

**1.72.7**  O Agni, knowing the arrangements of the settlements, you placed the glories one after another for living; knowing the inner paths that lead to the gods, untiring, you became the messenger and carrier of oblations.

**1.72.8**  Self-knowing, the seven great ones of heaven, knowing Ṛta, opened the doors of wealth; Saramā found the firm, enclosed cattle-pen — thereby now the human clan enjoys.

**1.72.9**  They who have obtained all good progeny, making the path to immortality; the earth extended with the greatness of the great ones — Aditi the mother with her sons for the nourishment of the bird.

**1.72.10**  They placed the lovely glory in him, when the immortals made the two eyes of heaven; then the rivers flow like those let loose — the ruddy ones going downward, O Agni, came to knowledge.

**1.73.1**  He who is like riches acquired by the fathers, the establisher of vigor, like the good guidance of the knowing — like a welcome guest of restful disposition being pleased, like a Hotā he penetrated the house of the worshippers.

**1.73.2**  He who, like the god Savitṛ, is true in thought — by his will he guards all the communities; widely praised, the prayer like truth — like the self he became dear, worthy of desire.

**1.73.3**  He who, like the god, dwells near the earth as the all-sustainer — like a king who is a beneficent friend; like heroes sitting in front, sitting in shelter — blameless like a wife beloved by her husband.

**1.73.4**  You — men attend to you always kindled in the house, O Agni, in the firm settlements; on you they placed abundant glory — be the all-life, the foundation of riches.

**1.73.5**  O Agni, may the generous ones win the nourishments, may the inspired ones win who give all life; may we win the prize in gatherings, the lord placing his share among the gods for glory.

**1.73.6**  For the cows of Ṛta, lowing, with full udders, have swelled, shared with the day; seeking good-will from the distance, the rivers have run evenly against the mountain.

**1.73.7**  In you, O Agni, seeking good will, the adorable ones established fame in heaven; they made the night and dawn of different form, and mixed the dark color and the ruddy.

**1.73.8**  The mortals whom, O Agni, you, the purifier, direct toward wealth — may we too be among those generous ones; like a shade you embrace all existence, having filled the two worlds and the atmosphere.

**1.73.9**  Protected by you, O Agni, may we win with our horses the horses of others, with our men the men — heroes; as lords of ancestral riches, may the inspired ones win a hundred winters for us.

**1.73.10**  May these prayers of yours, O Agni, O ordainer, be pleasing to your mind and heart; may we be capable of steering your wealth well — placing upon ourselves the god-granted glory.

**1.74.1**  Approaching the sacrifice, let us speak the sacred word to Agni who hears from afar and from near.

**1.74.2**  He who was the foremost among the closely allied clans that have come together — he protected the house of the worshipper.

**1.74.3**  Let the folk also say: 'Agni, the Vṛtra-slayer, was born, the winner of wealth in every battle.'

**1.74.4**  Whose messenger you are in his seat of power, you travel for the enjoyment of offerings — from him you make the sacrifice wonderful.

**1.74.5**  Him indeed, O Aṅgiras, O son of strength, the people call one of good offering, of good divinity, of good barhis.

**1.74.6**  You carry hither those gods here for acclaim — the oblations, O brilliant one, for their enjoyment.

**1.74.7**  Neither any noise of horses nor of the chariot is heard, when, O Agni, you go on your divine mission.

**1.74.8**  Protected by you, the prize-winner without reproach surpassed the one before him — the worshipper, O Agni, has set forth.

**1.74.9**  And you, O Agni, solicit from the gods the glorious and heroic, the great, for the worshipper, O god.

**1.75.1**  Enjoy the most widely spreading speech, the most god-beautifying — pouring oblations in your mouth.

**1.75.2**  Then may we speak to you, O most Aṅgiras-like Agni, O most ordaining one, the dear sacred prayer with a sharpened edge.

**1.75.3**  Who among the folk is your kinsman, O Agni? Who offers sacrifice in the rite? Who indeed are you leaning upon?

**1.75.4**  You are the kinsman among the folk, O Agni, you are the dear Mitra; a friend worthy of praise to friends.

**1.75.5**  Sacrifice to Mitra and Varuṇa for us, sacrifice to the gods and the great Ṛta; O Agni, worship in your own house.

**1.76.1**  What approach of the mind to its choicest wish, O Agni, would be most beneficent? What prayer? Or who, through sacrifices, has encompassed your skill? With what mind shall we offer to you?

**1.76.2**  Come, O Agni, sit here as Hotṛ; undeceived, be our good leader. May heaven and earth, all-sustaining, protect you. We sacrifice to the gods for gracious favour.

**1.76.3**  Burn away all the rākṣasas thoroughly, O Agni; be the guardian of the sacrifices against malediction. Then bring hither with your two bay horses the lord of Soma — we have prepared hospitality for him, the bountiful giver.

**1.76.4**  The bearer invokes with offspring-granting speech from the mouth; let him sit here together with the gods. You fulfill both the Hotṛ's and the Potṛ's office, O adorable one; be attentive, O leader of the rite, O begetter of riches.

**1.76.5**  As you, a sage yourself, worshipped the gods with the oblations and wise hymns of the inspired Manu — so, O Hotṛ most true, worship today, O Agni, with your gladdening tongue.

**1.77.1**  How shall we serve Agni? What hymn, beloved of the gods, is pronounced to the radiant one? He who, immortal among mortals, truthful, the most sacrificing Hotṛ, truly accomplishes the worship of the gods.

**1.77.2**  He who is most beneficent at sacrifices, the truthful Hotṛ — do honour him with reverences. When Agni chooses the gods for mortal man, then he worships with mind alert.

**1.77.3**  He is the power, he is the hero, he is the good one; like Mitra he has been the charioteer of the marvellous. Him the clans, eager for the gods, invoke first at sacrifices — the wonder-worker, the active one.

**1.77.4**  May he, the most heroic of heroes, Agni the destroyer of foes, come with his protection and favour our thought and hymn — and therewith those who are most bountiful and mightiest, who rouse the prize-contest and inspire devotion.

**1.77.5**  Thus was Agni the truth-possessor, Jātavedas, praised by the Gotamas, the inspired sages. May he among these increase brilliance, and strength, and prosperity; may he come, delighting, taking heed.

**1.78.1**  O Jātavedas, all-beholding one, we the Gotamas praise you with our hymn; with gleaming songs we sound your praise.

**1.78.2**  Him — even you — Gotama, desiring riches, honours with his hymn. With gleaming songs we sound your praise.

**1.78.3**  Him — even you, the greatest winner of prizes — we invoke in the manner of Aṅgiras. With gleaming songs we sound your praise.

**1.78.4**  Him — even you, the greatest slayer of Vṛtra, who shakest off the Dasyus — we praise. With gleaming songs we sound your praise.

**1.78.5**  We the Rahūgaṇas have spoken a honeyed word to Agni. With gleaming songs we sound your praise.

**1.79.1**  Golden-haired, spreading through the expanse of mid-air, the serpent-like one who roars and rushes like the wind — the gleaming dawns, purely shining, bringing news, glorious and energetic, true as those at work.

**1.79.2**  The fair-winged ones struck with their feathers; the black bull bellowed when this appeared. He came as if with auspicious, smiling ones; the mists fall and the clouds thunder.

**1.79.3**  When he, nourished by the milk of ṛta, guiding along the straightest paths of ṛta — Aryamā, Mitra, Varuṇa the wanderer, they blend the skin in the womb of the lower one.

**1.79.4**  O Agni, O youth of strength, lord of prize wealth rich in cattle — bestow on us, O Jātavedas, great renown.

**1.79.5**  He, the sage, the wealth-bearer, Agni, being kindled, worthy of praise by hymns — shine richly for us, O you whose front is first.

**1.79.6**  O king of the night, and by yourself, O Agni, of the morning and of the dawn — you of sharp fangs, burn the rākṣasas back in return.

**1.79.7**  Protect us, O Agni, with your aids in the bringing forth of the Gāyatra metre; you who are worthy of praise in all spiritual insights.

**1.79.8**  Bring us, O Agni, riches that altogether overcome the foe, excelling, irresistible in all combats.

**1.79.9**  Bring us, O Agni, wealth with clear wisdom, nourishing all life — grant us your grace to live.

**1.79.10**  Send forth purified words to Agni of sharp flame, O Gotamas — carry hymns cheerfully.

**1.79.11**  Whoever assails us, O Agni, whether near or far — let him fall, let him trip; be for our increase alone.

**1.79.12**  The thousand-eyed, all-perceiving Agni drives away the rākṣasas; the Hotṛ is hymned with praises.

**1.80.1**  For thus indeed, exhilarated by Soma, the chanting priest wrought enhancement. O mightiest wielder of the vajra, by your power you hewed the serpent out from the earth — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.2**  The exhilarating Soma, the bull-drink, brought by the eagle and pressed, exhilarated you — by which, O vajra-bearer, you smote Vṛtra away from the waters with your might — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.3**  Go forward, approach, be bold — your vajra shall not fail. O Indra, your might is truly heroic power; smite Vṛtra, conquer the waters — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.4**  O Indra, you smote Vṛtra off from the earth, off from heaven; release downward these waters accompanied by the Maruts, these life-sustaining ones — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.5**  Indra, incensed, strikes the roaring Vṛtra's ridge with the vajra; stepping upon him he beats him down, urging the waters onward to their flow — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.6**  Upon the ridge he beats him down with the hundred-jointed vajra; Indra, gladdened by the Soma-drink, seeks a path for his friends — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.7**  O Indra, O boulder-wielding, O vajra-bearer, the heroic deed belongs to you alone, unvanquished — since you slew that magic-wielding beast, him too with your own magic — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.8**  Your vajra-bolts sped apart along ninety rivers; great, O Indra, is your heroic deed; the strength is placed in your two arms — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.9**  A thousand sing together, twenty add their acclaim around him; a hundred echoed after — the brahman uplifted for Indra — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.10**  Indra drove away Vṛtra's might with strength that overpowers strength; great is that heroic deed of his — having slain Vṛtra he set free the waters — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.11**  Even these two great ones trembled with fear before your wrath — when, O Indra, O wielder of the vajra, together with the Maruts you smote Vṛtra with your might — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.12**  Not by his trembling, not by his thundering did Vṛtra terrify Indra; the iron vajra of a thousand spikes came flying at him — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.13**  When, O Indra, you fought Vṛtra and your lightning-bolt with the vajra — your strength that sought to slay the serpent was buckled tight in heaven — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.14**  At your battle-cry, O boulder-wielder, the standing and the moving world trembles; even Tvaṣṭṛ quakes in fear at your wrath, O Indra — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.15**  For surely what we have declared — no one excels Indra in heroic deeds; in him the gods have together placed heroic might, and wisdom, and powers — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.80.16**  The thought which Atharvan, father Manu, and Dadhyañc fashioned of old — in him, O Indra, the brahman-prayers and the recitations came together as before — singing in accord with your own sovereignty.

**1.81.1**  Indra has grown great for exhilaration, for might, as the Vṛtra-slayer, with his heroes; him we invoke both in great and small battles — may he protect us in the contests for prizes.

**1.81.2**  For you are the heroic lord of armies, you are the great giver of gifts in abundance; you are the grower even of the small; you bestow favour on the sacrificing and the pressing man — great is your wealth.

**1.81.3**  When the contests are started and prizes are placed before the bold one — yoke your two steeds who shake with exhilaration; whom will you smite? whom will you place in prosperity? Place us, O Indra, in prosperity.

**1.81.4**  Great by his will, terrible, his might has grown in accordance with himself; for glory, the tall one, near at hand, the lord of the bay horses, placed the iron vajra in his two jaws and hands.

**1.81.5**  He has filled the earthly realm; the luminous regions in heaven are held fast. No one like you, O Indra, has been born, nor shall be born; you have surpassed all things.

**1.81.6**  He who passes on the sustenance of mortal men to the devoted worshipper — may Indra bestow favour on us; distribute your wealth abundantly; may I partake of your bounty.

**1.81.7**  For at each bout of exhilaration the straight-minded one gives us herds of cattle; grasp together many goods from both sides, with hands full; sharpen us with riches, bring wealth.

**1.81.8**  Delight yourself, O hero, together with the pressed Soma, for strength, for bounty; for we know you to be the possessor of many goods — we have released our desires toward you; now be our protector.

**1.81.9**  These, your creatures, O Indra, cause all choiceworthy things to thrive; for among the peoples the possessor can see the wealth of the ungenerous — bring us that wealth.

**1.82.1**  Listen well to our hymns, O bounteous one, as a mother listens. When you shall bless those of us who have pleasant speech — then surely you will be entreated; yoke now, O Indra, your two bay horses.

**1.82.2**  The dear ones ate, they became cheerful; the beloved ones were honoured in abundance; the self-luminous inspired seers praised with their latest thought — yoke now, O Indra, your two bay horses.

**1.82.3**  You who are beautiful to behold, O bounteous one, we would praise; now praised, O you of the fully laden chariot, come following your will — yoke now, O Indra, your two bay horses.

**1.82.4**  He mounts that bull-like, cattle-winning chariot who recognizes, O Indra, the vessel ready-yoked with the bay horses, full to the brim — yoke now, O Indra, your two bay horses.

**1.82.5**  Let both right and left be yoked for you, O hundred-witted one; gladdened, go toward your dear wife with him, go to the Soma-drink — yoke now, O Indra, your two bay horses.

**1.82.6**  With sacred prayer I yoke your two long-maned bay horses; approach, come forward, take them in your two fists. The pressed Soma-juices have boldly exhilarated you; accompanied by Pūṣan, O vajra-bearer, you are jointly exhilarated with your wife.

**1.83.1**  Well protected by your aids, O Indra, the mortal man goes first among those who have horses and cattle. Him you do fill with ever-greater wealth, as the wise waters flow around the river from all sides.

**1.83.2**  Like divine waters the gods approach the worshipper worthy of sacrifice; they look downward as upon an extended realm. The gods with prayers lead forward the one who loves the divine; they cherish the one dear to the brahman, as lords their chosen one.

**1.83.3**  Upon the two he placed praise-worthy speech — the pair who attend with ladles held out in honour; one who is unbound dwells and thrives within your ordinance; auspicious power belongs to the sacrificer and the presser.

**1.83.4**  Then the Aṅgirases first established their strength — those whose fires are kindled, who by their righteous action — they together found out all the sustenance of the Paṇi, the cattle with horses, all the livestock, those heroes.

**1.83.5**  Atharvan first spread the paths through sacrifices; from them the sun, the guardian of ordinances, was born as Vena. Uśanas Kāvya drove the cattle together; we worship the immortal, the son of Yama.

**1.83.6**  Wherever the sacred grass is strewn for the sake of good progeny, or the hymn resounds its echo to the sky, or where the stone speaks and the lauding singer praises — there Indra rejoices at the gatherings.

**1.84.1**  The Soma has been pressed for you, O Indra, O mightiest, O bold one — come. Let the invigorating realm fill you as the sun with its rays.

**1.84.2**  The two bay horses carry hither Indra of irresistible might — to the praises of the seers and to the sacrifice of men.

**1.84.3**  Mount the chariot, O Vṛtra-slayer; your bay horses are yoked by prayer; may the pressing-stone with its sound turn your mind toward us.

**1.84.4**  Drink this Soma that has been pressed, O Indra, the foremost, immortal exhilaration; the streams of the bright one have flowed upon you in the seat of ṛta.

**1.84.5**  Sing praise to Indra now, and speak forth the recitations; the pressed drops of Soma have exhilarated; honour the foremost might.

**1.84.6**  None is a better charioteer than you of your two bay horses when you guide them, O Indra; none matches you in depth, none who has good horses attains to you.

**1.84.7**  He alone who finds and gives wealth to the mortal devotee — the lord, the irresistible — he is none other than Indra indeed.

**1.84.8**  When will he kick away the ungenerous mortal with his foot as a mushroom? When will Indra hearken to our hymns — Indra indeed?

**1.84.9**  Whoever, having pressed Soma, seeks to please you from the many directions — that terrible might rules, and it is Indra indeed.

**1.84.10**  Thus the wild deer drink of the sweet, exhilarating honey in both directions; those who rove together with Indra, the bull, are exhilarated for glory — the auspicious ones, in accord with his own sovereignty.

**1.84.11**  Those dappled ones, loving the dappled, cook the Soma; the dappled ones — dear cows of Indra — urge forward the vajra as a sharp weapon — the auspicious ones, in accord with his own sovereignty.

**1.84.12**  The wise ones serve his might with homage; his many ordinances they have followed for the purpose of foreknowledge — the auspicious ones, in accord with his own sovereignty.

**1.84.13**  Indra, the irresistible, with the bones of Dadhīca slew the ninety-nine Vṛtras.

**1.84.14**  Seeking the horse's head that lay hidden in the mountains, he found it at Śaryaṇāvat.

**1.84.15**  Here they found the secret name of the cow belonging to Tvaṣṭṛ — thus in the house of the moon.

**1.84.16**  Who today yokes to the shaft the cows of ṛta, these violent, fierce, ill-tempered ones, sharp-shooting, quick-striking, beneficent ones — he who discharges the debt of their maintenance shall live.

**1.84.17**  Who is impelled, who is shaken, who fears, who thinks of Indra as present? Who speaks out for offspring, for household, for wealth, for his own body, for his people?

**1.84.18**  Who worships Agni with oblation of ghee, with the ladle, with the fixed seasonal rites? For whom shall the gods quickly bring the invitation of the oblation? Who shall be deemed a sacrificer at the meal, a man of good gods?

**1.84.19**  You yourself, O god most mighty, commend the mortal; O bounteous one, there is no comforter other than you, O Indra — I speak this word to you.

**1.84.20**  May neither your gifts nor your protections, O Vasu, ever delude us. Measure out all human goods for us for the sake of men.

**1.85.1**  They who adorn themselves like women, like swift steeds on the road — the sons of Rudra, of wondrous deeds — the Maruts have made heaven and earth for their growth; the heroes exult in the assemblies, ardent.

**1.85.2**  Having grown to fullness, the Rudras reached their greatness; in heaven they established their seat. Singing the song they generated Indra's power; the sons of Pṛśni laid on themselves their glory.

**1.85.3**  The sons of the cow, when they adorn themselves with ornaments, they place shining gold upon their bodies; they drive away every adversary — along their paths flows the ghee.

**1.85.4**  They who shine splendidly with their lances, moving even the immovable by their force — swift as thought when the Maruts mount their chariots, the troop of bulls, you yoked the dappled mares.

**1.85.5**  When you yoked the dappled mares to the chariots, driving the stone swiftly in the contest, O Maruts — then the streams of the red one flow apart; with waters they thoroughly moisten the earth as with a skin.

**1.85.6**  Let your swift-stepping steeds, swift-flying, carry you hither; come forward with your arms. Sit on the sacred grass — a broad seat has been made for you; delight yourselves, O Maruts, in the Soma draught.

**1.85.7**  They grew by their own might into greatness; they stood at the vault of heaven and made a broad seat. When Viṣṇu strode forward, the exhilaration-shaken bull, like a bird settling on the beloved sacred grass.

**1.85.8**  Indeed like heroes who have battled, like those who come with desire for glory, they have striven in the battles; all beings fear the Maruts — the men are like kings terrible to behold.

**1.85.9**  When Tvaṣṭṛ, the fine craftsman, turned out the well-made golden vajra with its thousand spikes — Indra grasped it to perform heroic deeds; he smote Vṛtra and released the flood of waters.

**1.85.10**  They pushed the well upward with their might; even the firmly established mountain they split apart; blowing their pipe, the Maruts, the good-givers, in the exhilaration of Soma made joyful things.

**1.85.11**  They pushed the well sideways in that direction, pouring forth the spring for Gotama when he was thirsty; those of brilliant lustre, coming with aid, fulfilled the desire of the seer with their stations.

**1.85.12**  Whatever threefold shelters you have — extend them to the devoted worshipper, to the one who has offered. Grant those to us, O Maruts, O bulls; bestow on us wealth with good heroes.

**1.86.1**  That man in whose dwelling the Maruts, the greatly mighty ones, drink from heaven — he is the best-protected of men.

**1.86.2**  You who are conveyed by sacrifices, or by the thoughts of the inspired one — O Maruts, hearken to this call.

**1.86.3**  Or whose prize-winning steeds have sped after the inspired seer — he shall go to the cattle-rich fold.

**1.86.4**  On this hero's sacred grass, at the sacred moments, the Soma is pressed; the recitation and the ecstasy are proclaimed.

**1.86.5**  May all the realms and all that encompasses the peoples hearken to him — even the refreshments that have flowed toward the sun.

**1.86.6**  For with many autumns, O Maruts, we have served you with the aids that belong to the peoples.

**1.86.7**  Happy and blessed, O Maruts who are worthy of sacrifice, may that mortal be, whose offerings you carry across.

**1.86.8**  For the devoted man, or the strenuous one, O men of true might — do find the wish of the longing one.

**1.86.9**  You men of true might, manifest that through your greatness; pierce the rākṣasa with your lightning.

**1.86.10**  Hide away the hidden darkness; drive off every devourer; make the light that we desire.

**1.87.1**  Waxing great in body, powerful, mighty, unbent, unwavering, pressing the Soma juice — the most pleasing and most heroic, they have anointed themselves with unguents as with bright stars.

**1.87.2**  When you the Maruts came along some path in the hollows, having kindled motion like birds — the vessels drop upon your chariots; sprinkle the honey-coloured ghee on the singer.

**1.87.3**  At their onset the earth trembles violently as it were; when they yoke for beauty in their journeys — they who are playful, who shake things, whose lances gleam, who shake themselves, they by themselves make their greatness worthy of praise.

**1.87.4**  For he — the self-rushing, dapple-horsed, youthful host, lord of the unimpeded, surrounded with strengths — you are the true debt-averter, the unapproachable; you are the protector of this prayer — and so a bull-host.

**1.87.5**  We speak by the birth of the ancient father; the tongue of Soma proceeds with his sight. When the stone-pressers reached Indra with their toil — then they established the sacrificial names.

**1.87.6**  For beauty they have come together with their beams; with rays, with hymn-songs, easily pleased — they who have axes, who are eager, fearless, have found the abode of the beloved Mārutas.

**1.88.1**  Come, O Maruts, with lightning-bearing, sky-brightening chariots, bearing lances, with wings for horses; fly to us with the most exhilarating refreshment like a bird, O masters of good magic.

**1.88.2**  With their reddish-yellow horses of excellence, with tawny ones, for beauty they go with swift chariot-horses; the chariot's wheel-rim strikes the ground, brilliant as gold, like an axe.

**1.88.3**  For your beauty on your bodies the axes make the forests stand erect — for what do they hew? For you, O Maruts, well-born, of great lustre, they seek to win wealth by the stone.

**1.88.4**  The eager days have come around for you; this thought of the Vārkaras, and the divine song — making the brahman-prayer, the Gotamas with their songs have pushed upward the reservoir to drink from.

**1.88.5**  This and that has Gotama made known to you by the road, O Maruts — beholding your gold-wheeled, iron-tusked wild boars racing on.

**1.88.6**  This song of yours, O Maruts, the bearer that accompanies — the voice of the worshipping priest resounds back; it has resounded in the wake of the self-born ones' own nature, from the fist.

**1.89.1**  May auspicious thoughts come to us from all sides, undeluded, unenclosed, opening up; may the gods ever be for our increase — those who are unaging guardians day by day.

**1.89.2**  May the gracious will of the gods come to those who are upright; may the gift of the gods turn back toward us. We have attained the friendship of the gods; may the gods extend our life so that we may live.

**1.89.3**  Them we call with the ancient proclamation — Bhaga, Mitra, Aditi, Dakṣa the unfailing, Aryamā, Varuṇa, Soma, the two Aśvins; may Sarasvatī, the bounteous, make bliss for us.

**1.89.4**  May the wind blow us that healing which is blissful; may mother Earth and father Heaven give it. May the pressing-stones that press the Soma give that bliss — may you two Aśvins, the wise, hearken to that.

**1.89.5**  Him, the lord of the moving and the standing world, the stimulator of thought — we call for protection. May Pūṣan be for our increase with his possessions, our undeluded guardian and protector for our welfare.

**1.89.6**  May Indra of great fame grant us well-being; may Pūṣan the all-knowing grant us well-being; may Tārkṣya of uninjured rim grant us well-being; may Bṛhaspati grant us well-being.

**1.89.7**  The Maruts of dappled steeds, with Pṛśni as mother, going beautifully in the assemblies, those who come forth — with Agni as tongue, the Manus with the sun's eye — may all the gods come hither to us with protection.

**1.89.8**  O gods, may we hear auspicious things with our ears; O adorable ones, may we see auspicious things with our eyes; with firm limbs, with bodies, having praised — may we enjoy the life allotted by the gods.

**1.89.9**  A hundred autumns may we stand before you, O gods, during which you bring aging of our bodies to pass, during which sons become fathers — do not cut short our life in the middle of our going.

**1.89.10**  Aditi is heaven, Aditi is mid-air, Aditi is mother and father and son; all the gods are Aditi, the five peoples are Aditi; Aditi is what is born, and Aditi is what shall be born.

**1.90.1**  May Varuṇa lead us with straight guidance; may Mitra, the wise one, lead us; may Aryamā, joined in delight with the gods.

**1.90.2**  For they are lords of wealth, they who hold wealth, the undistracted ones by their greatness — they guard the ordinances every day.

**1.90.3**  May they extend shelter to us, the immortal ones to us mortals, driving away the haters.

**1.90.4**  May Indra and the Maruts clear our paths for easy going; may Pūṣan and Bhaga, the adorable ones.

**1.90.5**  Also may Pūṣan and Viṣṇu who move swiftly — make our thoughts rich in cattle, make us blessed with well-being.

**1.90.6**  Sweet blow the winds to the one who follows ṛta; sweet flow the rivers; may the plants be sweet to us.

**1.90.7**  Sweet be the night and sweet the dawn; sweet be the earthly realm; may our father Heaven be sweet.

**1.90.8**  May the lord of the forest be sweet to us, may the sun be sweet; may our cows be sweet.

**1.90.9**  Auspicious to us be Mitra, auspicious be Varuṇa, auspicious be Aryamā to us; auspicious be Indra and Bṛhaspati, and may Viṣṇu of wide strides be auspicious to us.

**1.91.1**  Thou, O Soma, art made known by wisdom; thou guidest us along the most direct path. By thy guidance, O Indu, the wise fathers obtained their share of treasure among the gods.

**1.91.2**  Thou, O Soma, hast become of excellent resolve through thy powers of will; through thy capacities, all-knowing and of surpassing skill. Thou, the bull, by thy bull-like greatness, hast become resplendent through thy glories, O thou who seest men.

**1.91.3**  Thy ordinances are even as those of King Varuṇa; great and profound is thy dwelling, O Soma. Thou art pure, beloved like Mitra; thou art as fit for worship as Aryaman, O Soma.

**1.91.4**  With all those thy forms that are in heaven, that are on earth, that are on the mountains, in the plants, in the waters—with all of these, O King Soma, graciously and without anger receive our oblations.

**1.91.5**  Thou, O Soma, art lord of the good; thou art king and also slayer of Vṛtra. Thou art the auspicious will.

**1.91.6**  And thou, O Soma, at our wish we shall not die but live. Thou art the lord of the forest, dear to those who praise thee.

**1.91.7**  Thou, O Soma, grantest great good fortune; thou grantest power to the young man devoted to the sacred law. Thou bestowest strength that he may live.

**1.91.8**  Do thou, O Soma, O King, protect us from every side against him who would harm us. A friend who has thee as his protector shall not suffer hurt.

**1.91.9**  O Soma, whatever blissful aids of thine exist for the worshipper—with all of those be thou our helper.

**1.91.10**  Come near, delighting in this sacrifice and this hymn of praise. O Soma, be thou for our increase.

**1.91.11**  We who are skilled in sacred words magnify thee, O Soma, with our songs. Enter into us with kindly grace.

**1.91.12**  O Soma, who art the sweller of homes, the dispeller of disease, the finder of wealth, the increaser of abundance—be thou a good ally to us.

**1.91.13**  O Soma, make us content in heart, as cows are content in their pastures, as a young man in his own home.

**1.91.14**  The mortal, O Soma, O god, who delights in thy friendship—him wisdom follows as the seer's attendant.

**1.91.15**  O Soma, protect us, rescue us from malediction, guard us from sin. Be to us a friend of beautiful grace.

**1.91.16**  Wax full and mighty; let thy strength as a bull gather together unto thee from all sides, O Soma. Be present at the meeting-place of abundance.

**1.91.17**  Wax full, O most exhilarating Soma, with all thy streams. Be to us a most glorious friend for our growth.
