**1.91.18**  Let thy milk-streams flow together, let thy food-prizes come together, let thy bull-powers that conquer the enemy come together, O Soma. Waxing full for immortality, fix thy highest glories in the sky.

**1.91.19**  Whatever of thy forms they worship with oblation—may all those of thee surround this sacrifice. O Soma, sweller of homes, guide onwards, rich in heroes, a man's leader; drive forward through the dwelling.

**1.91.20**  Soma gives the milk-cow, Soma gives the swift steed, Soma gives the hero fit for deeds—the sacrificial gift, the victor in assembly, worthy of the father's renown—to him who has offered to Soma.

**1.91.21**  Thee, the unconquerable in battles, the fulfiller in armies, the winner of the sun and waters, the herdsman of the settlement, born for the fight, of beautiful abode, of fair fame, the victorious—thee, O Soma, may we follow with joy.

**1.91.22**  Thou, O Soma, hast generated all these plants; thou hast generated the waters; thou, the cows. Thou hast stretched out the wide mid-air; thou by thy light hast driven away the darkness.

**1.91.23**  O divine Soma, with divine mind fight for our share of riches, O thou of a thousand powers. Let none deprive thee of lordship over thy might; discern well on both sides in the seeking of kine.

**1.92.1**  Lo, these Dawns have raised their signal; in the first half of the sky they anoint the sun. Bold, like those who polish their weapons, the ruddy cows, the mothers, advance to meet him.

**1.92.2**  The ruddy shining rays of Dawn flew freely upward; the self-yoked ruddy cows were harnessed. The Dawns have enacted their customs as of old; the ruddy ones have clung to the blazing sun.

**1.92.3**  Like craftswomen at their tasks, they gleam from afar, all yoked together with one harnessing. Bearing food to the generous patron, to the well-doing sacrificer, all days they shine for the soma-presser.

**1.92.4**  She puts on her adornments like a dancer; she uncovers her breast like a nursing mother. Creating light for all the world, Dawn, like cows streaming to their pen, has driven away the darkness.

**1.92.5**  The shining flame of her was seen facing us; she spreads forth, pushing back the monstrous darkness. Blazing her ornament in the assemblies like a beam of light, the daughter of the sky has mounted the bright sunbeam.

**1.92.6**  We have crossed to the farther shore of this darkness; Dawn as she shines makes the paths visible. Smiling for splendour like a verse of beauty, the fair-faced Dawn has aroused herself for our well-being.

**1.92.7**  Bright-shining guide of gracious words, daughter of the sky, thou art praised by the Gotamas. O Uṣas, bring near to us the prizes led by cows, whose vanguard is rich in men, whose foundation is horses.

**1.92.8**  O Dawn, may I obtain that glorious wealth, rich in fine heroes, surpassing in servants, grounded in horses. O thou of beautiful deeds, thou who shinest with fame, O fortunate one who sets the prizes in motion, grant me the great bounty.

**1.92.9**  The goddess, seeing all the worlds face to face, shines broadly with her wide-turned eye. Arousing all living things to movement, she has found the speech of all beings in both minds.

**1.92.10**  Born again and again, ancient, adorning herself with the same colour, the goddess wastes away a mortal's life-span, like a skilled dice-player scattering the pieces.

**1.92.11**  Uncovering the ends of the sky she has awakened; she drives her sister far away. She who diminishes the generations of men, the maiden shines with the glance of a lover.

**1.92.12**  Brilliant and fortunate, spreading herself out like herds of cattle, like a river's stream she has moved widely. Not violating the divine ordinances, she of the sun's rays has come forth to be seen.

**1.92.13**  O Uṣas, bring to us that brilliant bounty, O thou who art rich in prize-horses, whereby we may sustain both offspring and descendants.

**1.92.14**  O Uṣas, shine forth here today, rich in kine, rich in horses, richly shining, full of gracious gifts, for us with radiance.

**1.92.15**  For thou art rich in prizes, O Uṣas; yoke today thy ruddy horses. Then bring to us all good fortunes.

**1.92.16**  O Aśvins, bring your chariot near from us—the goal rich in cattle and gold, O wonder-working pair. Hold back your car with one mind toward us.

**1.92.17**  Ye two who have thus made the light from the sky, the signal for mankind—bring to us strength, O ye two Aśvins.

**1.92.18**  May the gods, blissful, wonder-workers, of golden path, who wake at Dawn, come hither and drive us to the drinking of Soma.

**1.93.1**  O Agni and Soma, hear well this my invocation, ye two bulls. Accept the well-spoken hymns; be a comfort to the worshipper.

**1.93.2**  O Agni and Soma, he who today serves you both with this word—grant him heroic power, increase of kine, abundance of horses.

**1.93.3**  O Agni and Soma, he who offers you an oblation, who presents you with the prepared offering—may he attain offspring, heroic power, and all-encompassing life.

**1.93.4**  O Agni and Soma, that heroic deed of yours is proclaimed whereby ye took away the cows from the niggardly Paṇi without shelter. Ye overcame the remnant of Bṛsaya; ye won one light for many.

**1.93.5**  These two, Agni and Soma, of one purpose, set these luminaries in the sky. You two released the rivers seized by reproach and disgrace, O Agni and Soma.

**1.93.6**  One Mātariśvan brought from the sky; the eagle shook the other loose from the rock. O Agni and Soma, waxing great through sacred prayer, ye made the broad world for the sacrifice.

**1.93.7**  O Agni and Soma, accept and enjoy the oblation that has been set forth; O ye bulls, be pleased with it. Be ye of beautiful shelter and good protection. Then grant to the sacrificer welfare and ease.

**1.93.8**  He who worships Agni and Soma with oblation, with god-directed mind and with ghee—protect his vow, guard him from sin, and grant great shelter to the people and the clan.

**1.93.9**  O Agni and Soma, rich in the same wealth, invoked together, favour our songs of praise. Ye two have come together in the divine realm.

**1.93.10**  O Agni and Soma, he who presents to you both this offering of ghee—for him let the great light blaze forth.

**1.93.11**  O Agni and Soma, may ye two together delight in these oblations of ours. Come near to us together.

**1.93.12**  O Agni and Soma, fill us as you would fill a horse; let the tawny morning cows, offerers of the oblation, swell. Place strength in us among the bountiful ones; make our sacrifice attentive to the call.

**1.94.1**  We have built up this hymn of praise for Jātavedas as one builds a chariot with wisdom. For his guidance is good for us in this assembly. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.2**  He for whom thou dost secure the sacrifice prospers; unchallenged he dwells, acquiring heroic power. Distress can never reach him, the oft-protected. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.3**  May we be able to kindle thee and accomplish our prayers. In thee the gods eat the oblation that has been offered. Bring the Ādityas hither, for them we desire. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.4**  We shall bring fuel and make oblations for thee, advancing through each sacred rite, being mindful. Accomplish our prayers for the prolonging of life. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.5**  As herdsman of the clans, creatures move through the nights—those that walk on two feet and those on four. Thou art the brilliant signal of the Dawn, the great one. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.6**  Thou art the adhvaryu and the ancient hotar; thou art the praśāstṛ, the potṛ, the purohita by birth. Wise one, thou dost flourish knowing all the priestly functions. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.7**  Thou who art of beautiful aspect from every side, appearing alike everywhere; though far away thou shinest through like lightning. O god, thou seest through even the darkness of night. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.8**  Let the chariot of the soma-pressers be foremost of the gods; let our praise overcome the malicious. Know this and foster our word. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.9**  With thy missiles slay the evil-speakers, the malicious—whoever the devouring ones may be, near or far. Then make easy passage for the sacrificer who sings thy praise. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.10**  When thou hast yoked the ruddy, red horses to thy chariot, wind-driven, thy roar is like that of a bull. Then thou dost fill the forests with thy smoke-bannered flame. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.11**  Then even the winged birds are frightened by thy roar, when the sparks that feed upon the plants fly apart. May that passage be easy for thy chariots. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.12**  This Agni, the wondrous one, is to be nourished for Mitra and Varuṇa; may the wrath of the Maruts pass away. May their mind be gracious to us once more. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.13**  Thou art a god of gods, the wondrous Mitra; thou art the wealth of the wealthy, beautiful at the rite. May we abide in thy shelter, most widely renowned. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.14**  That is auspicious for thee when, kindled in thy own home, fed with Soma, most merciful and ageless, thou bestowest treasure and riches on the worshipper. O Agni, may we never suffer harm in thy friendship.

**1.94.15**  He to whom thou, rich in wealth, hast given freely, him thou dost make sinless and whole, O Aditi. He whom thou dost impel with auspicious strength—may we be among those blessed with offspring and thy bounty.

**1.94.16**  So do thou, O Agni, knowing the nature of good fortune, prolong our life here, O god. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Sky grant us this.

**1.95.1**  Two beings of different form move toward the same goal, and each suckles a calf in turn. One is tawny, possessing inherent power, in one; in the other a bright one of beautiful radiance has appeared.

**1.95.2**  Ten untiring young women gave birth to this embryo of Tvaṣṭṛ, this widely carried one. They lead him around among the people, sharp-edged, self-glorious, shining radiantly.

**1.95.3**  Three races tend to him; one ocean is his, one in the sky, one in the waters. Following the former direction of earthly beings, he ordains the seasons and distributes them in due order.

**1.95.4**  Who among you has perceived this secret? The calf has generated the mothers by his own powers. The embryo of many active ones, dwelling in their lap, the great seer wanders forth by night, possessed of inherent power.

**1.95.5**  Entering in, the beautiful one grows upright on the lap of the crooked ones, of self-glory. Both consorts of Tvaṣṭṛ tremble at his birth; they who face away turn toward the lion with joy.

**1.95.6**  Both benefit from him, deeming nothing; the lowing cows have come near by his paths. He has become lord of the capable among the skilful; they anoint him with oblations on the right side.

**1.95.7**  He stretches both arms upward like Savitṛ; terrible, blazing, he strains on both sides of the channel. He drives the bright garment upward from himself; he sheds new garments for the mothers.

**1.95.8**  He makes his form most fierce when he mingles in his seat with the cows and waters. The inspired one polishes the foundation; that meeting of divine powers has come into being.

**1.95.9**  Thy wide expanse goes around the foundation, the dwelling-place of the great buffalo shining forth. O Agni, kindled with all thine own glories, guard us with thy inviolable protections.

**1.95.10**  In the desert he makes a watercourse, a way, a wave; with bright waves he approaches the earth. He holds all the prizes in his belly; within, he moves in the new birth-waters.

**1.95.11**  Thus, O Agni, waxing great by fuel, O Purifier, shine forth brilliantly for our fame. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Sky grant us this.

**1.96.1**  Born as of old with power, he straightway seized all the inspired wisdom. Accomplishing friendship with the waters and with Dhiṣaṇā, the gods sustained Agni, the giver of wealth.

**1.96.2**  By the first invocation of the two inspired sages, he generated these offspring of Manu. With the eye of Vivasvat, both the sky and the waters—the gods sustained Agni, the giver of wealth.

**1.96.3**  Him they praised as the first accomplisher of the sacrifice, offering to the races of strangers, the gliding one. The son of vigour, Bharata, of flowing liberality—the gods sustained Agni, the giver of wealth.

**1.96.4**  He who is Mātariśvan, rich in many gifts, found the path for his son, winning the sun. The herdsman of the clans, generator of heaven and earth—the gods sustained Agni, the giver of wealth.

**1.96.5**  Night and Dawn, assuming the same colour, both suckle the one child as they face each other. The golden one gleams between heaven and earth—the gods sustained Agni, the giver of wealth.

**1.96.6**  The foundation of riches, the meeting-place of goods, the banner of the sacrifice, the accomplisher of prayer and bird—those who guard immortality, the gods sustained Agni, the giver of wealth.

**1.96.7**  Now and of old the seat of riches, the earth of what is born and what is being born, the herdsman of what exists and what will be abundant—the gods sustained Agni, the giver of wealth.

**1.96.8**  The wealth-giver—may he advance the wealth of the swift; the wealth-giver of the munificent man. The wealth-giver shall grant us food rich in heroes; the wealth-giver shall bestow long life.

**1.96.9**  Thus, O Agni, waxing great by fuel, O Purifier, shine forth brilliantly for our fame. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Sky grant us this.

**1.97.1**  O Agni, burn away our sin; shine thou and bring us wealth. Burn away our sin.

**1.97.2**  We worship for good land, for easy passage, and for goods. Burn away our sin.

**1.97.3**  When the most glorious among them and our own patrons shine forth—burn away our sin.

**1.97.4**  When through thee, O Agni, thy patrons prevail, when we too prevail—burn away our sin.

**1.97.5**  When the rays of Agni the mighty go forth from everywhere—burn away our sin.

**1.97.6**  For thou art the one whose face turns to all sides, thou art the one who surrounds from every side—burn away our sin.

**1.97.7**  O thou whose face turns to all sides, carry us past our foes like a boat—burn away our sin.

**1.97.8**  May he carry us safely across like a ferry-boat over a river, to well-being—burn away our sin.

**1.98.1**  May we be in the grace of Vaiśvānara, for he is the resplendent king of all the worlds. Born from here, he surveys this whole universe; Vaiśvānara strives together with the sun.

**1.98.2**  Questioned in heaven, questioned in earth, Agni has entered into all the plants. Vaiśvānara, Agni questioned with power—may he protect us from injury by day and by night.

**1.98.3**  O Vaiśvānara, may that be true for thee; may bountiful riches attend us. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Sky grant us this.

**1.99.1**  We press Soma for Jātavedas; his knowledge burns down those who harbour ill will. May Agni carry us past all difficult places, across all evil paths, as a boat carries one across a river.

**1.100.1**  He who is the bull dwelling together with his bull-powers, the sovereign lord of the great sky and the earth, of truly heroic strength, to be invoked in battles—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.2**  Whose unimpeded course is like the sun's, whose Vṛtra-slaying fierceness exists in every battle, who is most bull-like with his own friends and companions—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.3**  Whose paths go forth like those yielding the seed of heaven, filled with power, who by his manly deeds pierces through enmities as one who has conquered—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.4**  He became most Aṅgiras-like with the Aṅgirasas, a bull with the bulls, a companion with companions; most hymn-rich with the hymn-singers, the eldest in the ways—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.5**  Skilful like the Rudras, his sons, having overcome his foes in the trial of men's strength, winning glorious deeds with his nest-companions—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.6**  He the wrathful one who brings about the conflict, who has won the sun through our heroes, who on this day is the lord of the good, much-invoked—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.7**  His aids delight in the winning of heroes in battle; the clans of settled land make him their protector. He alone is lord of all that is pitiable—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.8**  Men have longed for him of great strength at the festivals, man craving man for his aid and for wealth. Even in blind darkness he finds the light—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.9**  With his left hand he restrains even the strong; in his right hand are gathered his achievements. Even with a weak man he wins riches—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.10**  He shall win with the battle-hosts, shall win with the chariots; he is known today with all the peoples. By manly deeds he overcomes reproaches—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.11**  Whether in the assembly he meets with kindred or with non-kindred, O much-invoked, by his paths he wins offspring of the waters and descendants—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.12**  He who bears the thunderbolt, the slayer of Dasyus, terrible, fierce, of a thousand insights, of a hundredfold might, skilful—mighty as the bison of the five peoples—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.13**  His thunderbolt always roars, winning the sun; his tumult is terrible, blazing as the sky. Ancient gifts attend him, riches attend him—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.14**  He whose ceaseless praise and proclamation with power fills heaven and earth on every side—may he, exhilarated by his powers of will, bring us through—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.15**  Neither the gods and divine powers, nor mortals, nor even the waters have reached the limit of his might. He, the wandering one, with his skill fills both earth and sky—may Indra with the Maruts be for our aid.

**1.100.16**  The ruddy-and-dark dappled mare, of beautiful bite, wearing ornaments, belonging to the celestial wealth of Ṛjrāśva—she, the pleasant one, bearing a bull-led chariot in the yoke-teams, has been seen among the Nāhuṣa folk.

**1.100.17**  This is indeed that praise of thine, O bull Indra, the bounty which the Vārṣāgiras celebrate. Ṛjrāśva with yoked teams, Ambarīṣa, Sahadevas, Bhayamāna, Surādhās—these sing thee.

**1.100.18**  Having slain the Dasyus and the Śimyus by his paths, O much-invoked one, Śarva has crushed them into the earth. He of the good thunderbolt won the field with white companions, won the sun, won the waters.

**1.100.19**  May Indra be our advocate every day; unchecked, may we win the prize. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Sky grant us this.

**1.101.1**  Sing forth an invigorating, nourishing hymn of praise to him who drove out the black-bellied ones together with Ṛjiśvan; seeking his aid, we call upon the mighty one, right-handed with the thunderbolt, him accompanied by the Maruts, for friendship.

**1.101.2**  He who with exulting fury smote Vyaṁsa, who slew Śambara, who struck down lawless Pipru; Indra who turned back dry Śuṣṇa, who drains not — him, accompanied by the Maruts, we call for friendship.

**1.101.3**  Whose virile might heaven and earth proclaim as great; in whose ordinance Varuṇa moves, in whose ordinance the sun; whose ordinance the rivers, Indra's rivers, follow — him, accompanied by the Maruts, we call for friendship.

**1.101.4**  Who is the sovereign lord of horses and of cattle, and who remains firm in every deed when invoked; Indra the strong, the death-blow to the one who presses no soma — him, accompanied by the Maruts, we call for friendship.

**1.101.5**  Who is the lord of all that breathes in the world, who first found the cows for the devotee; Indra who hurled the Dasyus downward — him, accompanied by the Maruts, we call for friendship.

**1.101.6**  He who is to be invoked by the brave and by the timid, who is called upon by those who race into battle and by those who have won; Indra, upon whom all the worlds have set their hopes — him, accompanied by the Maruts, we call for friendship.

**1.101.7**  The all-seeing one goes in the quarters of the Rudras; the maiden stretches wide the expanse with the Rudras; with devotion one praises the renowned Indra — him, accompanied by the Maruts, we call for friendship.

**1.101.8**  Whether, O Indra attended by the Maruts, thou dost rejoice in the highest abode, or whether in the lower habitation — come hither to our sacrifice; for thy sake we have prepared the oblation, O true-gifted one.

**1.101.9**  For thee, O Indra of great skill, we have pressed the soma; for thy sake we have prepared the oblation, O thou who art borne by prayer; now with thy team of horses, together with the Maruts as thy company, rejoice at this sacrifice upon the sacred grass.

**1.101.10**  Rejoice, O Indra, with thy bay horses; unloose thy cheeks, release the two reins; may thy bays bring thee, O thou of lovely chin, willing — accept our oblations with pleasure.

**1.101.11**  May we, guarded by the praise of the Maruts, win the prize of strength together with Indra. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that boon.

**1.102.1**  I offer unto thee this great and mighty prayer — the intelligence attaches itself to the praise of this great one; him unconquered in contest and in battle, Indra, the gods exulted in his power one after another.

**1.102.2**  The seven rivers carry his glory; Heaven and Earth bear his fair form in their lap; in us the sun and moon, for their illumination, travel back and forth trustingly — grant us this, O Indra.

**1.102.3**  Urge forward that victorious chariot of thine, O Maghavan, to win the spoil — the chariot we have praised for thee in the encounter; drive it forward with thy mind, O Indra praised by many; grant shelter to those who are devoted to thee, O Maghavan.

**1.102.4**  May we conquer, with thee as our ally, the prize that is ours; lift up our share in battle after battle; make wide and easy room for us, O Indra; shatter the virile strength of our enemies, O Maghavan.

**1.102.5**  For various clans call on thee, O upholder of wealth, with their praises, seeking thy aid; mount our chariot for the winning of the prize, for thy mind, O Indra, is set on victory.

**1.102.6**  His arms win cattle; boundless in counsel, unsurpassed, the hundredfold helper in every deed, the hero — Indra equalled none with his strength; and so the peoples who desire to conquer call upon him from every side.

**1.102.7**  Thy glory among peoples rises above the hundred, above even more, surpasses a thousand; the intelligence intensifies thee, measureless and mighty; even so thou slayest the Vṛtras, O Puraṁdara, fort-destroyer.

**1.102.8**  The measure of thy might has a triple-threaded element; three are the earths, O lord of men, and three the bright realms; thou hast surpassed all this universe; without enemy from birth, thou art ever, O Indra.

**1.102.9**  Thee we invoke first among the gods; thou hast been the unconquered one in battles; may Indra drive this eager, most devoted, bursting-forth one's chariot of ours to the fore at the start of the contest.

**1.102.10**  Thou hast conquered; thou hast not shut off the spoils; in small battles and in great, O Maghavan — we sharpen thee, the fierce one, for our protection; now, O Indra, impel us forward in our invocations.

**1.102.11**  May Indra be our advocate always; undeviated, may we win the prize. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that boon.

**1.103.1**  That highest power of thine, the seers of old established pointing outward; one part of it is here on earth, another in the sky — together they meet like a single standard of an army.

**1.103.2**  He sustained the earth and spread it wide; having smitten with the thunderbolt, he released the waters; he slew the serpent, he cleft Rauhinī apart; Maghavan cut down Vyaṁsa with his mighty deeds.

**1.103.3**  He, the strong-natured, placing faith in his might, went about shattering the fortresses, distributing among the Dāsīs; knowing, O Vajrin, hurl thy weapon at the Dasyu; increase the Aryan's dominion and glory, O Indra.

**1.103.4**  That Maghavan, bearing a name worthy of praise, spoke to the ancient one for these ages of men; when the Vajrin advanced to slay the Dasyus and the son established a name for glory.

**1.103.5**  Behold this abundance, this great prosperity of his — place your faith in the heroic deeds of Indra; he found the cows, he found the horses, he found the herbs, he found the waters, he found the forests.

**1.103.6**  We press soma for the abundant-actioned bull, the vigorous one, whose strength is true; he who, like a hero lying in wait on the road, goes distributing spoil among those who do not sacrifice.

**1.103.7**  Thou didst accomplish that heroic deed surpassing what had been done before, when thou didst rouse the lying serpent with the thunderbolt; the consorts and the birds rejoiced after thee, all the gods exulted after thee.

**1.103.8**  Śuṣṇa, Pipru, Kuyava, and Vṛtra thou didst slay, O Indra, and didst shatter the fortresses of Śambara; may Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that boon.

**1.104.1**  A seat has been made for thee, O Indra, in which to sit; sit down therein like a mighty man, not like a steed; let loose the birds and unyoke the horses at the approach of evening and dawn, thou who art fit to carry us forward.

**1.104.2**  Lo, those men have approached Indra for aid; may he reach them straightway even now on the path; the gods, having crushed down the wrath of the Dāsa, may they bring our tribe to well-being.

**1.104.3**  The all-knowing one draws by himself the wave downward, by himself draws down the foaming water; bathed in milk — may the two consorts of Kuyava, once he is slain, lie on the slope of the ravine.

**1.104.4**  The navel of the higher one's offspring has been severed; the hero surpasses with his ancient powers in ruling the land; the two gracious, axe-wielding wives of the hero, urging milk forward, bear it with the waters.

**1.104.5**  When that dwelling of the Dasyu was seen in the way, it did not go to the abode knowing its direction; therefore act for us, O Maghavan; do not abandon us as the generous man abandons the ungifted supplicant.

**1.104.6**  Be thou for us, O Indra, blameless in the sun and in the waters; grant us a share in the praise of the living; do not cut short our portion in the middle; this we entrust in faith to thy great might.

**1.104.7**  Now I believe faith has been placed in thee by us; O bull, urge us forward to great wealth; do not give our food-supply, O Indra much-invoked, to the hungry in an unworked condition.

**1.104.8**  Slay us not, O Indra, give us not away; steal not from us our dear enjoyments; shatter not our eggs, O Maghavan, O mighty one; break not the vessels that were born together with us.

**1.104.9**  Come hither — they call thee the lover of soma; this soma is pressed, drink of it for exhilaration; fill thy wide belly as a bull; hear us as we call thee, as a father hears.

**1.105.1**  The moon runs in the waters, the eagle soars in the sky; the lightning-bolts with golden rims find not your track — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.2**  Only for a purpose does the wife draw her husband to her — the one who is in need; they press out the manly milk; having set aside the essence, they milk it — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.3**  May not, O gods, yonder sky fall down from heaven; may we never be in the void of the soma-yielding, the auspicious one — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.4**  I ask the lowest sacrifice; that messenger will proclaim it: where has the primordial order gone, who now carries it onward? — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.5**  Ye gods who stand in the three bright regions of the sky — what is right and what is wrong among you? Where is your ancient oblation? — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.6**  What is the foundation of ṛta? What is the vision of Varuṇa? How shall we traverse the great path of Aryaman, we the perplexed? — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.7**  I am he who in an earlier time spoke certain things at the pressing of soma; afflictions assail me — like a wolf assailing a thirsty animal — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.8**  The ribs press against me on both sides like rival wives; the middle ones gnaw through like mice gnawing at sinews — they consume thy praiser, O Śatakratu of the hundred powers — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.9**  Those seven rays — my navel-cord is stretched out to them; Trita Āptya knows that and sings it to make kin — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.10**  Those five bulls who stand in the midst of the great heaven — those that are worthy to be proclaimed among the gods have turned back all together — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.11**  The eagles sit in the intermediate region of the ascent of the sky; they ward off from the paths the wolf who crosses over the swiftly flowing waters — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.12**  That new recitable hymn, set forth for the gods, easy to proclaim: the rivers flow with ṛta, the sun has spread truth — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.13**  That recitable thing of thine, O Agni, belongs to the gods as their treasure; do thou, seated there more wisely than Manu, worship the gods for us — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.14**  The Hotṛ-priest seated there, wiser than Manu, faces the gods; Agni, the wise god among gods, well sets the oblations moving — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.15**  Brahmā Varuṇa makes the path-knowing one; we implore him; he uncovers with the heart the thought — may the new ṛta be born — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.16**  Yonder path of the sun set in the sky to be proclaimed — O gods, that one none transgresses; O mortals, ye do not see it — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.17**  Trita, cast down into the well, calls upon the gods for aid; Bṛhaspati heard this and made wide space from the narrow confine — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.18**  The tawny wolf once saw me as I was going along the path; the one who must be kept hidden rises up, sick in the spine like a carpenter's wife — understand this for me, O heaven and earth.

**1.105.19**  With this hymn of praise, attended by Indra, may we overcome in the settlement, full of all heroes; may Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that boon.

**1.106.1**  We invoke Indra, Mitra, Varuṇa, Agni for our aid, the host of the Maruts, and Aditi. O Vasus, bounteous givers, like a chariot from a difficult pass, deliver us from every distress.

**1.106.2**  Come hither, O Ādityas, for our complete welfare; be gracious, O gods, in the conquests of Vṛtra. O Vasus, bounteous givers, like a chariot from a difficult pass, deliver us from every distress.

**1.106.3**  May the Fathers, easy to invoke with fair laudations, help us; and the two divine goddesses, daughters of the gods, who promote ṛta. O Vasus, bounteous givers, like a chariot from a difficult pass, deliver us from every distress.

**1.106.4**  We call hither with hymns of praise Narāśaṁsa the mighty, the lord of steeds who quickens prizes, Pūṣan, ruler of men. O Vasus, bounteous givers, like a chariot from a difficult pass, deliver us from every distress.

**1.106.5**  O Bṛhaspati, make our path ever easy; we seek that blessing and prosperity which Manu established for you. O Vasus, bounteous givers, like a chariot from a difficult pass, deliver us from every distress.

**1.106.6**  The sage Kutsa, hemmed in a ravine, called Indra, the Vṛtra-slayer, lord of might, for his aid. O Vasus, bounteous givers, like a chariot from a difficult pass, deliver us from every distress.

**1.106.7**  May divine Aditi with the gods protect us; may the divine Protector guard us without slackening. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.107.1**  The sacrifice returns gracious favour from the gods; O Ādityas, be merciful to us. May your benevolent thought be turned hither toward us — that which is most spacious even amid distress.

**1.107.2**  May the gods come to us with aid, praised with the hymns of the Aṅgirasas. May Indra with his powers, the Maruts with their troop, and Aditi with the Ādityas bestow shelter on us.

**1.107.3**  May Indra, Varuṇa, Agni, Aryaman, and Savitṛ bestow that delight upon us. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.108.1**  That most brilliant chariot of yours, O Indrāgnī, which surveys all the worlds — come riding together in that very chariot, and drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.2**  As vast as all this world extends, wide-spreading and deep in its expanse — let this Soma be ready for you both to drink, O Indrāgnī, fitting for your minds.

**1.108.3**  For you have established the auspicious name Sadhrīya; you are both Sadhrīcīna and Vṛtra-slayers. Sit together, O Indrāgnī, and drink deep of the bull Soma, you mighty ones.

**1.108.4**  When the fires are kindled and the ladles are held ready, and the sacred grass is spread — come hither, O Indrāgnī, with the poured, potent Soma offerings, for our well-being.

**1.108.5**  The heroic deeds and the forms and the manly powers that you two, O Indrāgnī, have performed, and those ancient friendships that are auspicious between you — with those qualities, drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.6**  I first declared my desire for you both: this Soma of ours calls you here, not the Asuras. Come hither to that true faith, and then drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.7**  Whether you two, O Indrāgnī, are revelling in your own house, or among the brahman, or in the royal hall, O adorable ones — come hither from there, you mighty ones, and drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.8**  Whether you two, O Indrāgnī, are among the Yadus and Turvaśas, or among the Druhyus, Anus, and Pūrus — come hither from there, you mighty ones, and drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.9**  Whether you two, O Indrāgnī, are on the lowest earth, the middle, or the highest — come hither from there, you mighty ones, and drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.10**  Whether you two, O Indrāgnī, are on the highest earth, the middle, or the lowest — come hither from there, you mighty ones, and drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.11**  Whether you two, O Indrāgnī, are in heaven, on earth, in the mountains, among the plants, or in the waters — come hither from there, you mighty ones, and drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.12**  Whether you two, O Indrāgnī, take delight of your own accord at the rising of the sun, in the midst of heaven — come hither from there, you mighty ones, and drink of the pressed Soma.

**1.108.13**  Even so, O Indrāgnī, having drunk of the pressed Soma, win all riches for us. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.109.1**  I have looked about with the mind seeking better things — O Indrāgnī, I would know you, or else the kinsmen. No other guardian is mine but you; I have fashioned for you both this thought that wins the prize.

**1.109.2**  I have heard that you both are more lavishly giving than a maternal uncle or a brother-in-law. And so, as the Soma is offered to you both, O Indrāgnī, I create a fresh hymn of praise.

**1.109.3**  Imploring, let us not sever the cords nor withdraw the ancestral powers. What mighty ones rejoice in both Indra and Agni? — truly the two pressing stones in the lap of Dhiṣaṇā.

**1.109.4**  For your delight, O Indrāgnī, the divine Dhiṣaṇā, full of eagerness, presses the Soma. Come, you with auspicious hands and beautiful fingers like the Aśvins — hasten and mingle it with honey in the waters.

**1.109.5**  You two, O Indrāgnī, most mighty ones, are heard of in the division of riches and in the slaying of Vṛtra. Seat yourselves on the sacred grass at this sacrifice and, O ye champions of men, exhilarate yourselves with the pressed Soma.

**1.109.6**  You two excel all men in the contests of battle; you exceed both earth and heaven; you surpass the rivers and mountains in greatness. O Indrāgnī, you two surpass all other beings.

**1.109.7**  Bring your gifts, O you wielders of the thunderbolt; protect us, O Indrāgnī, with your powers. These rays of the sun by which our fathers had communion with you — may they be ours.

**1.109.8**  O breakers of forts with thunderbolt in hand, protect us, O Indrāgnī, in battles. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.110.1**  My work is spread out, and it spreads anew; the sweetest thought is proclaimed for praise. This ocean here belongs to all the gods — O Ṛbhus, be satisfied with what has been hallowed with svāhā.

**1.110.2**  When you went forth seeking enjoyment, O you who came from afar, proceeding eastward, O Saudhanvanā Ṛbhus — having traversed all the regions of your journey, come to the home of the generous Savitṛ.

**1.110.3**  Savitṛ has impelled for you that immortality which, when you went forth, was proclaimed as indestructible. That single cup used by the Asura for drinking you made into four.

**1.110.4**  Having peacefully settled through their skill of swift crossing, these mortals, being mortal, attained to immortality. The Saudhanvanā Ṛbhus, bright-eyed as the sun, were united throughout the year with their devotional works.

**1.110.5**  Like a field they measured out the wobbling single cup with precision, O Ṛbhus. Praised and most worthy of praise, imploring, they sought renown among the immortals.

**1.110.6**  Let us pour thought to the men of the mid-air like ghee poured into the ladle, with knowledge. The Ṛbhus, who by their swift skill followed their father, mounted to the realm of heaven for the prize.

**1.110.7**  Indra is like an Ṛbhu, ever renewed in strength; the Ṛbhu gives wealth with prizes and good things. By your aid, O gods, on that beloved day of victory, may we prevail over the armies of those who press no Soma.

**1.110.8**  The Ṛbhus fashioned a cow from the hide; they reunited the calf with its mother. O Saudhanvanā Ṛbhus, by your wonderful skill, you, O men, made your two parents — decrepit and aged — young again.

**1.110.9**  Help us, O Indra, rich in prizes, to win wealth in the contest of prizes; show forth your wondrous bounty, O Ṛbhavan. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.111.1**  The skilful ones fashioned a chariot with good wheels; they fashioned the two bay horses, bearers of Indra, rich in might. The Ṛbhus fashioned youthful vigour for the parents; they fashioned the cow who stays with her calf.

**1.111.2**  Fashion for our sacrifice a Ṛbhu-like vigour, for wisdom and skill, a food-supply with fine offspring, so that we may dwell with all our people in heroic strength. Bestow on us complete Indra-power.

**1.111.3**  Fashion a prize for us, O Ṛbhus; a prize for the chariot, a prize for the steed, O men. Magnify our prize that ever conquers, kin or unkin, victorious in battles.

**1.111.4**  I call Indra-Ṛbhukṣan for aid, and the Ṛbhus and Vājas, and the Maruts, for the drinking of Soma. May both Mitra-Varuṇa and now the Aśvins spur us on to win the prize, for thought and for victory.

**1.111.5**  May Ṛbhu sharpen the prize for the contest; may Vāja, the battle-winner, assist us. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.112.1**  I invoke Heaven and Earth for prior wisdom, and Agni the warmth, the bright one, on our journey for worship. With those aids by which you quicken the pressing of the prize-portion, O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.2**  The never-failing, easy-carrying ones mounted the chariot for your gift, as if to reach a spoken word. With those aids by which you protect devotions for the worship-rite, O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.3**  You two rule over those celestial beings under heaven's governance, by virtue of the great immortal realm. With those aids by which you swell the milk-cow and the horse, O men, O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.4**  With those aids by which the All-surrounding One glorifies the full greatness of his son who has two mothers, swift and rushing; with those by which Trimanta became discerning — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.5**  With those aids by which you raised up Rebha, sunken and buried under the waters, to see the light of day; with those aids by which you helped Kaṇva who was striving forward — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.6**  With those aids by which you sustained Antaka who was dying in a distant place, and Bhujyu without wavering; with those by which you quickened Karkandhu and Vayya — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.7**  With those aids by which you helped Śucanti, the prize-seeker, who sits securely, and by which the burning warmth came with shelter for Atri; with those by which you aided Pṛśnigu and Purukutsa — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.8**  With those powerful aids, O mighty ones, by which you enabled the blind and lame to go away and see; with those by which you freed the quail that had been swallowed — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.9**  With those aids by which, never-failing, you quickened the honeyed Sindhū and Vasiṣṭha, you two undecaying ones; with those by which you aided Kutsa, the glorious and manly one — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.10**  With those aids by which, with a thousand rewards, you quickened Viśpalā the prize-seeker, the Atharvan, in the battle; with those by which you aided Vaśa, the horse-owner Preṇi — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.11**  With those aids by which, for the merchant Auśija Dīrghaśravas, the honey-vessel overflowed, O bounteous ones; with those by which you aided Kakṣīvant the praiser — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.12**  With those aids by which you filled the Rasā, whose waters are sweet as milk; with those by which you helped the horseless chariot to victory; with those by which Triśoka drove forth the dawn-cows — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.13**  With those aids by which you go around the sun in the far distance; with those by which you aided Mandhātṛ in the lordship of his field; with those by which you aided the inspired Bharadvāja — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.14**  With those aids by which you helped the great Atithigva Kaśojuva, Divodāsa, in the slaying of Śambara; with those by which you aided Trasadasyu in the breaking of forts — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.15**  With those aids by which you honour Vaṁra, sorely afflicted and already praised; and the blind Kali who has lost his wealth; with those by which you aided Vyaśva and Pṛthi — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.16**  With those aids by which, O men, you helped Śaya and Atri, and formerly showed the way to Manu; with those by which you drove the chariot of Śārī with Syūmaraśmi — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.17**  With those aids by which — through the greatness of Jaṭhara's belly — the fire blazed forth clearly, kindled on the march; with those by which you aided Śaryāta in a great prize contest — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.18**  With those aids by which you freed Aṅgiras by mind, going to the forefront in the opening of the cow-pen; with those by which you aided the heroic Manu with food in battle — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.19**  With those aids by which you brought brides to Vimada; and those by which you indeed gave instruction to the Aruṇīs; with those by which you carried pious Sudāsa to divine bliss — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.20**  With those aids by which you become beneficent for the worshipper, and aided Bhujyu; and those by which you aided Adhrigu, the bearer of shelter and abundance, who praises ṛta — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.21**  With those aids by which you honour Kṛśānu in the contest for the prize; with those by which you aided the young man's swift horse; and with those by which you bring sweet honey for the bees — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.22**  With those aids by which you quicken the man who fights for cows, in the battle of heroes, for the winning of a field and a son; with those by which you aid chariots and horses — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.23**  With those aids, O you of a hundred powers, by which you aided Kutsa the son of Arjuna, and Turvīti and Dabhīti; with those by which you aided Dhvasanti and Puruṣanti — O Aśvins, come with those aids.

**1.112.24**  Grant us, O Aśvins, a speech bearing good results; fashion our prayer, O wonderful ones, O mighty ones. I call upon you today for aid and to increase us; be with us for the winning of prizes.

**1.112.25**  Guard us by day and by night with auspicious, bountiful protections, O Aśvins. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.113.1**  This best light among lights has come; the bright discernment was born far-shining. As prompted by Savitṛ for his sending forth, even so Night has yielded her station to Dawn.

**1.113.2**  She has come, the bright one with her bright offspring, having put to flight the Dark One from her dwelling-places. Immortal sisters, moving together in turn, Heaven and Dawn exchange their colours at the dawn.

**1.113.3**  The common path of the two sisters is endless; they walk it, each in turn, taught by the gods. They do not collide, nor do they stand still; harmonious though different in form, Night and Dawn move together in one mind.

**1.113.4**  The bright leader of glad tidings has appeared; the brilliant one has opened the doors for us. Setting the world in motion, she has revealed riches to us — Dawn has awakened all the worlds.

**1.113.5**  O bountiful one, you cause movement for those who lie crooked, and stir to seek and enjoy riches. You show your broad radiance for those who see little — Dawn has awakened all the worlds.

**1.113.6**  For sovereignty are you, for glory you are great; for worship and as the very goal of effort do you come. You reveal the varied spectacle of living beings — Dawn has awakened all the worlds.

**1.113.7**  She has appeared, the daughter of heaven, the young maiden, shining forth in bright garments. Mistress of all earthly wealth — O Uṣas, shine forth here today, O auspicious one.

**1.113.8**  She follows the path of those that have gone before, and is the first among the countless ones yet to come. She, the Dawn, shining forth, stirs the living to action; she wakens no one who is dead.

**1.113.9**  O Uṣas, when you kindled the fire, when you unveiled with the eye of the sun, when you stirred men who would perform worship — thereby you have wrought auspicious gain among the gods.

**1.113.10**  How many Dawns have already passed? And how many that are now shining? She longs after the earlier Dawns, calling eagerly; glowing she goes harmoniously with the others still to come.

**1.113.11**  Those mortals have gone who saw the Dawn shining in the earlier time. Now she has come to be seen by us; those will come also who will see her in future times.

**1.113.12**  You who drive away hatred, who guard ṛta, who are born of ṛta, who confer bliss and send forth kind words, who bring the most auspicious gifts, who carry the divine feast — O Uṣas, shine forth here today as the most excellent.

**1.113.13**  Ever before has the divine Uṣas shone forth; and today again, she the bountiful one has shone. And she will yet shine forth for more days to come — she, immortal, ageless, moves by her own powers.

**1.113.14**  Lighting up the approaches of heaven with her rays, the divine one has driven away the black shroud. Awakening all, Dawn goes forth with her reddish horses, in her well-harnessed chariot.

**1.113.15**  Bringing nourishing and desirable gifts, the knowing one makes a wondrous banner. The foremost of those that have passed, among the countless countless, among those that shine — Dawn has dawned first.

**1.113.16**  Rise up — the living breath has come to us; the darkness has departed; light is coming. She has opened the path for the sun to travel; we have arrived where life is prolonged.

**1.113.17**  The singer, the bearer of the hymn, raises his voice with the stringing of words, praising the shining Dawns. Shine forth today that, O bountiful one; shine your offspring-bearing light of life upon us.

**1.113.18**  Those Dawns rich in cattle and in all heroes that shine forth for the mortal worshipper — as a surge of glad tidings like the wind — may he who presses Soma obtain those givers of horses.

**1.113.19**  Aditi, mother of the gods, the banner of sacrifice, shine forth in your greatness. O you who confer renown, shine forth for the brahman among us; create offspring for our people, O you who hold all that is desirable.

**1.113.20**  That wondrous good fortune which Dawn brings for the man who knows and strives — may Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.114.1**  We offer these hymns to powerful Rudra of the braided hair, the ruler of heroes, so that all may be well with our bipeds and quadrupeds, so that all in this village may prosper and be free from disease.

**1.114.2**  Be gracious to us, O Rudra, and bring us welfare; we would honour you with worship, O ruler of heroes. Whatever health and happiness Manu, our father, won through sacrifice — may we attain that under your guidance, O Rudra.

**1.114.3**  May we attain your grace, O Rudra the shower of blessings, ruler of heroes, through worship of the deity. Come graciously to our people; with our heroes unhurt may we pour oblation to you.

**1.114.4**  We call hither for aid the impetuous Rudra, accomplisher of the sacrifice, the agile seer. Let his divine wrath be far from us; we choose only his benevolence.

**1.114.5**  The red boar of heaven, of braided hair, of fearful form — we call upon with worship. May he, holding in his hand desirable healing herbs, bestow shelter, armour, and refuge upon us.

**1.114.6**  This word is spoken as praise and increase for Rudra, father of the Maruts, sweeter than the sweet. Grant to us, O immortal, what mortals may enjoy; be gracious to us, to our own selves, and to our children and their children.

**1.114.7**  Slay not our great one, nor our little one, not our growing youth, nor our grown man; slay not our father, nor our mother; harm not, O Rudra, any of those dear to us.

**1.114.8**  Harm not our children or our children's children, nor our life, not our cows, not our horses; slay not our heroes in anger, O Rudra; we who bear oblations call upon you ever.

**1.114.9**  I have brought you praises as a herdsman brings his cattle; grant us your favour, O father of the Maruts. For your benevolence is auspicious and most merciful — therefore we choose only your grace.

**1.114.10**  Far from us be your cow-slaying and man-slaying, O ruler of heroes; let your favour be with us. Be gracious to us and speak well of us, O God, and give us doubly-woven shelter.

**1.114.11**  We who desire aid have spoken reverence to him; may Rudra with his Maruts hear our call. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.115.1**  The wondrous face of the gods has arisen — the eye of Mitra, Varuṇa, and Agni. He has filled heaven, earth, and the mid-air; the Sun is the soul of all that moves and stands.

**1.115.2**  The Sun approaches the shining divine Uṣas from behind, as a young man follows a woman. Where men, devout in their worship, spread out the ages, setting the auspicious against the auspicious.

**1.115.3**  Auspicious are the golden horses of the Sun, wondrous and diverse, worthy of praise. Reverently they have mounted to the back of heaven; they circle heaven and earth in a single day.

**1.115.4**  This is the divinity and this the greatness of the Sun — that in the midst of his course he drew in the stretched expanse. When he has unharnessed his golden horses from their station, at once Night draws her garment over all.

**1.115.5**  For Mitra and Varuṇa to behold, the Sun manifests his form in heaven's lap. One of his bright rays extends without end; the golden horses carry away the other, the dark one.

**1.115.6**  Today, O gods, at the rising of the Sun, deliver us from sin and free us from reproach. May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, Sindhu, Earth, and Heaven grant us that blessing.

**1.116.1**  I press out hymns to the two Nāsatyas as one strews the sacred grass, as wind drives the cloud. You two who carried the bride to young Vimada on your chariot swift as the army's vanguard.

**1.116.2**  Whether by swift-rushing, firm-flying courses, or by the impulse of the gods, ever-triumphant — the ass won that thousand for you, O Nāsatyas, in the contest at the race of Yama.

**1.116.3**  Tugra had dispatched Bhujyu into the cloud-ocean, as one sends away one who might die — without finding riches. You two carried him back in self-moving ships that filled the air, that did not founder in water.

**1.116.4**  For three nights and three days, O Nāsatyas, you carried Bhujyu in flying boats, over the far shore of the watery desert sea, with three hundred-footed, six-horsed chariots.

**1.116.5**  You displayed your heroism in that ocean without footing, without resting place, without holding — when, O Aśvins, you carried Bhujyu home in a ship with a hundred oars.

**1.116.6**  The white horse which you two gave to Pedu, the son of Agha — ever for his welfare — that gift of yours is great and praiseworthy. The steed of Pedu, the victor, is always to be invoked by the noble.

**1.116.7**  For Kakṣīvant the Pajriya who praises you, O men, you both gave forth wisdom. From the hoof of the victorious horse you poured a hundred jars of liquor.

**1.116.8**  You two shielded Agni from the cold heat of frost and bestowed on him food with nourishment. You rescued Atri from the pit, O Aśvins, and raised him up with all his people in safety.

**1.116.9**  You two pursued the far-off Nāsatyas; you made the watering-trough with its high bottom and slanting edge. Like flowing waters, you poured forth a thousand gifts of cattle for Gotama who thirsted.

**1.116.10**  O Nāsatyas, you stripped from the ageing Cyavāna his worn-out garment as one removes a robe, and extended his life that had been abandoned. You marvellous ones made him the husband of maidens.

**1.116.11**  That, O Nāsatyas, was a praiseworthy and successful act of protection that you performed — when you, with full knowledge, like one unearthing a hidden treasure, raised Vandana up to a sight of beauty.

**1.116.12**  That wonderful deed of yours, O men, for the winning of prizes, I make manifest like thunder that brings rain. When Dadhyaṅc Ātharvaṇa told you about the Soma nectar through the head of a horse.

**1.116.13**  The bountiful, wise Vadhrīmatī called to you, O Nāsatyas, on a great journey, O much-favouring ones. You heard — as at the bidding of a teacher — and gave her a golden-handed son, O Aśvins.

**1.116.14**  You two, O men, O Nāsatyas, freed the quail from the very jaws of the wolf in the encounter. And you made the seer who was weeping, O much-favouring ones, able to see again.

**1.116.15**  For the wing of a bird, like a leaf, was cut off in the battle at the time of Khelā's deadly contest. Instantly you fitted Viśpalā with an iron leg, so she could run when the prize was set.

**1.116.16**  His father blinded Ṛjrāśva who had given a hundred rams to a she-wolf. To him, O Nāsatyas, O wondrous healers, you gave eyes to see clearly, you who are unharmed.

**1.116.17**  The daughter of the Sun mounted your chariot conquering, like a cloth spread over it. All the gods agreed with their hearts; and you, O Nāsatyas, are joined together with beauty.

**1.116.18**  When you two came bringing aid to Divodāsa and Bharadvāja, O swift-going Aśvins — your friendly chariot bore much wealth, with a bull and a dolphin yoked to it.

**1.116.19**  Bearing wealth, good lordship, long life, and heroic might, O Nāsatyas — you came to the Jahnāvī river with prizes, as she who receives her share three times daily, with harmonious mind.

**1.116.20**  You carried Jāhūṣa, hemmed in on all sides, through the night by easy passages across the dust-clouds. With the Vibhindhu chariot, O ever-youthful Nāsatyas, you cleft the mountains as you went.

**1.116.21**  In a single day, O Aśvins, you came to help Vaśa win a thousand in the prize contest. You two mighty ones, allied with Indra, drove away misfortune from the far-famed Pṛthuśravas.

**1.116.22**  From the stalk of the reed Ārcat you two drew water upward from below to drink. For Śaya who was wasting away, O Nāsatyas, you made the barren cow give milk with your powers.

**1.116.23**  For Kṛṣṇiya who seeks protection, who praises, who prays sincerely, O Nāsatyas, with your powers — like a lost animal found again — you gave Viṣṇāpū to Viśvaka for his sight.

**1.116.24**  For ten nights and nine days, bound and battered, submerged within the waters, you raised up Rebha, scattered and buried in the water, as one draws out Soma through a strainer.

**1.116.25**  I have declared your wondrous deeds, O Aśvins; may I be the master of good cattle and good heroes. Seeing and enjoying long life, may I reach old age like a home to be reached.

**1.117.1**  The ancient Hotṛ seeks to win you two, O Aśvins, for the joy of the sweet Soma. The gift is rich in sacred grass and the hymn is released — come, O Nāsatyas, with prizes and food.

**1.117.2**  That chariot of yours, O Aśvins, swifter than mind, with good horses, that comes to the clans — with which you go to the house of the pious worshipper — come to us on that path, O men.

**1.117.3**  You two, O men, freed the seer Atri who belongs to five peoples from the pit of distress. Destroying the wiles of the wicked Dasyu, you mighty ones urged him on in proper order.

**1.117.4**  Like a horse hidden in a bad place, O Aśvins, O mighty men, the seer Rebha was buried in the waters; you released him, scattered as he was, with your wonders. Your ancient deeds do not grow old.

**1.117.5**  Like one deeply sleeping in the lap of destruction, like the sun dwelling in darkness, O wondrous ones — like a gleaming ornament buried and hidden for adornment — you raised up Vandana, O Aśvins.

**1.117.6**  That, O Nāsatyas, O all-surrounding ones, is your praiseworthy deed — with Kakṣīvant the Pajriya — when from the hoof of the victorious horse you poured for the people a hundred jars of honey.

**1.117.7**  You two, O men, gave Viṣṇāpu to Viśvaka who was praising you for Kṛṣṇiya. Even for Ghoṣā sitting in her father's house, to her who was growing old, O Aśvins, you gave a husband.

**1.117.8**  You gave the bright one to dark Śyāva, O Aśvins, and the great earthy one to Kaṇva. That deed of yours, O mighty ones, is worthy to be proclaimed — how you bestowed fame upon Nārṣada.

**1.117.9**  Assuming many forms, O Aśvins, you brought the swift horse to Pedu — the prize-winner of a thousand, unvanquished, the serpent-slayer, glorious, the conqueror.

**1.117.10**  These deeds of yours, O bounteous ones, are the praise-worthy abode of hymns for both worlds. When the Pajriya people call upon you two, O Aśvins — come with food and prizes to the knower.

**1.117.11**  Praised with the prayer of a son, O Aśvins, you who sustain the inspired seer and grant him the prize — swelling with the prayer of Agastya, O Nāsatyas, you restored Viśpalā.

**1.117.12**  Going where to receive the excellent praise of the poet, O sons of heaven, O mighty ones, O youthful ones? Like a golden pot buried underground you raised him up — on the tenth day, O Aśvins.

**1.117.13**  You two, O Aśvins, made the ageing Cyavāna young again with your powers. The daughter of the Sun chose your chariot, O Nāsatyas, together with its beauty.

**1.117.14**  You two, with your ancient ways, became like new-spirited youths for Tugra again. You carried Bhujyu out of the ocean with your swift-flying, straight-dashing horses.

**1.117.15**  The son of Tugra called to you two, O Aśvins, as he was cast into the ocean, unshaken though he had gone in. You carried him out in your well-yoked chariot, swift as thought, O mighty ones, in safety.

**1.117.16**  The quail called out to you two, O Aśvins, when you freed her from the very mouth of the wolf. You went victoriously over the mountain ridge and struck down the newborn Viṣvāc with poison.

**1.117.17**  Ṛjrāśva, who had given a hundred rams to the she-wolf, was led into darkness by his wicked father. You two, O Aśvins, placed eyes in Ṛjrāśva; you made light for the blind so he could see clearly.

**1.117.18**  She-wolf called out to you, O mighty Aśvins, O men, carrying welfare for the blind: 'A lover like a youth, giving freely, Ṛjrāśva gave a hundred and one rams.'

**1.117.19**  Great and bringing joy is your aid, O Aśvins; O wise ones, you heal even the paralysed. And so the wise one called upon you both; come to her, O mighty ones, with your aids.

**1.117.20**  You caused the barren, dry, infertile cow to give milk for Śaya, O wondrous Aśvins. With your powers you carried the woman of Purumitra as wife to Vimada.

**1.117.21**  Sowing barley with the plough, milking food for mankind, O wondrous Aśvins — blowing the Dasyu away with a pipe, you made broad light for the Ārya.

**1.117.22**  You set on Dadhīca Ātharvaṇa an equine head, O Aśvins. Walking in truth, he told you the sweet nectar — that Tvāṣṭra mystery which is your secret, O wondrous ones.

**1.117.23**  I always, as a sage, have sought your kindness; O Aśvins, favour all my prayers. Bestow on us, O Nāsatyas, great wealth accompanied by offspring and worthy of being heard of.

**1.117.24**  You two, O men, O bounteous Aśvins, delighting, gave a golden-handed son to Vadhrīmatī. You set Śyāva who was split in three parts upright and alive again.

**1.117.25**  These heroic deeds of yours, O Aśvins, the people have proclaimed, the ancient deeds. O mighty ones, forming the prayer for you both, may we, with good heroes, speak our wisdom at the assembly.

**1.118.1**  Let your chariot come to us, O Aśvins, that flies like a hawk, that is gracious, that carries good things — that chariot which is swifter than a mortal's thought, three-seated, O mighty ones, that races like the wind.

**1.118.2**  Come to us in the three-seated, triple, three-wheeled chariot, rolling well. Swell our cows, quicken our horses, O Aśvins, and increase the hero among us.

**1.118.3**  Moving forward with your well-rolling chariot, O wondrous ones, hear this praise from the pressing stone. What offering, O Aśvins, do the inspired sages of old name as most fitting to win you back?

**1.118.4**  May your hawks carry you, O Aśvins — swift flying ones harnessed to your chariot — those that are airborne like heavenly vultures, that carry food to you, O Nāsatyas.

**1.118.5**  A young woman mounts your chariot here, having been welcomed, O men — the daughter of the Sun. Your beautiful, bird-like, reddish horses carry you in the encounter.

**1.118.6**  You raised up Vandana with your wonders; you raised up Rebha, O wondrous ones, O mighty ones, with your powers. You bore the son of Tugra across the ocean; you made Cyavāna young again.

**1.118.7**  You placed warm nourishment and shelter for Atri who had fallen in distress, O Aśvins. And you, delighting in fair praise, restored sight to Kaṇva who had been robbed of it.

**1.118.8**  You filled with milk the cow for Śaya the ancient needy one, O Aśvins. You freed the quail from distress, and fitted Viśpalā's leg.

**1.118.9**  You two, O Aśvins, gave the white horse to Pedu — urged by Indra, serpent-slayer — the ever-invoked, the lord's, the mightily conquering, the prize-winner of thousands, the mighty-limbed bull.

**1.118.10**  You, O well-born men, O Aśvins, we call for our own protection, imploring you. Come to us near in your wealth-bearing chariot, delighting in hymns, for our well-being.

**1.118.11**  Come to us with the new speed of a hawk, in unison, O Nāsatyas. For the one who pours oblation calls upon you, O Aśvins, ever and ever at the shining forth of Dawn.

**1.119.1**  I call your chariot hither, O Aśvins, for our life — full of wonders, swift as thought, with spirited horses, worthy of worship; thousand-bannered, laden with wood, with a hundred treasures, prompt to hear, space-giving, to this libation.

**1.119.2**  Upright is the prayer at the going of this one; it was laid down in the chanting; the directions came together. The aids come sweetening the warm drink — the Ūrjānī woman has mounted your chariot, O Aśvins.

**1.119.3**  When the contestants, striving against each other, came together in the contest, the exuberant, uncounted, swift ones shone for glory in battle. Your chariot is perceived going downhill, O Aśvins, as you carry the patron to the prize.

**1.119.4**  You two carried Bhujyu who was tossing in the waters, in self-impelled ships, bringing him back to his fathers. You two, O mighty ones, won the conquest — your greatness and your aid to Divodāsa is well known.

**1.119.5**  The two women have guided your chariot, harnessed in youth for beauty, with its swift troop, O Aśvins. The maiden came seeking your friendship and companionship — she chose you two as her lords.

**1.119.6**  You two protect Rebha from distress, and the heat of frost searing Atri. You two filled the shelter of Śaya with a cow's milk — Vandana prolongs his life through a long span.

**1.119.7**  You two, O wondrous ones, restore Vandana from ruin through your craftsmanship, as workmen restore a chariot. You bring the inspired one back from the field with your skill — let the wonders of you both, who are to be pleased, shine forth here.

**1.119.8**  You came to the weeping one in the distant land, hemmed in and abandoned by his own father. Your aids that carry sunlight and that come from here have become wondrous reliefs in the moment of encounter.

**1.119.9**  And that sweet honey-dropping bee pours in the joy of Soma — the son of Uśij calls upon you. You two came to seek the mind of Dadhīca; and then the horse-head spoke your secret to you.

**1.119.10**  You two honour Pedu with the manifold gift, O Aśvins — the white horse, the vanquisher of rivals. With his arrows he strikes the luminous one, unshakeable in battles — Indra-like, overpowering all men.

**1.120.1**  Who shall fulfil the priestly office for you two, O Aśvins? Who shall delight you both? How shall the unwitting one arrange this?

**1.120.2**  Only the knower should ask of the knowing two; the non-knower goes astray, ignorant. Now indeed has any mortal accomplished it?

**1.120.3**  Those two who know — we call upon you; those two who know — speak the prayer to us today. The one who longs for you called out, praising.

**1.120.4**  I ask like a simple one before the gods, O wondrous Aśvins, of the marvellous one for whom the vaṣaṭ-call is made. Protect us from what overcomes, and from what strikes us down.

**1.120.5**  That song shines forth like the roaring of a fire — with that voice the Pajriya worships you both. The knowing one has sent it forth as one dispatches a messenger.

**1.120.6**  I have heard the Gāyatra song of Takvan; I too have been extolling you two, O Aśvins. The lords of beauty gave eyes to one.

**1.120.7**  For you two have rejoiced greatly in the home, or in whatever you have spread out together. Be our two good protectors full of treasure; guard us from the wicked, malicious wolf.

**1.120.8**  Give us not over to any hostile one; let not the milk-cows be taken anywhere from our houses — the young calves that suck the udder.

**1.120.9**  May the cow give milk for the gratification of Mitra, and you two, O eager ones, measure out to us prize-winning wealth and food rich in cattle.

**1.120.10**  The horseless chariot of the two Aśvins, full of prizes — with that I have greatly desired many things.

**1.120.11**  This very man, spreading out, does not decay among the peoples — the chariot of Soma-drinking is a pleasant one.

**1.120.12**  Thus both sleep and the wealthy man who enjoys without giving are seen to perish — both of them pass away at dawn.

**1.121.1**  How does he who is swift become the worthy vessel of god-seeking men, hearing the hymns of the Aṅgirasas? When he advances into the clans from the dwelling-place, the adorable one strides broadly at the sacrifice.

**1.121.2**  He indeed propped up heaven as its support; the skilful Ṛbhu sprinkled wealth in cattle for the heroes at the prize-contest. The great bull looked upon its own kind, the beloved companion, the mother of the horse — the cow.

**1.121.3**  The ruddy one, the primeval king, shall come swiftly to the call, following the days of the Aṅgirasa clans. He fashioned the thunderbolt with its team of steeds and propped up heaven — for the four-footed and the two-footed, for the hero's sake.

**1.121.4**  In his exhilaration he gave heavenly light to Ṛta — the host of the dawn-cows whose faces were veiled. When at the release he turned back Trikakubh, he drove away from you the guiles of mortal deceit.

**1.121.5**  The milk that the two parents brought to you, O swift and active one — the boon of good seed — that was your endowment; the pure wealth they offered you: the milk of the dawn-cow that yields together with the Sun.

**1.121.6**  Then the swift one was born and rejoiced; he blazed forth like the Sun at this Dawn. Indu struck with those offerings that bring sweat, pouring with the ladle toward the ancient dwelling.

**1.121.7**  When the forest-axe, kindled by its own fuel, sees the Sun at the sacrifice surrounded by the enclosures of the cow; when you shine through the days along the ritual paths — for the swift one who knows no weariness, who seeks cattle.

**1.121.8**  He smote the great heaven, bringing hither the two tawny steeds — glorious, conquering, fighting toward the spring. When they pressed out for your growth the tawny exhilarating one — swift as the wind, the one to be pressed on stones, the udder of cattle.

**1.121.9**  You turned back the iron stone of heaven brought close — O skilful one — belonging to the cow; where, O much-invoked Indra, conquering for Kutsa, you encircle Śuṣṇa with boundless deadly blows.

**1.121.10**  Before the Sun emerged from the darkness — O stone-wielder — you seized his cleft weapon. Even the might of Śuṣṇa that was set around him, firmly knotted from heaven — that you took away.

**1.121.11**  The two great expanses, Heaven and Earth, rejoiced following your deed, O Indra — let them exult. You slew with the great thunderbolt Vṛtra lying across the streams, the boar.

**1.121.12**  O Indra, heroic one — the men you favored, stand with the best-harnessed team of the wind. The exhilarating, Vṛtra-slaying thunderbolt for crossing over which the wise Uśanā Kāvya gave you — he fashioned it.

**1.121.13**  For this his might the seers, the Ṛbhus of good insight, fashioned their hymns and songs — those who illumine the glories of the prize-winning, the sacrificial — and Sarasvatī, the light of the Sun.

**1.121.14**  And indeed gazing at that one's act — the fathers at the sacrifice looked below like those of the lower one. I praise Ṛbhu and Cakra for their insight; may all beings cross over for his sake.

**1.121.15**  The guardian of the quarters, the roaring one who gives strength — an exhilarating shout for Indra at the pressed Soma. The divine waters press around the trough; grant me such a boon of Soma in the assembly.

**1.122.1**  I ask the joyful host of the Maruts, in their goodness, together with Vivasvat — Heaven and Earth: who are they who, following Ṛta in common, do not take account of mutual treachery?

**1.122.2**  Hither then, O Kavis, hither — O ye who touch Ṛta, great ones who touch Ṛta — hither Varuṇa, greatly; hither Mitra, the finder of heaven, the finder of heaven — you have borne them continuously.

**1.122.3**  Bring a bull's worth of riches from the south, O lord of the clans. Either from here may they rightly speak to you as you return, or from here may they rightly speak to you as you turn back.

**1.122.4**  Those who are the constant ones dwelling in the mighty powers of the arm — may Indra now perceive them at the pressed Soma; may he take heed of them.

**1.122.5**  May the stone-armed one thrust aside our adversarial boulder with his great weapon, thunderbolt in hand. Active Vāyu too — and Pūṣan — and the milch-cows of you two, O Aśvins, which have given milk to you.

**1.122.6**  Grant us vigor for our two-footed and four-footed creatures, following the ordinance of the bountiful one attending us. Both by day and night, O liberal one, strive — with the wealth of Soma's riches, with the full abundance.

**1.122.7**  May Pūṣan by his natural law come to our share before the clans make assault. Worship Ṛta, and also grant a wide share; O much-invoked one, bestow upon us all that is well yoked.

**1.122.8**  Come here, O Indra — you are great; great are those who carry praises; come. When Indra looks around, encompassing all common supports, he shines in heaven like the Sun.

**1.122.9**  Sing forth the hymn of praise to him who is the maternal uncle — with seven dark-bay mares; seven green chariots have come to you, like swans in the skilled songs.

**1.122.10**  Whatever boon belongs to you, O Indra, at the pressed Soma — of that exhilarating one — verily that is your boon. He who embellishes us, drawing us within his divine aid — that greatness of yours we shall praise.

**1.122.11**  May Mitra and Varuṇa come to us who have pressed Soma; let someone whose horse is swift come to our wish while it is moist. May a milch-cow come, may cattle come, may our transport come — may those two great ones increase us.

**1.122.12**  Sing to us the great one, O Mitra; O Varuṇa, the worthy of sacrifice in your greatness; may the god Bṛhaspati, he who reaches the highest, Puraṁdhi, come always bringing us brilliance.

**1.122.13**  Following a luminous, swift thought, the gods come along the path of approach. Thrice seven — three times seven — do they go to him with homage; homage to him who is revered with homage.

**1.122.14**  Possessors of Ṛta, yoked with Ṛta, well-milked, surpassing charioteers — you two occupy the foundation. Upholding the invincible one at the pressed Soma, O Mitras in the expanse of sky — favor us and bless us.

**1.122.15**  When Varuṇa proceeds to the breadth of the wealth of Mitra and of heaven, O king — be as a father to us with your aid; we call upon the inspired one for glory and sustenance.

**1.123.1**  Dawn indeed now shines; the goddess is truthful and bestows good guidance. For those whose chariot she carries — with dark bays at the front — Dawn is the countersign of the gods with their benevolence.

**1.123.2**  She goes before, Dawn goes forth; daylight has become apparent, greeting the Dawn. The good glory, strength, and bounty of Āyāsya extend forward day by day, as it were — extending day by day.

**1.123.3**  Ancient wisdom proclaims the new: what is ever desirable, O Uṣas, shall come to be. Like cows going in herds to the pen, she illumines all with her light — shining upon all as she comes near.

**1.123.4**  The sacrificial leavings go up to the thunderbolt-wielder with ghee; the righteous ones follow the path of Ṛta. The swift ones, the portions of the seven-wheeled, two-footed one, travel around the world like the seeds of the ocean.

**1.123.5**  O Uṣas, this truthful one calls forth the living world for you; sing praises; carry onward the undecaying one. By these means may the chariot dwelling in heaven — truthful and imperishable — follow the path of our right deeds, O goddess.

**1.123.6**  O Uṣas, awaken the father who is bountiful; come, come as the best of dawns. The truthful she-camel goes like night; the brilliant one is born, freed from darkness.

**1.123.7**  Granting long life at her approach, made visible — auspicious, fortunate, well-disposed — Uṣas has come. She unfurls his banner without obstruction, like a well-dressed wife who does not fear her husband.

**1.123.8**  You have shone, O truthful one — you who seize the cows; may the clear hymn become the treasure of her. For he who, when she scatters her gifts, does not rejoice in your wish — the wise one who desires proper measure shall find you wealthy in true goods.

**1.123.9**  How long until that Dawn shall be seen from near at hand — even this clear, shining, distant one? Night has embraced herself with the night, making light, driving the righteous onward.

**1.123.10**  O Uṣas, shining brilliantly — the wealth-bearing, the newly-born, the new day that carries one onward. When you both kings shall shine together upon each other, grant us good heroes and cattle separately.

**1.123.11**  O Uṣas, those who, with your gifts, truthful and leading, make crossings — may the straight one drive along the plains and ridges; may those who are wise be triumphant in the acquisition of wisdom according to Ṛta.

**1.123.12**  Those who, O Uṣas, grasp your benevolence — like a horse capable now of winning the prize — may their wise one lead and may the longing for that thought be joined with you, O divine Dawn.

**1.123.13**  The most brilliant one, Agni the lord of the clan, has now purified you the brilliant one in grace toward good intent. Agni has shouted toward the gods for the great winning of the prize.

**1.124.1**  The ruddy sisters come unseen from afar, dispersing long darkness in the distance. The one who yokes the seven in heaven, devoted to their source — bestowing upon the great one, he has become readied.

**1.124.2**  They who spread light, who give strength — they are coming; the eastern one dawns, pressing forward with radiance. Dawn upon dawn they become the same; at Dawn men reach for riches — those are her men.

**1.124.3**  Against her the fearful ruddy one has indeed rushed, striking the buffalo like a drum with her feet. Let us proclaim Dawn fully glorious — a goddess of great might, not to be surpassed.

**1.124.4**  These Dawn-goddesses have come to the foundation of Ṛta, following the path that belongs to the gods' seat. The ruddy ones have shone, shedding their bodies — these are called forth like cows to their resting-place.

**1.124.5**  The ruddy ones drive the cows here for him — within the womb all beings are born together. Polishing and bestowing wealth, Dawn comes like the great waters to the nourishment of the land.
