**1.124.6**  O Dawn, you who go — rest away from those who slumber, and those who, fortunate, seek your bounty. This benevolent Dawn has dawned wide; she is most generous with bounty to human Manu, with her generous gift to us.

**1.124.7**  Dawn has revealed this choice of his, radiant in its restraint, full of brilliance. The Dawns move swiftly across and beyond — the great Sindhu flooding, crossing like the oceans.

**1.124.8**  O Uṣas, bring wealth quickly for the Soma-drinking — you who are the divine one who carries the divine in front. The mother goddess set in seven rays of renown; the divine one reached the wealth in pursuit of the son's prosperity.

**1.124.9**  When they rejoice now at the highest abode, man follows the path of Ṛta. O Uṣas, bring to us all wealth — good heroism, the increase of riches.

**1.124.10**  O Uṣas, come along the path — the share, the exhilaration of the Aṅgirasas, cattle-rich, with good hymns for us. The earlier ones followed at your dawnings; O wealth-bearing one, bring riches and return with riches.

**1.124.11**  The auspicious tawny-green horses of varying kinds, by which the wife of the Sun is well-nourished — O Uṣas, driving along the broad ordinance, bring us wealth; let no praise of ours be fruitless.

**1.124.12**  O divine goddess, most divine of the wealthy and goods — with your arts, well-disposed toward us — awaken at this dawn today; awaken on this day for the pressing, for the praisers.

**1.124.13**  O Uṣas, grant to us wealth of wide spread in many forms — with offspring, with horses. May the divine seer, being purified along the extension of his life by the law, praise what is set as the measure, greatly lauded.

**1.125.1**  Proclaim for me, the very pious one, a good praise — is he truly the great, noble one, O Indra? He who goes to war with the highest one — to him grant a cow, yield the cattle-wealth with protection.

**1.125.2**  He who has helped him along — surely he, O Śakra, is insight in the village during the prize-fights of your fortress. May the one who would set aside golden ornament and wisdom for any man — let us, praising, go to riches, well-crossed.

**1.125.3**  Moreover, the sight of Kakṣīvat — he has enjoyed the plump wealth, the sin is upon him. For he who stands best in aid goes toward those who call for help, as you have declared good thought to Kaṇva.

**1.125.4**  Both the brahmin and the rājanya hear this — the prize-winning stallion shall be at hand. He who has heard shall hear; the most sacrifice-worthy one — may the god, the Vṛtra-slayer, be praised constantly.

**1.125.5**  He who goes away from the circle for twelve months — he is the one graciously devoted to you, O people of mine. To him grant a cow from among ten cows — a herd with a hundred leaders — and the prosperity of riches, a good path.

**1.125.6**  He who gave gold to Gaviṣṭhira, five hundreds — with the cattle of Gaviṣṭhira — you have spoken the wondrous greatness of Kṛṣṇa; shall that one now be for us with cattle and horses?

**1.125.7**  May the patron sit around this one with the host of the gods; let the rich one share him a name of beautiful riches. May he in whom today there is the share of this grass — the great prize-winner — place wealth here.

**1.126.1**  Those of good deeds indeed come from the heavens, going around, entering the kingdoms. The all-living god Aditi is worthy of sacrifice — we have yoked ourselves to their host for praise.

**1.126.2**  This is that hymn for the one going beyond — all the gods follow with dark bays in front. The pure Indu goes forth to the discerning one; having drunk, then understand what is destined for the good-invoked one — having drunk.

**1.126.3**  O Indra, much-invoked one — praised now by the ancient seers with sacrifices; now, too, O Indra, help us greatly; be in front as a direction of your wealth; be for us as heroes.

**1.126.4**  May the patron come to us with all invocations — with horses, with gold, with cattle. Let not the demons harm us in well-being; speak for us from the height of heaven.

**1.126.5**  A poet among poets, he is poetic in his nature — he who is in the great glory of Ṛta. Bring those fires to us for our greatness; I sing the seven necklace-wearers.

**1.126.6**  With a rosy-hair garland, ruddy steeds growing high — Maruts with prize-winning streaks on their bodies. I, coming from among the Aṅgirasas, have come to the generous giver for twenty-five years.

**1.126.7**  May they who are generous see the fire-priest nearby for a hundred winters. May our wealthy ones cross over; come together, O prize-winning ones, to the hundred greatnesses.

**1.127.1**  Pure, purifying, very bright, pure in the womb, pure on the earth, clothed in purity — with pure names, the worshipful one worthy of sacrifice — we praise divine Agni with hymns of praise.

**1.127.2**  He who, delighting in the gods, blazes following the seven, well-born like a shining child with flame — when the pure one went out and separated from the hard ones, may he share the gods with Vāyu at the good kindling.

**1.127.3**  One has fashioned it by his own self, like a single beast; the powerful one stands established in the womb. In the prize-contest, like one free of harm and bearing good prizes — invoke Agni as the calling priest, Agni.

**1.127.4**  O Agni, the gods praise you, possessing Indra among the churning sticks, as Agni, for our calling-sacrifice. May our sacrifices at the ritual — with Agni as their ocean, Soma-drinking ones — have a share, and also the simple ones.

**1.127.5**  At whose will he is born together — I praise him — the Agni of highest essence whom Indra rules. O Agni, you are the indefatigable person; you indeed are the divine good dwelling-place.

**1.127.6**  You are the first giver of wealth in the foremost place — horse-giving, bestowing shares; come then to us. You delight well in good hymns for traversal, like chanting ones — you who shine bright like wealthy widows.

**1.127.7**  This is your seasonal womb — may they anoint it all around with homage and blessings. Those who are worthy of sacrifice, who indeed seek to gain Indra and Agni with homage and good praises.

**1.127.8**  He whose great flame of the brilliant one grows downward broadly and greatly extends beyond — let it be forth for us. O beautiful one of all treasures, hold back for yourself what is well-disposed; he is of good mind, of good insight, worthy of nourishment.

**1.127.9**  Agni as the calling priest has sat down; Ṛta is sacrificed; all the gods rejoice following. Agni, the lord of the clan who delights heroic men — as the calling priest, place the prosperity of wealth for the sacrificer.

**1.127.10**  This fire — the kindling has roused it; our mind shines knowingly among mortals. O seer, when the great bull is worthy by its power — either by day in the sacrifice or in the acquiring of wisdom.

**1.127.11**  O Indra, these many powers of yours — or your greatness, O Śakra, let them now speak forth. Those men who, like Manu, assemble at your side — be the guide of our fame, of our call.

**1.128.1**  He has blazed like a man against the day, has spread brilliant, bright gold. He who blazes in ghee with these, of good wisdom — when the she-camels come forward, harnessed and dwelling in him.

**1.128.2**  He who, bearing oblations, newest of the restrainers — the brilliant Agni has spread between the two worlds. That blazing one for the son of him of brilliant color — that seer, that ancient one, shines in awe.

**1.128.3**  The much-desired calling priest advances, truly invoked in turn against each desire — invoking the folk. When the measured flames blaze forth with their rays — may the chariot-team come with its spear as with brilliance.

**1.128.4**  Sitting down, O resolute will-power — you who distribute good intent, stand beside the quiver of arrows; O Śakra, grant the share of the pressed one, the portion of the pressed Soma. Sing to him the cowherd songs; spur these on; grant a share of heaven well-entered.

**1.128.5**  Those men who celebrate Agni as the calling priest who has sat down by Ṛta, who is established by Ṛta in the womb — those who share in the Gāyatrī and Tṛṣṭubh meters partake of the Bṛhatī and its glory.

**1.128.6**  Those who, gaining the sacrifice by sacrifice — for me, O Indu, the sight of the pressed one comes in like rain. For Agni indeed carries the sacrifice-worthy one along in the sacrifice — the great one with the great ones, the carrier of hymns.

**1.128.7**  May Agni bear steady wealth and all life for long life and the sacrifice. May Agni look upon the gods with desire; may waters and Dawn become proclaimed for us.

**1.128.8**  All of them, beloved, approach Agni with flames; the young man's good intent belongs to all the folk. May you speak of the great seat of heaven, the tawny-golden one — the broad wealth laid out, that share.

**1.129.1**  O hero of good deeds, let vigor and good dwelling come to us through most pleasant thoughts. Your hiding places rich in cattle and renown, O Indra — encompass us in the contests, O Jātavedas.

**1.129.2**  You, O Aṅgiras, the first warrior — swiftly rousing the aides of the gods; come to the drinking of the pressed Soma; be of us, be for us of the pressed ones.

**1.129.3**  When, O Aṅgiras, all the Maruts and all the gods attend the Indu of all treasures, combined in the highest — this patron consecrated with 'Vaṣaṭ' at the ritual — may your praises never fail.

**1.129.4**  O divine Agni, O bright goddess — let the possessor of Ṛta sit within, within the wide and broadly praised — that he of truth may grant a share to this one and that of the wealth of the blameless one, the beneficent one who possesses Ṛta.

**1.129.5**  Going headlong with Svāhā-cries they come and wither away — any whose hostilities toward us burn fierce as Bhṛgu's. May Agni and Soma put those below us in the firmament and on the earth — protect us from the Rākṣasa.

**1.129.6**  O Indra, lead toward us the most excellent prosperity of wealth with a thousand gifts; extend it. Be our shelter; be our vigor — O Indu, make the boon most plentiful and be for us.

**1.129.7**  The generous one who gives thousands and leads hundreds — all beloved ones approaching him. You who are non-terrible — think of us too; hear the praiser in the prize-contest. Those heroes who are fully readied with generosity — go among them.

**1.129.8**  O Indra, all peoples — let none of us dwell quietly with calm neighbors. The Vṛtra-slayer — for us, as such — this word goes forth, O Indra; whatever becomes impelling, following in its wake.

**1.129.9**  Drive forth with our hymn of praise toward what is great, what is heard — having drunk the Soma, invigorating, you who have been such. With brilliant wealth come near to us, O widely heard one; be for us who have pressed the Soma.

**1.129.10**  The chariot of your yoke-companion for the prize comes, O Indra, bringing thousands at the good praise. The mighty one, leader of peoples — come to the praisers from the sky, come from all stations.

**1.129.11**  This is your praiser now, O Indra — he who is invoked in this day, O generous one, present everywhere. When, O Indra, you distribute your gifts at the pressed Soma — do that for him.

**1.130.1**  Come — for we have heard; come to our calling, O you of a hundred deeds. He has announced the gift again; now come to the gods for the share. Come to the prosperity of wealth.

**1.130.2**  He who is together with his favorable ones, the allied force like a ram increasing — even Agni with arrows may I come. He who knows no equal, most worthy of sacrifice, bearer of oblations — I press forward the pressing of the gods' pressed Soma.

**1.130.3**  The good hymn — he who is the charioteer of charioteers, in its separate golden arrow of the Paṇis — bring that to Indra the Soma-drinker, fragrant for us; do make it so.

**1.130.4**  Whatever and however many — all of us, like every Ātreya approaching a tree, have stood before him; he who is the hero, hard to restrain, the powerful one — leaving those behind, he abandoned them beyond the sky.

**1.130.5**  Praise that greatness of his too — that, urged on, he drinks the Soma without dwelling still. The charioteer drives Indu with good praise — certain sacrificers were in the new chariots.

**1.130.6**  They say: in the enclosure the far-shining one saw — when Vṛtra covered all things before the undoing of darkness, standing around, covering with his mouth the waters — 'Let the streams be seen, let them arise!'

**1.130.7**  Praise his greatness for him too — the praises by which the kindling of golden fires is assembled. When the well-dressed women with their lover — like well-dressed women.

**1.130.8**  This indeed has been said: to whichever man the two seers teach with understanding — when you two make the extension of Agni thousandfold, manifold, to be grasped.

**1.130.9**  Praise also for him this one, O Indra — this firm one who discerns with his seasons the earth: he who is the great ruler of peoples, whose vigor the wise one, the inspired one, wishes to nourish.

**1.130.10**  For this fierce, mighty one let the prize-winner be well fashioned at the prize-contest, O Indra, through words of truth. For you are the most pious one of the people — may riches come to us like joyful intentions and the capacity of action.

**1.131.1**  You bear, O generous Indra, well-hymned thousandfold wealth, most blessed with Soma-drinking — you who are gathered together with the sacrificial ones, O Indra, and are marshaled with your patrons.

**1.131.2**  Bring to us, O Agni, great good thought for us; lead forth the praisers as you have been told. You are also the generous one among them, the much-praised.

**1.131.3**  O Indra, may all acts of good intention abide in our dwelling; be our protector. O Indra, O you of great heroic power — let a hundred riches, many in number, come, streaming forth from the cows.

**1.131.4**  To attain the great one, the rejoicing one, O warrior, come near, come to the assembly. Be our protector; with your allies, a warrior in the assembly with the heroes.

**1.131.5**  May your offspring increase you, may the pressed exhilarating juices increase you. Be our herdsman, O Śakra, of wide fame, shooting arrows, speeding — the patron's thought.

**1.131.6**  The mighty prayer, the great one, is set in place; with all your speech the months have come toward you. The Soma-drinker — for Indra's aid; yesterday, the two horses were the wheel for Indra.

**1.131.7**  O generous one, establish good intentions among us — breaking open fortresses, you are well praised for your singer. O Indra, share all wealth with the generous one, well praised with song.

**1.132.1**  He saw from the station of his father and aroused all the wives and Vāyu. Indra alone, in the race with sevenfold substance — he does not strike the one who is not a warrior fit for your battle.

**1.132.2**  For him, for Indra — and for us, let this come forth abundantly, whatever approaches all pervading. Now indeed, when the arrows of wealth fly — he bestows something from all the pressed ones.

**1.132.3**  This sacrifice, this pressed Soma — bring it to Indra with homage. Let this one, with hymns, pierce through and make our well-known shout reach the peoples.

**1.132.4**  This is what those many have spoken, against his power: for Indra who drinks Soma most fully — the king of the world has seized all things.

**1.132.5**  O Indra, for him and for us may we — the generous heroes — share in the gift of these; of the pressed ones, the laws of Ṛta, heroic in our resolve.

**1.132.6**  Indra the good protector, with good guidance, with beautiful hands — in cattle a good giver by design. May he, all-living, divine — share with us all nourishments, the patron's kingdom.

**1.133.1**  The good protector of wide bounty — Indra I beseech; a hundred sacrifices — and for him a hundred more; may Indra be victorious. Those who are not zealous for him — born to fail, may they be defeated in the contest.

**1.133.2**  Now the bright one turns it homeward for the treacherous, wretched one — may Indra yoke for the battle. Away from the white bull's deceits; from Kṛṣṇa's — may all of that know heaven.

**1.133.3**  Indeed your golden gift for a hundred men places the yoke today in this day too. Before you, O Indra, let the bright gifts of the deceivers now go downward — like waters flowing downward to the nourishment.

**1.133.4**  Rise up, O patron, for our wealth; do not neglect the good disposition of the mind. Come rushing toward those clans who know; we draw away the waters.

**1.133.5**  Drive away those knowing clans with whose divine powers I am not — those whose thought for Indra, as for the most sacrifice-worthy one, does not prosper — drive them away.

**1.133.6**  Extend for us homage, O Indra, for light. With all good praise or riches — O Indu, make the nourishment grow.

**1.133.7**  O Niṣkaṇva, seize all beings two-footed and four-footed. Whatever good there is for you — for gracious intent — O Vāyu, Viśvabheda, bring that best thing to us; homage.

**1.134.1**  O Vāyu, yoke the two tawny-maned ones to the chariot — the tawny, tawny ones he has covered; you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma, you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma. You have urged on the oblation poured below among all, among the gods; be beloved; O Vāyu, come here.

**1.134.2**  O Vāyu, O bold one, may your thousands of chariots with their teams of tawny horses swoop to us — tawny after tawny; you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma, you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma. May they carry hither the Soma-presser, the fire — and spur Vāyu forward for the conquering one; homage.

**1.134.3**  O Vāyu, your warriors of a hundred fronts, with teams of tawny horses, in front — you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma, you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma. These pressed ones, sweet with honey, pressed on stones — like eagles they fly down — of the pressed ones.

**1.134.4**  O Vāyu, you who are brilliant for the hymn — come to the drinking of the pressed Soma, come to the drinking of the pressed Soma. May the son's oblation go forth for your aid; establish all good things for the sacrificer.

**1.134.5**  O Vāyu, you deserve the drinking of these pressed Soma-drops and also of the former one, you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma, you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma. Moreover at all the rituals may you set your pleasure; set the path of the pressed ones that is dear to you, your vital food.

**1.134.6**  O Vāyu, you who are peerless — you deserve the first drinking of these Soma draughts, you deserve the drinking of the pressed Soma. Moreover, of the clans with wide-scattering impulse, who have been set apart — all your milch-cows yield the mixture with ghee, they yield the admixture with ghee.

**1.135.1**  The sacred grass is strewn — come to us for the feast, O Vāyu, with a thousand steeds in your team, with a hundred in your team. For to you the gods have extended the first drinking, O god among gods. Forth have your pressed, sweet Soma-draughts stood ready — they have stood ready for exhilaration and for action.

**1.135.2**  For you this Soma flows around, strained through the stones — desirable, clothed in beauty, it flows around the vessel, clothed in brilliance it flows. This Soma is your share, invoked among the gods among living beings. Drive here, O Vāyu, with your team — come desiring us, come desiring us with joy.

**1.135.3**  Come to our ritual with your teams of hundreds, of thousands — O Vāyu, come for the feast; come for the oblations. This your share is seasonal, following the Sun's rays. Borne by the Adhvaryus, they have been arranged, O Vāyu — the bright ones have been arranged.

**1.135.4**  May the chariot with its team bring you two here for aid, toward the well-laid out enjoyments — O Vāyu, for the feast, for the oblations. Drink of the sweet juice — to you belongs the first drinking. O Vāyu, come with gleaming bounty, and Indra too — come with bounty.

**1.135.5**  May our prayers have turned the two of you toward the ritual; they have polished this swift Indu like a prize-winning, swift steed. Drink of those, desiring us — come here today. O Indravāyu, you two, of the pressed ones pressed with stones, for exhilaration — you two the prize-givers.

**1.135.6**  These Soma draughts pressed for you two in the waters here — borne by the Adhvaryus they have been arranged, O Vāyu; the bright ones have been arranged. These have been let flow toward you two through the filter, swiftly. Like eager young men passing through the wool of the sheep — the Soma draughts passing through the wool.

**1.135.7**  O Vāyu, pass by those who ever sleep; where the pressing stone speaks — go there, to that house; Indra too shall go there. Truth-speech has been seen; ghee flows; come to the ritual with your full team, O Vāyu, and Indra come to the ritual.

**1.135.8**  Here indeed carry this sweet oblation — the one that the wives attend under the aśvattha-tree; may those wives be ours. Together the cows give birth, the barley is cooked; O Vāyu, your milch-cows do not fall short, the milch-cows do not fail.

**1.135.9**  These who are yours, O Vāyu, of strong-armed power — bulls fly in the rivers between them, the bulls swell greatly. Even on the desert plain those who are tireless, those swift ones dwelling on the mountain: like the rays of the Sun, hard to restrain in the hands — hard to restrain in the hands.

**1.136.1**  Bring forward the greatest offering, the rich oblation, the thought, to those twain who bestow grace most sweetly, most graciously. Those two sovereigns, consecrated with ghee, are praised at every sacrifice. Therefore their dominion cannot be assailed from anywhere, nor can their divinity ever be challenged.

**1.136.2**  The broad, most spacious path of ṛta has been seen; it was gathered together by the rays of the sun, by the rays of Bhaga's eye. Luminous is the seat of Mitra, of Aryaman, and of Varuṇa. Thus they assume a great and laudable glory, a great glory sung in praise.

**1.136.3**  They two, ever wakeful, ever watchful day by day, attend upon Aditi who bears light and sustains all living beings and the sunlit realm. Those Ādityas, lords of bounty, share the lustrous dominion. Mitra among them and Varuṇa order all men to righteousness, as does Aryaman who sets men aright.

**1.136.4**  May this Soma, most health-bestowing, be the share of Mitra and Varuṇa at the draughts, the divine one sharing among the gods. May all the gods together delight in him today with one accord. So shall ye two kings who uphold ṛta grant what we implore of you, upholders of truth what we implore.

**1.136.5**  The mortal who has devoted worship to Mitra and Varuṇa without stumbling — him do ye protect, the bountiful man, from anguish, from sin. Aryaman guards him who walks upright in their ordinance. He who adorns their commandment with hymns, who decks their ordinance with songs of praise — him Aryaman protects.

**1.136.6**  I would offer homage to great Heaven and to the two worlds; I would speak to Mitra and to bountiful Varuṇa, the most graciously merciful, most bountiful. Praise too the sky-dwelling Indra, Agni, Aryaman, and Bhaga. May we live long, accompanied by offspring, and be united under Soma's protection.

**1.136.7**  Under the protection of the gods, we with Indra as our ally and with the self-glorious Maruts, may we be favored. May Agni, Mitra, and Varuṇa grant us shelter — may we, the bountiful patrons, attain that boon, and we ourselves attain it.

**1.137.1**  Come hither readily, O Mitra and Varuṇa — these exhilarating Soma-draughts, pressed by the stones, mixed with milk, are ready, exhilarating. O ye two kings who touch the sky, come to us here. These Somas for you, O Mitra and Varuṇa, are blended with milk, bright, blended with milk.

**1.137.2**  Come here to these drops, these Soma-juices blended with curds, pressed out and blended with curds. And at the dawning of the day, together with the rays of the sun, may the pressed Soma be offered to Mitra and Varuṇa to drink, the dear draught offered for drinking to ṛta.

**1.137.3**  Like a milch-cow at dawn they milk the Soma-stalk with the pressing-stones, they milk the Soma with the stones. Come hither to us, both of you, turn toward us for the drinking of Soma. This Soma, O Mitra and Varuṇa, has been pressed by men for you to drink, pressed for your drinking.

**1.138.1**  Ever and again the greatness of Pūṣan, mightily born, is proclaimed; the praise of this vigorous one ceases not, his hymn of praise does not cease. I worship him, seeking his favor, him whose protection comes at the last and who brings well-being — Pūṣan the god who knits together the mind of all creation, who as the divine Makha knits all together.

**1.138.2**  I urge you forward, O Pūṣan, swift as a swift horse on the journey, with hymns as with a camel that overflows with milk — for you overcome resistance. I invoke you, the divine bringer of well-being, as a mortal calls for friendship. Make our praises full of splendor; make us glorious in contests.

**1.138.3**  Those inspired sages who, even in their wisdom, have enjoyed your friendship and your protection, O Pūṣan, have indeed enjoyed it by their intelligence. Following that same path, we seek of you a fresher wealth, a band of treasure. O widely praised one, be not displeased — be our guide, be our guide at every contest.

**1.138.4**  O goat-horsed one, be present for us here, without displeasure, bringing gifts, for the winning of this booty — let the goat-drawn car speed on! We would turn you back toward us, O wonderful one, with these effective praises. Never, O Pūṣan, O radiant one, do I disregard you; never do I deny your friendship.

**1.139.1**  Let it be heard — śrauṣaṭ! First set Agni in place with devotion; now let us choose that divine host, let us choose Indra and Vāyu. When in the active one, at the navel of Vivasvat, the newer bond was made — then may our prayers come forth and go toward the gods, our prayers go toward them.

**1.139.2**  When you two, O Mitra and Varuṇa, by your own will took hold of untruth beyond ṛta, by your own impulse of wisdom — in your very abodes we have seen the golden one. We have seen it with our thoughts, with our minds, with our own eyes, with the eyes of Soma's own.

**1.139.3**  The god-seeking descendants of Āyu have made their call resound to you Aśvins with their hymns, like a resonant sound; they called you two with their oblations. Upon you two who know all wealth, all glories and refreshments rest. The fellies of your golden chariot, O Dasrās, sprinkle abundantly — of your golden chariot.

**1.139.4**  It has become manifest, O Dasrās — you two have opened up the vault of heaven; the chariot-yokers harness your car in the heavenly courses, without blemish in the heavenly courses. Your standing place is on the chariot-seat, O Dasrās, on the golden chariot. Driving as if along a path, you guide the aerial space, swiftly guiding space.

**1.139.5**  O Aśvins, rich in might, with your mighty deeds show favor to us by day and by night. May your bounty never fail us, never at any time may your gift to us fail.

**1.139.6**  O mighty Indra, these are the inebriating Soma drops pressed by the stones, gushing forth, pressed out for you, gushing up. May they exhilarate you to give the great, the splendid bounty. O praised one, borne by hymns, come, lauded by songs; come to us as one most gracious.

**1.139.7**  O Agni, when invoked, do listen to us; you speak to the worshipful gods, the worthy kings to be worshipped. When once the gods bestowed that cow upon the Aṅgirases, Aryaman and the she-cutter milked her in company — that one I know in my own company.

**1.139.8**  Let not those manly powers, those ancient glories of yours, fall away from us; let them not vanish from before us. Whatever of yours is wondrous, age after age renewed, resounding, immortal — that, O Maruts, fix upon us; and what is hard to win, fix that hard-won thing upon us.

**1.139.9**  Dadhyac, Aṅgiras the elder, Priyamedha, Kaṇva, Atri, and Manu — these knew my birth; those foremost ones, Manu knew. Their access is to the gods; our kinship lies with them. By their footstep may I bow down to the great one with my hymns — to Indra and Agni may I bow with my hymn.

**1.139.10**  The Hotā priest shall worship, the trees of the forest shall receive the desired gift; Bṛhaspati sacrifices, and Veṇa with the bulls, with many-desired bulls. We have grasped the far-resounding call of the stone by its own power. The wise one of good purpose has upheld the yielding places — many abodes, the skilled one.

**1.139.11**  Ye gods who are eleven in heaven, who are eleven upon the earth, who dwell in the waters — ye eleven in your greatness — be pleased with this sacrifice of ours.

**1.140.1**  Bring the nourishment as if to its resting-place, to Agni who loves the sacred seat, who loves his dear abode, who blazes bright. Clothe him in your thought as with a garment — the pure one, the chariot of light, the bright-hued, the dispeller of darkness.

**1.140.2**  Agni of double birth, with three-fold food, is urged on; he has grown throughout the year, eating and again consuming. With one mouth's tongue he is a hero worthy of song; with another he licks clean the trees, the defender.

**1.140.3**  His two abiding companions, the black and the tawny, tremble; both mothers bend over the child. Eastward-tongued, sending smoke aloft, poured from the ladle, bearing the companion of Kupa, the sustainer of his father.

**1.140.4**  Eager to be freed, for Manu, for the offspring of Manu, the swift-rushing ones with black wake, the fleet ones — unequal, nimble, swift of leap, driven by the wind, the swift ones are yoked together.

**1.140.5**  Then his attendants, scattering sparks, roam about; making a great form, a black, formidable one, crackling. When he eagerly licks across the broad earth, hissing, thundering, he goes roaring.

**1.140.6**  Like one who adorns himself, they bow above the tawny ones; like a bull approaching his mates, he comes bellowing. Growing in strength he makes his body resplendent; terrible as one wielding horns, he flourishes hard to grasp.

**1.140.7**  He who is spread out and spread abroad gathers himself; knowing, he lies ever among those who know. Again they grow and attain the divine; they take on another form together from both parents.

**1.140.8**  The long-haired, virgin ones have sung together to him; they stand erect, though seemingly dead, ready to go forth again. Loosening their age, he goes roaring, generating a supreme life, the un-felled living flame.

**1.140.9**  Licking round the garment of his mother, he goes forth with his great-eating heroes, spreading everywhere. Bestowing sustenance, he licks for the four-footed ever; the hawk-like one follows the paths of day.

**1.140.10**  Shine forth, O Agni, among our patrons; then like a roaring bull, the household lord. You have shone upon the mothers with their young who are to be unveiled — like armor in battle, thundering around them.

**1.140.11**  O Agni, may this that is well-uttered be more dear to you than what is ill-uttered, even than what is cherished in the heart; may it be more pleasing to you. By that bright, pure glory that blazes from your body, do win for us a treasure.

**1.140.12**  O Agni, grant us a boat for the chariot, for our house a vessel with a perpetual rudder, bearing feet. And may it carry across our heroes, our bountiful ones, and our people — carrying them safely across.

**1.140.13**  O Agni, may heaven, earth, and the waters, well-pleased, guard our utterance. Going forward seeking cattle and grain, with long call may the reddish ones claim the boon.

**1.141.1**  Truly so — for beauty it was fashioned, the visible splendor of the god born of power. When the inspired thought draws near and accomplishes its purpose, the streams of ṛta flow on and are led forth.

**1.141.2**  His nourishing form, full of food, lies ever in his place; a second is among the seven kindly mothers. A third of this bull is for milking — the ten-directing one — the women bring it forth.

**1.141.3**  When the wise, sovereign ones with power step forth from the base of the mighty bull's form — when following from the primeval time, from the sweetness of the pressing, Mātariśvan churns what is hidden within.

**1.141.4**  When he is led forth from the highest of his fathers, round about plants and creeping things grow thick and lush. When both mothers set in motion his birth — then the youngest one blazed up, pure with radiant glow.

**1.141.5**  Then he entered his mothers in whom he shines pure; unhurt, he grew abundantly. Climbing his former timeless ones who were ever-swift, he glides downward into newer and lower ones.

**1.141.6**  Then they choose him as the Hotā at the heavenly journeys; they go to meet him as if anointing Bhaga. When, wide-praised in wisdom and greatness, he goes altogether seeking mortal praise to sustain himself.

**1.141.7**  When the worshipful one driven by wind stands forth — crooked as a snake, advancing with age but not weakened — on the path of that eager one of dark strides and pure birth, the realm of space was shot through.

**1.141.8**  Like a chariot driven by the skilled, built with skill, he moves through heaven with his red-glowing limbs. Then his dark, keen ones, the wise patrons — like the swiftness of a hero — they take wing with impetuosity.

**1.141.9**  Through you, O Agni, Varuṇa of firm ordinance, and Mitra, and Aryaman of good gifts have been established. Following their purpose, all-pervading everywhere, you were born as one who encompasses all, as the felloe encompasses the spokes.

**1.141.10**  You, O Agni, youngest, send the divine bounty to the striving one who presses Soma. You, ever new, scion of strength — let us now focus upon you as upon Bhaga in our work, O bearer of great treasure.

**1.141.11**  You have not blended upon us the household wealth like the Sun's bounty, the portion and power supporting us. He who guides both births like reins — of the gods' praise and of ṛta — he is the wise one of good purpose.

**1.141.12**  And may the brightly shining, swift-horsed, joyful Hotā, with his gleaming chariot, hear us. May Agni, the blameless one with the finest charioteers, lead us — lead us toward the good, toward well-being, toward wealth.

**1.141.13**  Agni has been praised with energetic hymns, laying the groundwork for universal sovereignty. Both those bounteous ones and we ourselves — like the sun through mist, we have burst forth in brightness.

**1.142.1**  O Agni, enkindled, bring the gods hither today for the one who reaches out with the ladle. Extend the primeval thread for the generous one who has pressed the Soma.

**1.142.2**  O Tanūnapāt, thou dost approach the ghee-filled, honey-sweet sacrifice of this inspired, well-disposed, and generous worshipper.

**1.142.3**  Pure and purifying, wonderful, Narāśaṁsa sprinkles the sacrifice with sweetness. The god, worthy of worship among gods, comes down three times from heaven.

**1.142.4**  O Agni, invoked, bring here the dear and wondrous Indra. For this thought of mine calls to you, O fair-tongued one.

**1.142.5**  Spreading the litter, reaching out with the ladle at the sacrifice, at the well-ordered rite, I choose the most god-reaching, wide-spreading shelter for Indra.

**1.142.6**  Let the great divine doors, upholders of ṛta, swing open for the passing of the gods, the purifying, much-desired, never-failing divine doors.

**1.142.7**  The two rejoicing, close at hand, fair-formed Night and Dawn — the two great mothers of ṛta — may they take their seat on the sacred grass, bringing good things.

**1.142.8**  The two divine seers, the Hotā-priests with honey-tongued voices, ever welcoming — may the two poets sacrifice this our offering today, bringing it surely to the sky-touching goal.

**1.142.9**  Pure Hotrā, laid before the gods among the Maruts, is Bhāratī. Iḷā, Sarasvatī, and Mahī — the three goddesses worthy of worship — let them sit on the sacrificial grass.

**1.142.10**  May Tvaṣṭar, who loves us, the center of riches, release that wonderful nourishment, full of many gifts, bountiful by his very nature, for prosperity and wealth.

**1.142.11**  O Vanaspati, send forth and worship the gods of your own accord. Agni, the wise god among gods, will convey the oblations rightly.

**1.142.12**  To Pūṣan's company, to the Marut-host, to the All-Gods, to Vāyu — svāhā! Make the oblation to Indra who is moved by the Gāyatrī.

**1.142.13**  Come here to the things consecrated with svāhā; come to the oblations for your pleasure. Come here, O Indra, hear the call — they call you at the sacrifice.

**1.143.1**  I bring a stronger and newer thought, an utterance of speech, to Agni, son of strength. He, the child of the waters, who dwells dear with the Vasus, the Hotā, has taken his seat on the earth in due season.

**1.143.2**  Being born in the highest heaven, Agni became manifest to Mātariśvan. By the wisdom of this enkindled one, by his greatness, he made heaven and earth shine brilliantly with his radiance.

**1.143.3**  His fierce, ageless rays, the rays of this fair-faced, brightly blazing one are beautiful to behold. Like rivers flowing past the night, blazing and ageless, O Agni, they flash.

**1.143.4**  Him whom the Bhṛgus set in motion, all-knowing, at the navel of the earth, at the center of existence — urge that Agni with your hymns in your own home, he who alone rules over riches as Varuṇa rules.

**1.143.5**  None can prevent him, like the roar of the Maruts; like a hurled army, like the bolt of heaven — Agni devours and consumes with his sharp teeth; like a warrior driving back his foes, he overpowers the forests.

**1.143.6**  Will Agni be a hero worthy of our praise? Will the good one bring wealth and desires with his riches? Will the impeller speed our prayers to the winning? With this thought I praise him of bright face.

**1.143.7**  The one with ghee-bright face, seated in the pole of ṛta — enkindled, men approach Agni as they approach Mitra. The enkindled, active one blazing at assemblies — may he raise up our bright-hued thought.

**1.143.8**  O Agni, never faltering, with your unfaltering, gracious, effective guardians protect us. With your unerring, undeceived, unswerving, unwinking guards, protect our offspring all around.

**1.144.1**  The Hotā goes forward, carrying out the ordinance of these two enchanters, holding aloft the pure-formed thought. He strides toward the ladles, turning to the right — those that have from the beginning licked his station.

**1.144.2**  The milch-vessels of ṛta have sung along for him, in the divine abode, in the womb, enclosed around. When, nourished in the lap of the waters, he dwelt there — then the self-ordering one drank from those by which he moves.

**1.144.3**  The two of equal age, wishing to separate, share indeed that one form with a common purpose, yet excel each other mutually. Then, like Bhaga who is to be invoked, as a charioteer gathers the reins, he gathered himself together from us.

**1.144.4**  Him whom two equals together worship, dwelling in the same womb, a pair sharing one abode — neither by day nor by night does his grey old age befall him; moving through many human ages, he is ageless.

**1.144.5**  Ten sisters urge him forward; mortals call upon this god for help. From a slope of the bow he rushes with those who come clustering, assigned to new tracks.

**1.144.6**  You, O Agni, reign over the heavenly realm; you are like the guardian of cattle for the earthly realm by your own nature. These two great tawny ones, radiant with glory, golden and graceful, lie down upon the sacred grass for you.

**1.144.7**  O Agni, receive and love that speech, O joyful, self-nourishing, born of ṛta, wise one. You who face outward in all directions, visible, pleasant to behold, like a nourishing abode when seen.

**1.145.1**  Ask of him — he has gone, he knows; the discerning one moves, now he moves indeed. In him are the teachings, in him are the offerings; he is the lord of vigor, of strength, of power.

**1.145.2**  They ask of him; but he does not divide to ask — when the wise one grasps it as with his own mind. He does not misapprehend the first word, nor the latter; by his wisdom one attends on him, undespised.

**1.145.3**  To him go the ladles, to him go the oblation-bearers; may he alone hear all my words. With many impulses, swift, accomplishing sacrifice, the young one of unbroken help takes hold and grasps eagerly.

**1.145.4**  Coming near, he moves about, all ready; newborn, he goes at once with his companions to the goal. He touches the gladdened one with joy, for delight, when the eager ones approach him as he rests upon them.

**1.145.5**  That wild beast, of the waters, the forest-rover, has been set down close beside the topmost skin. Agni, knowing, truly finding ṛta, declared the paths to mortals — for he is indeed true.

**1.146.1**  I praise Agni of three heads and seven rays, undiminished, in the lap of his two parents. Seated, of one who moves yet is fixed, he has filled all the luminous realms of heaven.

**1.146.2**  The great bull has spread himself over these two, the ageless one stands mighty, with aid from the hither side. He sets the foot of the broad one on the summit; his red-glowing ones lick his udder.

**1.146.3**  Moving about together toward their common calf, the two well-built cows wander in different directions. Measuring out the paths without deviation, receiving all purposes upon the great one.

**1.146.4**  The wise seers lead to the station, guarding the indestructible one with varying hearts. Desiring to win, they looked around upon the stream; the sun became manifest to these men.

**1.146.5**  Visible in all quarters, worthy of hymning, praiseworthy, worthy of praise for great and small alike to live. When the sun arose for those of this day, the bountiful one became visible to all the embryos of the world.

**1.147.1**  How, O Agni, did the purifying ones of Āyu bestow their gifts on you, gaining swiftly with their prizes? When the gods, holding both offspring and progeny, made merry at the song of ṛta.

**1.147.2**  Hear this my word, O youngest, O self-nourishing, bountiful bearer of the most generous offering. One reviles you, another praises you in turn; I, a worshipper, worship your body, O Agni.

**1.147.3**  Those watchmen, O Agni, children of Māmateya, seeing the blind, protected him from wrongdoing. The all-knowing one who does good deeds has protected them; the foes who sought to harm them have not deceived them at all.

**1.147.4**  The enemy of ours, O Agni, the malevolent one, the niggard who would harm us with his deceit — may the weighty spell recoil upon him; may his body be smeared with ill words.

**1.147.5**  Or if some powerful mortal, knowing evil, harms another mortal with deceit — from that protect the one who praises you, who lauds you, O Agni; let nothing lead us toward wrongdoing.

**1.148.1**  When the pervading Mātariśvan churned forth the Hotā, rich in all forms, belonging to all gods — him whom they placed among the human tribes, shining with a wondrous, brilliant form like the sun.

**1.148.2**  Those who give — the thought does not harm them — Agni has chosen to be my shelter. May they delight in all his deeds, in the praise of the bard who bears the offering.

**1.148.3**  Him whom the worthy worshippers have grasped even in his own abode and established with praises — leading him forward, seizing him at the offering, like racing chariot-horses neighing in delight.

**1.148.4**  The wondrous one breaks down many things with his jaws; then he gleams, the all-brilliant one, in the forest. Then the wind blows behind his flame; like an archer shooting arrows, he sends it through the days.

**1.148.5**  The foes, the harming ones, do not injure him while he abides in the womb; the blind, unseeing ones cannot harm him by their glance. His own eternal guardians protect him as he goes forth.

**1.149.1**  The master goes seeking great wealth; he who gives, the powerful lord of the powerful — of riches, at the station — the stones serve only him who presses on.

**1.149.2**  He who is the bull, not of men, not of the two worlds — he who exists in fame, pouring forth a life-giving stream — who flows forward and clings to his birthplace.

**1.149.3**  He who has shone upon the unassailed stronghold — the swift, wise one, the cloud-born, not to be warded off — blazing like the sun, of hundredfold nature.

**1.149.4**  Agni of double birth, blazing, has stood over the three luminous realms, over all the spaces. He is the most effective Hotā, in the abode of the waters.

**1.149.5**  He it is, the Hotā, he of double birth, who bears all desirable things, worthy of praise. Happy is the mortal who generously gives to him.

**1.150.1**  O Agni, I the bountiful one would call you a stranger, a near kinsman. Like shelter in the greatness of one who goads.

**1.150.2**  Of the unniggardly, wealthy one — even among those going vigorously forward — even of those not honoring the gods — when at any time they depart.

**1.150.3**  He, the bright one, the inspired mortal of the great ones, most exuberant in heaven — may we, O Agni, be among the winners of your favor.

**1.151.1**  Him whom the diligent, who strive for cattle, have generated like Mitra among cows, of good understanding, in the assembly, in the waters — Heaven and Earth have trembled with his luster, with his song, in recognition of the dear, adorable one, the protection of creatures.

**1.151.2**  When indeed the self-sustaining worshippers, like Mitras, have placed in honor that gift of yours, O Mitra-Varuṇa, for the Soma-pressing, much-besought one — then, O bull-like ones rich in dwellings, find the purpose, find the path; sing also what is famed.

**1.151.3**  May the settlements adorn you, O bull-like ones — the birth of both Worlds is to be proclaimed for your great might. When you two carry it for Ṛta, when for the swift steed, when you lead the sacrifice forward with the priestly call, with the labor-song.

**1.151.4**  That settlement, O Asura, which is the dear and great one — you two, the Ṛta-rich ones, proclaim Ṛta aloud and mightily. You two harness the encompassing power of high Heaven and the waters like a cow to the yoke.

**1.151.5**  By your greatness you two open the great enclosure here; at the impelling place swiftly [come] dust-free milch-kine to the seat. They sound their note before the sun as it sets at dusk, like swift morning-birds at dawn.

**1.151.6**  For Ṛta, O Mitra-Varuṇa, the long-haired ones have sung toward you, where you two set the path in motion with song. Release the streams of your own accord, swell the insights — you two rule over the thought-songs of the inspired poet.

**1.151.7**  The inspired poet-Hotṛ who worships you both, laboring with sacrifices, offering devotedly, accomplishing his song-thought — come to him nearby, traverse the sacrifice toward him; come with your songs, come with your benevolence, eager for us.

**1.151.8**  You two, the Ṛta-rich ones, were first anointed by the sacrifices with cows, like the yoking of the mind's drives. The songs with their thought-weaving, restrained, carry to you — with unhaughty mind you two partake of abundance.

**1.151.9**  You two bestow rich vigor, you two partake richly, O manly ones, guarding the great one with your powers through help. Neither the days, nor the rivers, nor the Paṇis have attained to your divine state, to your bounty.

**1.152.1**  You two clothe yourselves in rich garments; your counsels and your torrents are indeed without flaw. You have overcome all falsehoods — Mitra and Varuṇa, you follow Ṛta.

**1.152.2**  Even this their wise one does not discern — the true, poet-praised hymn strikes with rage. The fierce four-cornered one smites the triangular; the blasphemers of the gods have indeed perished from the very first.

**1.152.3**  The footless one goes foremost of the footed — who among you, O Mitra-Varuṇa, perceives that? The embryo bears a burden even of this one; Ṛta rescues, untruth causes to fall.

**1.152.4**  We see the lover of virgins going forth, not coming near. Spread open, the beloved abode of Mitra and Varuṇa is clothed in never-shrinking garment.

**1.152.5**  Born without a horse, without a bridle, the strong one neighing flies upward on the summit. The young men have rejoiced in the unconscious sacred word, praising aloud in Mitra's domain, in Varuṇa's.

**1.152.6**  The milch-kine coming forward, those of Māmateya helping, have swollen in this same udder, dear to the sacred word. Let the knowing one beg sustenance, having learned the laws; winning over Aditi as if pressing Soma, let him preserve her.

**1.152.7**  I would turn toward you, O Mitra-Varuṇa, with homage and with protection, O gods, the acceptable oblation. May our sacred formula be victorious in battles; may our rain from heaven be easily fordable.

**1.153.1**  We worship you two together in accord, with great oblations, O Mitra-Varuṇa, with reverences, with butter-offerings, O drippers of ghee — when the adhvaryus carry to us, to you, with their mental labors.

**1.153.2**  The praise is your seat, not mere deployment — I have sent to you, O Mitra-Varuṇa, the fine hymn of praise. When the Hotṛ anoints you at the assemblies, the inspired patron wishes to sacrifice to your benevolence, O bull-like ones.

**1.153.3**  Aditi the cow has given milk for Ṛta, for the people, O Mitra-Varuṇa, O givers of oblation. When the one who honors you impels you at the assembly — that man whose offering is given is like a human Hotṛ.

**1.153.4**  And among the clans the inebriating Soma-plants, the cows, and the divine waters have swelled for you. And the ancient lord, granting this to us — drink deeply of the milk of the ruddy cow.

**1.154.1**  Now I shall proclaim the heroic deeds of Viṣṇu, who measured out the earthly realms, who propped up the higher station, striding in three steps — he of wide-going, praised on every side.

**1.154.2**  Viṣṇu proclaims this through his heroic might — a terrible wild beast who roams the mountains. In his three wide strides all beings dwell.

**1.154.3**  Let the praise-song go forth to Viṣṇu, the mountain-dweller, widely praised, the bull — who alone measured out this long, stretched-forth station with just three steps.

**1.154.4**  His three steps are full of honey, inexhaustible, rejoicing through their own inherent power; who alone supports the triple-natured earth and heaven and all beings.

**1.154.5**  I would reach that dear station of his, where the god-devoted men exult — for that is truly the companion of the wide-strider: the fount of honey in Viṣṇu's highest step.

**1.154.6**  We desire to go to those homesteads of yours where the many-horned, untiring cows are. Indeed there that highest step of the widely-praised bull shines down abundantly.

**1.155.1**  Your drink of the Soma-plant is set forth for him who thinks — sing also for the great heroic Viṣṇu, you two who stand undeceived on the mountain ridges in your greatness, like a swift horse on the right path.

**1.155.2**  Truly glorious is the encounter of the two vigorous ones — Indra and Viṣṇu; the Soma-drinker guards you both. You two who protect for the mortal what is being aimed at — you ward off the swift arrow of the archer.

**1.155.3**  They two increase greatly his manly power; the Mother leads him down to the enjoyment of the seed. The son places the lower and the higher, the father's third name upon the shining realm of heaven.

**1.155.4**  That and that manly deed of his we sing — of the sitting protector, wolf-free, the bountiful one, who strode widely on the earthly realms with just three strides, for the widely-praised one, for life.

**1.155.5**  His two steps visible to mortals — the mortal supports himself looking at them. Not even the flying birds can dare to reach his third step.

**1.155.6**  Together with four and ninety names he shook out the interweavings like a wheel set in motion. Of great body, measuring with his praise-songs, the ever-youthful one comes forward to meet the call.

**1.156.1**  Be you like Mitra, dear and blessed, with the pour of ghee, O abundantly-bright one, you who spread forward in the right course. Then, O Viṣṇu, even for the knower the praise-song is worthy, and the sacrifice is to be accomplished by the oblation-bearer.

**1.156.2**  He who offers to the ancient, ever-renewing, wise Viṣṇu with good knee-bending — he who proclaims the great birth of the great one, he shall indeed be united with glories, even with the noble.

**1.156.3**  Let the praisers fill him up in accordance with ancient knowledge, the womb of Ṛta by birth. Knowing even his name, speak it out — of your greatness, O Viṣṇu, we partake of your benevolence.

**1.156.4**  King Varuṇa follows that purpose of his, and the Aśvins follow the wisdom of the Marut-wise one. Dakṣa, the highest, knows the days; Viṣṇu, as companion, opens the cow-pen.

**1.156.5**  He who, most well-doing among the well-doing, has yoked Viṣṇu for companionship with divine Indra — the wise one of three stations has sped the Ārya-man's noble work; in the share of Ṛta he has caused the sacrificer to partake.

**1.157.1**  Agni has awoken; the sun rises from the earth; the bright dawn has greatly helped with her flame. The Aśvins have yoked their chariot to travel; the divine Savitṛ has impelled each living thing apart.

**1.157.2**  When you two, O Aśvins, yoke your bull-like chariot, sprinkle our sovereignty with ghee, with honey. Invigorate our sacred word in battles — may we partake of riches in the winning of the heroic.

**1.157.3**  Let the three-wheeled, honey-carrying chariot of the Aśvins come hither well praised, with swift horses. The bountiful one with three seats, with all good fortune — may it bring blessing to us, to the biped and the quadruped.

**1.157.4**  Bring us nourishment here, O Aśvins, you two; sprinkle us with your honeyed whip. Carry our lifespan safely across, wipe away harm, repel hatred — be you helpers who accompany us.

**1.157.5**  You two place the embryo in creatures, you two are within all the worlds. You two, O Aśvins, O bull-like ones, have set in motion Agni and the waters and the forest-lords.

**1.157.6**  You two are indeed physicians with medicines; and moreover you are chariot-lords with charioteers. Moreover, O fierce ones, you bestow sovereignty upon him who offers you oblation devotedly in his mind.

**1.158.1**  O Vasus, O Rudras, of many counsels, growing mighty — O bull-like ones, O wondrous ones, honor us in the conquest. For the wealth of Aucathya is indeed yours when you two have flowed forth with your never-failing aids.

**1.158.2**  Who shall offer to you for even this your benevolence, O Vasus, when you two sit with homage at the cow's footprint? Seize for us rich, understanding maidens — moving like one impelled by desire in his mind.

**1.158.3**  For indeed when Tugra's son Bhujyu was yoked to you, he was placed amid the ocean's midst — let me come to your shelter, O Aśvins; like a hero not through the currents in battle-courses.

**1.158.4**  May Upastuta preserve the Aucathyan — may these two winged ones not milk me dry. May the tenfold-piled kindling not burn me, for I am bound in myself gnawing the earth when your bound one gnaws the ground.

**1.158.5**  The rivers most motherly have not swallowed me; the Dāsa slaves did not shake the tightly bound one loose. When Traitana cut off his head, the Dāsa himself gnawed into his own chest and shoulders.

**1.158.6**  Dīrghatamas, son of Māmata, grown old in the tenth age — the Brahman becomes the charioteer of the purpose of waters, of those who strive.

**1.159.1**  With sacrifices I laud the great Dyāvāpṛthivī — Heaven and Earth, who augment Ṛta, O wise ones, at the assemblies. Those children of gods, those with divine offspring, with wondrous deeds — they adorn what is desirable with such intent.

**1.159.2**  I regard the mind of the blameless Father as great, of the self-powerful Mother — with their libations; the two parents of good seed have established for progeny the broad immortal domain with their choosing gifts.

**1.159.3**  Their sons, of good deeds, of wondrous power, have generated the two great Mothers for primordial perception. The path of the son, undivided in mind, is in the truth and ordinance of the standing and the moving.

**1.159.4**  They, the wise ones of good insight, the skillful, have measured out the two kinswomen of one womb, the twins who share one dwelling. They weave ever anew the thread in heaven, within the ocean — the poets of bright flame.

**1.159.5**  That excellent bounty of Savitṛ divine we contemplate today at the god's impulsion. O Dyāvāpṛthivī, with clear insight bestow on us riches abounding in wealth, a hundredfold in cattle.

**1.160.1**  For Dyāvāpṛthivī, they two — benevolent to all, Ṛta-rich, the two wise ones who hold the realm — between the two fair-born, auspicious ones the pure Sun god and goddess move in accordance with the ordinance.

**1.160.2**  Of wide expanse, the two great inseparable ones — Father and Mother guard the worlds. The boldest among the bold, the two Worlds, as if for ornament — when the Father clothed her with his forms.

**1.160.3**  He, the bearer, the son of the two parents, pure, purifies the worlds wisely with his power. The dappled cow and the bull of good seed have on all days milked his shining milk.

**1.160.4**  He is the most active of the active gods, who generated the two Worlds benevolent to all. He who measured out the two realms with his well-ordered wisdom has fitted them together with his never-aging props.

**1.160.5**  O Dyāvāpṛthivī, you two great ones, when praised, shall bestow great fame, vast sovereignty. That wondrous strength with which we have spread ourselves over the peoples on all days — pour that power abundantly into us.

**1.161.1**  What greatest, what youngest came to us? What embassy is sent — what did we say? We do not revile the cup of great lineage; O Agni, brother, we declared prosperity only in the wooden vessel.

**1.161.2**  Make the one cup into four — so the gods said to you, and so I came to you. O sons of Sudhanvan, if you do this, you shall become worthy of sacrifice together with the gods.

**1.161.3**  When you sent word back to Agni as your messenger: 'A horse is to be fashioned, and a chariot too is to be fashioned here; a cow is to be fashioned, two young animals are to be fashioned' — for those deeds, O brother, we come following after you.

**1.161.4**  Having accomplished that, the Ṛbhus inquired: 'Where then has he gone who came to us as messenger?' When he had reported that the four cups were fashioned, Tvaṣṭṛ straightway hid himself among the women.

**1.161.5**  When Tvaṣṭṛ said 'Let us strike them down' — those who found no fault with the cup for the gods' drinking — they give them other names at the pressing; a young woman contended with them using other names.

**1.161.6**  Indra yoked his two bay horses; the Aśvins took possession of a chariot of all forms; Ṛbhu, Vibhvan, and Vāja went to the gods. You who are skilled workers came to a sacrificial share.

**1.161.7**  With your thoughts you released the cow from her hide; you who made two old ones young again — O sons of Sudhanvan, from the horse you fashioned a horse; having yoked a chariot you drove up to the gods.

**1.161.8**  'Drink this water' — so they said to you — 'or drink this water used for washing the muñja-grass.' O sons of Sudhanvan, if you do not desire that, then be made glad at the third pressing.

**1.161.9**  One said: 'Water is the greatest'; another said: 'Fire is the greatest'; one spoke out to the many: 'She who hurls the weapon' — speaking truthfully, they fashioned the cups.

**1.161.10**  One drives water to the lame cow; one shapes meat brought by the slaughter-knife; one carried dung up from the valley at sunset. What indeed did the two parents bestow upon their sons?

**1.161.11**  In the upland pastures, O men of skill, you made grass for him; in the lower pastures, water. That which you fashioned in the house of Agohya who cannot be hidden — that today, O Ṛbhus, you do not follow after.

**1.161.12**  When you crept around through the worlds lying still — where indeed did your two ancient parents sit? He who took away the stump of your hand cursed you; who spoke out to him? Tell him that.

**1.161.13**  The Ṛbhus who had been sleeping then asked: 'O Agohya, who has awakened us?' A goat said to a dog, its awakener: 'The year — today it has looked upon this thing.'

**1.161.14**  The Maruts travel by day, Agni by earth, this wind travels through the atmosphere; Varuṇa travels with the waters, with the seas — the sons of Strength, seeking you.

**1.162.1**  May Mitra, Varuṇa, Aryaman, Āyu, Indra Ṛbhukṣan, and the Maruts not look askance at us, when we proclaim at the assembly the heroic deeds of the divine-born swift horse.

**1.162.2**  When they lead forward the gift of the one clothed in ornamental garment with golden trappings, seized by the mouth — the well-formed goat bleats, of all forms, and attains the dear portion of Indra and Pūṣan.

**1.162.3**  This goat, led forward before the swift horse, is the share of Pūṣan to be offered to all the gods. Tvaṣṭṛ quickens this one along with the horse toward the dear fore-offering cake, for the sake of good fame.

**1.162.4**  When men lead the horse around thrice in order at the proper season on the god-directed path of the oblation, Pūṣan's first share comes here, as the goat announces the sacrifice to the gods.

**1.162.5**  The hotr̥, the adhvaryu, the āvayā, the fire-kindler, the stone-presser, and also the eloquent reciter — fill the flanks through that sacrifice, well-made with its own proper sound, properly offered.

**1.162.6**  Those who cut the sacrificial post and those who carry the post, and those who carve the ring for the horse's post, and those who bring together the cooking for the horse — may our praise song also stir them.

**1.162.7**  He came forward, one who has found my hymn good was placed — with back bared — meeting the expectation of the gods; the inspired seer-poets rejoice after him. In the flourishing of the gods we have made a close friend.

**1.162.8**  The tethering rope, the binding cord of the swift horse, the head-rope, the halter-cord — and whatever bundle of grass has been placed in his mouth — let all that be yours among the gods.

**1.162.9**  Whatever flies have settled on the raw flesh of the horse, whatever clings to the axe or to the cleaver, whatever is on the hands of the slaughterer, whatever on his nails — let all that be yours among the gods.

**1.162.10**  Whatever flows out from the belly as excrement, whatever is the smell of the raw flesh — let the slaughterers make that good; let them cook the sacrificial flesh to proper ripeness.

**1.162.11**  Whatever of your body, roasted by fire, drips down upon the spit of the slain one — may that not cling to the earth, may it not cling to the grass; let it be offered to the gods who desire it.

**1.162.12**  Those who watch over the cooked horse, those who say 'carry it out, it is fragrant,' and those who await the distribution of the horse's meat — may our praise also stir them.

**1.162.13**  The hook for the flesh-cooking vessel, the vessels for pouring the broth, the steam-lids of the cauldrons, the hooks — the butchers adorn the horse with all these.

**1.162.14**  The stepping forth, the sitting down, the rolling over, and the hobble of the horse, and whatever he drank, and whatever grass he ate — let all that be yours among the gods.

**1.162.15**  Let not the fire that blazes brightly singe you while you come near to your dear self; let not the axe remain in your body. The gods receive that horse who has been offered, presented, praised, and consecrated with the vaṣaṭ-call.

**1.162.16**  The garment they spread for the horse as a covering, the overgarment, the golden trappings for him — they cause the beloved, tethered, hobbled horse to proceed to the gods.

**1.162.17**  Whatever struck you in your seat of pride with the heel or with the whip — all those as if the sacrificial ladle at offerings I set aright for you with the sacred word.

**1.162.18**  Thirty-four ribs of the horse belonging to the gods — the axe comes together with them. Keep the limbs uncut with skill; cut them apart joint by joint, calling out to each.

**1.162.19**  One is the carver of Tvaṣṭṛ's horse; two are the holders — such is the rule. Whatever portions I make in their proper order from your limbs, those portions one by one I offer into the fire.

**1.162.20**  Let not the fire singe you as you draw near to your dear self; let not the axe remain fixed in your body; let not the greedy unskilled cutter, making a mistake, mar your limbs with the knife.

**1.162.21**  You do not truly die here, nor are you harmed — you go to the gods by easy paths. The two bays become your yoke-mates, the dappled mares; the swift horse has stood beside the shaft of the donkey.

**1.162.22**  May the swift horse bring us good cattle and good horses, male offspring, and wealth nourishing all; may Aditi make us guiltless; may the horse bearing the oblation win lordship for us.

**1.163.1**  When you cried out first, being born, rising from the ocean or from the earthy matter — the wings of an eagle, the arms of a deer — great is your birth, O swift horse, worthy of praise.

**1.163.2**  Given by Yama, Trita harnessed him; Indra was the first to mount him; the Gandharva grasped his rein. From the sun you fashioned the horse, O Vasus.

**1.163.3**  You are Yama, you are Āditya, O swift horse; you are Trita by your secret ordinance; you are separated from Soma in like manner. They say you have three bonds in heaven.

**1.163.4**  They say you have three bonds in heaven, three in the waters, three within the ocean. To me you seem like Varuṇa, O swift horse, they say your highest birthplace is there.

**1.163.5**  These are your cleansing instruments, O vāji; these are the receptacles for the securing of your hooves. Here I saw your good reins, the guardians who protect the cosmic order.

**1.163.6**  I recognized your self from afar with my mind — a bird flying downward from the sky by day. I saw the head, the winged one straining along easy, dustless paths.

**1.163.7**  Here I saw your highest form, striving to win, at the place of the cow's refreshment. When a mortal reached up for your enjoyment, then you seized on the herbs and quickened them.

**1.163.8**  After you came the chariot, after you the warrior, O swift horse; after you the cattle, after you the luck of maidens; after you your bands of companions went to your friendship; after you the gods measured out your heroic power.

**1.163.9**  Golden-horned, his feet of iron, swift as thought, lower than Indra — the gods came to his oblation-eating, he who first mounted the swift horse.

**1.163.10**  Sweating, with wasp-like waists, the valiant, heavenly swift horses — they strive in line like swans when they have taken the heavenly course.

**1.163.11**  Your body is eager to fly, O swift horse; your mind rushes along like the wind; your horns scattered in many places wander roaming in the forests.

**1.163.12**  The swift horse came forward to the slaughter, meditating with god-directed mind. The goat led in front is his navel-cord; behind, the inspired singers go after.

**1.163.13**  The swift horse came forward to the highest resting place, toward father and mother. Today he is most pleasing to the gods to go to; then he shall declare desirable gifts to the devout offerer.

**1.164.1**  Of this beautiful gray hotr̥, his middle brother is the devourer; the third brother, ghee-backed, is this one's. There I saw the lord of the settlement with seven sons.

**1.164.2**  Seven yoke the single-wheeled chariot; one horse with seven names draws it. The wheel is three-hubbed, imperishable, not worn out — on it all these worlds stand.

**1.164.3**  The seven who stand on this seven-wheeled chariot — seven horses draw it. Seven sisters call out together, where the seven names of the cattle are set down.

**1.164.4**  Who saw the one being born first, the one with bones, which the boneless one bears? The earth's vital breath, blood, and soul — where are they? Who has gone to the wise one to ask this?

**1.164.5**  Childlike I ask, not knowing in my mind — what are the hidden tracks of the gods there? The poets have stretched out, as if for weaving, seven threads upon the young calf — so they say.

**1.164.6**  Not knowing, even among the knowing, I ask the poet-seers here for the sake of knowledge, I who do not know — what is the one thing that in the form of the unborn has propped up these six spaces apart?

**1.164.7**  Let him speak here who truly knows the hidden track of this lovely bird. The cows milk milk from his head; clothed in wrappings they have drunk water with their feet.

**1.164.8**  The mother apportioned the father through the cosmic order; at the beginning of thought she met him with her mind. She, full of desire, with embryo-essence laid in her, the reverential ones went to the invocation.

**1.164.9**  The mother was yoked to the right-hand shaft; the embryo stood within the enclosures. The calf lowed; it saw the cow in pursuit of all-encompassing forms in the three yokings.

**1.164.10**  Bearing three mothers and three fathers, the one stood upright — they do not weaken it below. On the back of that heaven they confer, speaking the all-knowing speech that not all know.

**1.164.11**  The twelve-spoked wheel of the cosmic order rolls round the sky and does not decay — on it, O Agni, stand paired children, seven hundred and twenty.

**1.164.12**  The five-footed father with twelve forms they call the bountiful one in the upper half of the sky. But others here say of the far-seer standing below that he is set upon a seven-wheeled, six-spoked wheel.

**1.164.13**  On the five-spoked wheel revolving all the worlds have taken their stand. Its axle is not heated though it bears a great load. From the ancient time the hub does not break.

**1.164.14**  The wheel with complete rim, ageless, revolves; ten yoked ones draw it on the outstretched path. The sun's eye moves enveloped in the mist; on it all worlds are set.

**1.164.15**  The seventh of the simultaneous-born they call the only-born; six are the twin-born seers, born of the gods they say. Their desires are laid out in the habitations; they who stand firm vibrate in changing forms.

**1.164.16**  They are females, yet the knowing ones call them males — one who has eyes and looks sees it; the blind one does not discern it. The son who is a seer has understood them; he who knows them would be the father of his father.

**1.164.17**  Below the upper and above the lower, the cow has stood up bearing the calf on her feet. Toward which half has she gone away? Where does she give birth? Surely not within the herd.

**1.164.18**  Whoever knows the father — above the lower and below the upper — who here acting as a seer might proclaim the divine mind from whence it was born?

**1.164.19**  Those who face this way they call those who face away; those who face away they call those who face this way. Indra and Soma — those things you two have made, yoked to the shaft, carry the atmospheric space.

**1.164.20**  Two eagles of beautiful wings, companions and friends, clasp the same tree. One of the two eats the sweet fig; the other, not eating, looks on.

**1.164.21**  Where the eagles unblinkingly chant their portion of immortality in the assembly — the master, the guardian of the whole world, the wise one — has entered the simple one there.

**1.164.22**  On the tree where the honey-eating eagles make their home and give birth, the whole world is above — they say the sweet fig is at the top; he will not reach it who does not know the Father.

**1.164.23**  What is set in the gāyatrī beyond the gāyatrī, what they fashioned in the triṣṭubh beyond the triṣṭubh, what is the step set in the jagatī beyond the jagatī — those who know that have attained immortality.

**1.164.24**  With the gāyatrī he measures out the sacred song; with the song, the sāman; with the triṣṭubh, the vāk. With speech he measures speech, two-footed and four-footed; with the syllable they measure the seven vāṇīs.

**1.164.25**  With the jagatī he propped up the river in the sky; in the rathantara he beheld the sun round about. They say the gāyatra has three fuel-sticks; from that it has surpassed all in greatness by its greatness.

**1.164.26**  I call hither this good-milking cow; a skillful hand should milk her and urge her on. May Savitṛ impel the best impulsion for us; the heated cauldron ablaze — that I now declare.

**1.164.27**  The cow, lady of treasures, making the hiṅ sound, came in her mind seeking the calf. Let this inviolable one be milked for the two Aśvins; may she thrive for great good fortune.

**1.164.28**  The cow lowed after the calf gazing at her; she made the hiṅ sound at the head, in her maternal desire. Lowing for the seething pot with parted lips, she measures out her might with streams of milk.

**1.164.29**  This is the one that bellows, by whom the cow surrounded lows its call, resting on the two nostrils. She by her powers has laid the mortal low; becoming lightning she has pushed aside the covering.

**1.164.30**  The breathing one lies down; the fleet-moving living one stirs about the firm one in the middle of the settlements. The living one wanders among the dead by its own powers — the immortal one, of the same womb as the mortal.

**1.164.31**  I saw the herdsman who does not dismount, going along the paths hither and thither. He, clothing himself in converging and diverging forms, moves round and round within the worlds.

**1.164.32**  He who made it does not know it; he who saw it — from him it conceals itself. Enclosed within his mother's womb, he who has many offspring has entered into dissolution.

**1.164.33**  Heaven is my father, my begetter; the navel is here; this great earth is my mother, my kin. Between the two spread bowls is the womb; here the father placed the embryo in the daughter.

**1.164.34**  I ask you about the furthest end of the earth; I ask where is the navel of the world; I ask you about the seed of the vigorous horse; I ask about the highest heaven of speech.

**1.164.35**  This altar-ground is the furthest end of the earth; this sacrifice is the navel of the world; this Soma is the seed of the vigorous horse; this brahman priest is the highest heaven of speech.

**1.164.36**  The seven half-embryos, the seed of the world, stand by the ordinances in the domains of Viṣṇu's far-spreading. Those wise and thoughtful ones, encompassing beings, surround everything on all sides.

**1.164.37**  I do not know what this thing is that I am — bound in mystery, I wander with my mind. When the firstborn of the cosmic order came to me, then I attained a share of this speech.

**1.164.38**  Going backward and forward, seized by his own power, the immortal of the same womb as the mortal — the two, moving perpetually apart in different directions, have perceived the one, have not perceived the other.

**1.164.39**  In the imperishable highest heaven in which all the gods have taken their seat — what will he do with the ṛc who does not know that? Only those who know it are seated here together.

**1.164.40**  May you be blessed with good pasture; may we too be blessed. Eat grass, O inviolable one, ever and always; drink pure water as you go about.

**1.164.41**  The shining cow shaped waters, crafting them — she who is one-footed, two-footed, four-footed; she who has become eight-footed, nine-footed, possessing a thousand syllables in the highest heaven.

**1.164.42**  From her the seas flow forth in all directions; by her the four quarters live. From her flows the syllable, on that the whole world lives.

**1.164.43**  From afar I saw a dung-smoky cloud rising at the equinox, above the lower and below the upper. The heroes cooked the dappled bull; these were the first ordinances.

**1.164.44**  Three long-haired ones reveal themselves in order at the proper season; one of them shaves at the year's end; one gazes over the whole world with his powers; of one the rushing is seen but not the form.

**1.164.45**  Speech is measured out in four quarters; the wise Brāhmaṇas know them. Three are placed in secret and do not stir; of speech, humans speak only the fourth.

**1.164.46**  They call it Indra, Mitra, Varuṇa, Agni, and it is the divine, winged Garutmān. The one being the seers speak of in many ways: they call it Agni, Yama, Mātariśvan.

**1.164.47**  Golden-hued eagles, wearing the waters, fly upward to the black track in the sky. They have returned from the seat of the cosmic order; then the earth is spread out with ghee.

**1.164.48**  Twelve fellies, one wheel, three hubs — who has understood that? On it are set together three hundred pins and sixty, and they do not budge.

**1.164.49**  O Sarasvatī, that breast of yours which is inexhaustible, which bestows well-being, with which you nourish all desirable things — the treasure-bearing, wealth-finding, generous one — bring that here for us to drink.

**1.164.50**  The gods worshipped sacrifice with sacrifice; those were the first ordinances. These great ones have attained the vault of heaven where the ancient Sādhya gods are.

**1.164.51**  This same water rises up and comes back down by days; the rains quicken the earth; the fires quicken the sky.

**1.164.52**  The heavenly eagle, the great bird of the sky, the embryo of the waters, the shining one of herbs — Sarasvan who refreshes those who desire him with his rains — I call him with repeated calls for help.

**1.165.1**  By what splendor have the Maruts, of equal age, of the same nest, of the same band, mingled together? By what thought, from where have these come — the vigorous ones who sing their strength, desiring riches?

**1.165.2**  Whose sacred formulations have the young ones delighted in? Who has turned the Maruts to the sacrifice? Like swift hawks through the atmosphere — by what great thought shall we find pleasure together?

**1.165.3**  O Indra, being great, why do you go alone, O lord of the good? What then is your purpose? Meeting together equally with the splendid ones — declare to us what you have for us, O one with the bay horses.

**1.165.4**  My sacred formulations and thoughts are potent; the pressed stone sets my strength in motion. The recitations summon, the hymns come back in answer — let these two bay horses carry you here to us.

**1.165.5**  For this reason we, joined with our nearest companions, adorning our bodies with our own domains, now yoke these with our great powers. O Indra, you have indeed become for us according to our own nature.

**1.165.6**  Where was your solidarity, O Maruts, when you left me alone in the serpent-slaying? For I am the fierce, mighty, powerful one; I have crushed every foe with my weapons.

**1.165.7**  You have done much for us with our companions, O bull, with the manly powers shared among equals. We shall do many things, O strongest Indra, with resolve, as the Maruts desire.
