**1.165.8**  I slew Vṛtra, O Maruts, with Indra's power, becoming mighty with my own fury. I the one of bright-all splendors, the thunderbolt-armed, made the waters flow freely for Manu.

**1.165.9**  Your power is unsurpassed, O Maghavan; none now knows a deity like you. Neither one being born can attain you nor one already born; what you will do — do that, O most exalted one.

**1.165.10**  Let my power be preeminent even alone; what I shall do, I will do with daring thought. For I am the fierce one who knows, O Maruts — of those things I set moving, Indra alone is master.

**1.165.11**  The hymn of praise has gladdened me here, O Maruts, that the men made the sacred word worthy of hearing — for me Indra, the bull, the exceedingly good, as friends with their bodies in friendship to my body.

**1.165.12**  Thus these very ones, shining brightly toward me, bearing blameless glory — seeing me, O Maruts, of resplendent forms, shine for me and now give me delight.

**1.165.13**  Who among these praises you, O Maruts? Go forth toward your friends, O friends. Setting in motion their varied formulations — be for me a knower of the cosmic laws of these.

**1.165.14**  As a poet serves at the call of one who deserves service, the wisdom of the one worthy of honor has made us its own. O Maruts, turn now toward the inspired one; the singer has praised your sacred formulations.

**1.165.15**  This is your praise-hymn, O Maruts, this is the song of the poet Māndārya, worthy of honor. May this go forth for the body's nourishment; may we find this settled community that gives life swiftly.

**1.166.1**  Let us now declare for your impetuous birth the former greatness of the bull, for his banner to be known — even as flames in your course, O Maruts of mighty roar, so, O mighty ones, do you perform powerful deeds as if in battle.

**1.166.2**  As if carrying the perpetual honey for a son, they play their games, these spirited ones, in the assemblies. The Rudras reach with their help the devout worshipper; the self-mighty ones do not neglect the sacrificial oblation prepared for them.

**1.166.3**  To him for whom the immortal helpers have granted wealth-abundance, to him who has worshipped with oblation — the Maruts sprinkle for him, as if appointed, the many realms with milk that gives delight.

**1.166.4**  They who filled the realms with their mighty powers — your swift steeds, self-harnessed, rushed forward. All the earthly beings tremble in fear; your march is radiant when your spears are hurled.

**1.166.5**  When in your impetuous course you make the mountains resound and the manly ones have shaken the back of heaven — every forest tree trembles at your onslaught, and the herbs dart forth as if galloping in a chariot-race.

**1.166.6**  Do you, O fierce Maruts, of clear discernment, whose village is unharmed, grant us your favour. Where your lightning gnaws with its sharp teeth, it bursts open the pen of cattle as if with a well-aimed axe.

**1.166.7**  The lavish givers, of unfailing generosity, never diminishing, rightly praised in assemblies — they sing the hymn to drink the exhilarating draught; they have known the foremost manly deeds of the hero.

**1.166.8**  O Maruts, protect with your hundredfold arms from violence and misdeed the one whom you have favoured; the one whom the fierce, mighty, and mighty-bodied ones guard in assemblies from false speech, in the nourishment of his offspring.

**1.166.9**  All auspicious and mighty things are laid upon your chariots, O Maruts, as if in mutual rivalry. On your shoulders are the bracelets along the ridges, and at once your axle and wheels have turned together.

**1.166.10**  Many auspicious ornaments on their manly arms, golden breastplates on their chests, the impetuous ones anointed — on their shoulders these decorations, razor-blades on their wheels; they have spread their glories like birds spreading their wings.

**1.166.11**  Great in greatness, all-pervading, of all-pervading splendours, seen afar as the heavenly stars — pleasant-voiced, with good tongues, resounding with their lips, the Maruts mingle together with Indra in their surrounding praise.

**1.166.12**  That greatness of yours, O well-born Maruts, your generous gift is long as the vow of Aditi; even Indra does not deflect that with violence for the man to whom, for his righteous deed, you have made it available.

**1.166.13**  That brotherhood of yours, O Maruts, in former ages — you immortals aided many a praiser. With this very prayer, wrapping obedience for Manu, the heroes have shone forth together with wondrous deeds.

**1.166.14**  By which, O Maruts, we have long grown strong, the swiftly advancing ones, through your abundance — when the people have spread out in the settlement by these sacrifices — may I attain that desired goal.

**1.166.15**  This is your praise-song, O Maruts, this is the hymn of Māndārya, the worthy poet. May it go forth for his body as vigour — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.167.1**  A thousand of your helps be ours, O Indra; a thousand refreshments, O bay-horsed one, most welcome of all! A thousand of riches to rejoice in — may thousandfold prizes come to us.

**1.167.2**  May the Maruts come towards us with their aids, or with their great-shining eldest ones, wise in their ways — even when their most distant teams rush thundering on the far shore of the ocean.

**1.167.3**  Among them has come the well-placed ghee-streaming one, golden-robed, like an uplifted spear — moving in secret like a woman of Manu, the speech comes together in the assembly like one meant for an assembly.

**1.167.4**  The bright, impetuous ones have poured out far and wide as if sharing a common path, O Maruts. The terrible ones did not drive away the two worlds; the gods joyfully embraced their growth in friendship.

**1.167.5**  When the mighty lady of sovereignty desired to join them, with hair dishevelled, the two worlds taking delight in heroes — she came like the sun going to the worshipper's chariot, with a blazing countenance, not to be led back from the sky.

**1.167.6**  The young ones have caused the maiden to stand, the Pajra woman veiled for beauty in the assemblies — when the oblation-bearer, the singer of the Pajra clan, sings a song for you, O Maruts, with pressed soma, honouring you.

**1.167.7**  I declare him worthy of speech whose true glory of these Maruts exists — when he, the bull-spirited one, self-glorious, bears along even steady women, the fortunate ones, in his company.

**1.167.8**  Mitra and Varuṇa protect from reproach; Aryaman keeps down the unworthy. And even the immovable, fixed things are shaken; the Maruts, lavish givers, have grown mightily.

**1.167.9**  Indeed, O Maruts, none among us, even from afar, has reached the end of your might. They who have grown great with bold strength stand around hatred as around a flood, boldly.

**1.167.10**  Today we would proclaim ourselves the dearest of Indra; tomorrow in the assembly we would say the same. May that greatness of ours be with us through all the days; may the Ṛbhukṣan, lord of men, follow us therein.

**1.167.11**  This is your praise-song, O Maruts, this is the hymn of Māndārya, the worthy poet. May it go forth for his body as vigour — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.168.1**  At each and every sacrifice, hastening to your common gathering, the god-seeking one lays down thought after thought for you. I would turn you hither-ward for our welfare, with beautiful verses, for the help of the two worlds, for great protection.

**1.168.2**  Like covered ones of their own kind, the self-mighty, shakers, have won refreshment and the sun. Thousandfold like waves of the waters with their mouths — cows worthy of reverence, and bulls.

**1.168.3**  Like pressed soma juices, they whose limbs are satisfied, who have drunk to the heart, who serve — they do not sit idle. On their shoulders, as if clasping, is fixed the decoration; in their hands, the bracelet and the sword are joined together.

**1.168.4**  The immortals, self-harnessed, have gone down freely from heaven, urged onward by a whip of their own accord. Dustless, born in might, the Maruts of flashing spears have shaken even what is firm and fixed.

**1.168.5**  Who trembles of himself among you, O Maruts of lightning spears, as if with a tongue in the jaw? Swept from the desert in the rush of the courses, the never-sleeping Naitaśa calls on many to advance.

**1.168.6**  Where is the farther limit of this great realm, and where the nearer, O Maruts, in which you go about? When you set tottering what seemed fixed together and fly apart, cleaving the terrible ocean with your stone.

**1.168.7**  Your winning is filled with violence and solar light, O Maruts, blazing and all-ripening, swelling. Your bounty to the worshipper is auspicious as a sacrificial gift, spreading wide its way, crackling as if with sovereign power.

**1.168.8**  The rivers resound back from their wheel-rims when you raise the cloud-born voice. The lightnings smile downward upon the earth whenever the Maruts sprinkle their ghee.

**1.168.9**  Pṛśni bore for the great battle the blazing host of the impetuous Maruts. They, of fair appearance, brought forth the monstrous thing; and then they looked around upon their vigorous power.

**1.168.10**  This is your praise-song, O Maruts, this is the hymn of Māndārya, the worthy poet. May it go forth for his body as vigour — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.169.1**  Great as you are, O Indra, when you go to these great ones — great too are you as the protector from their abandonment. Knowing the Maruts, O wise one, win for us your blessings; they are indeed your most beloved.

**1.169.2**  They have yoked your teams, O Indra, among all peoples, knowing the hindrance among mortals. The battle-cry of the Maruts is laughing out in the contest for the winning of the heavenly, bountiful prize of battle.

**1.169.3**  Straight toward us is that spear of yours, O Indra; the Maruts urge the monstrous one onward unwaveringly. For Agni, indeed, blazing for him, lay their offerings like waters around an island.

**1.169.4**  But do you, O Indra, give us that wealth as a gift with your most powerful right hand. And those that have taken delight in being praised by you have nourished prizes like the honey-sweet breast of Vāyu.

**1.169.5**  In you, O Indra, the most enriching leaders of any righteous man reside. May those Maruts be gracious to us — those gods who of old moved as if seeking a path.

**1.169.6**  Go forward to meet the bountiful heroes, O Indra; strive in the great earthly seat. And where these broad-based streams have stood at the ford with their manly deeds —

**1.169.7**  I hear the approaching thunder of these terrible, impetuous Maruts coming. They who with their helps fling down the mortal who fights against them like a debtor with their rushing charges.

**1.169.8**  Grant us, O Indra, together with the Maruts, all-people riches, the roaring gifts that have cattle at their head. O god, you are praised by those who praise you, by the gods — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.170.1**  There is neither now nor tomorrow — who knows what is wonderful? The mind of another is not to be approached, and even what is well pondered vanishes.

**1.170.2**  Why, O Indra, do you wish to strike us? The Maruts are your brothers. With them act rightly — do not slay us at the encounter.

**1.170.3**  Why, O brother Agastya, being our friend, do you think too highly of yourself? We know your mind — you do not wish to give to us.

**1.170.4**  Let them prepare the altar; let them kindle Agni in front. There, with awareness of the immortal, let us two spread out the sacrifice.

**1.170.5**  You are the lord of riches, O lord of wealth; you are the best bestower of friendship, O lord of friends. O Indra, agree together with the Maruts; thereafter eat the oblations each in due season.

**1.171.1**  I come to you with reverence and with a well-spoken hymn I implore the favour of the swift ones. O Maruts, who rejoice in what is to be known, lay aside your anger and unyoke your horses.

**1.171.2**  This your reverent praise-song, O Maruts, fashioned from the heart, has been set by the mind, O gods. Come hither pleased in mind; for you are indeed those who grow great through worship.

**1.171.3**  May the Maruts, praised, be gracious to us; and may the praised Maghavan be most gracious. May our desirable forests rise upward; may we be victorious, O Maruts, through all the days.

**1.171.4**  Fleeing from this mighty power, trembling in fear of Indra, O Maruts — the oblations that were prepared for you, those I have set far away. Be gracious to us.

**1.171.5**  By whom the cows are made to shine at the dawnings of the perpetual ones with power — do you, O bull, mighty, firm and strong-giving, establish fame for us with the Maruts, together with the fierce ones.

**1.171.6**  Protect us, O Indra, protect the mighty men; together with the Maruts let your anger be appeased. Conquering, holding with clear wisdom — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.172.1**  Radiant be your march and radiant your help, O generous givers! O Maruts shining like serpents!

**1.172.2**  Far from you, O generous Maruts, is the gleaming arrow; far is the stone that you hurl.

**1.172.3**  Turn away the people of Tṛṇaskanda now, O generous givers; raise us up to live.

**1.173.1**  As a bird sings a heavenly sāman, let us praise that which has grown great, possessed of the sun — when the undeceived milch-cows come to the kusa grass, worshipping the heavenly seat.

**1.173.2**  The bull has sung with bulls and with sweet-smelling oblations — as a wild beast goes past those that eat, surpassing. The gladdening, welcome Hotā bears the mind; the worshipful young man bears the pair.

**1.173.3**  The Hotā reaches around the measured seat as he goes, bearing the embryo of the autumns of the earth. The horse neighs and the cow lows as it is led; the speech moves as a messenger between the two worlds.

**1.173.4**  Those of variegated acts bear forward impulses for him who is devoted to the gods. Indra of wondrous brilliance has rejoiced, easy to invoke like the Nāsatyas, standing on the chariot.

**1.173.5**  Praise that Indra who is a real fighter, who is a hero, who is the bountiful one, who stands on his chariot. Most martial even against those who face him, the bull-endowed destroyer even of those hidden in darkness.

**1.173.6**  When thus he is great enough for the heroes, the two worlds are not sufficient for his girth. Indra wrapped around the settlement as the earth does; the self-powerful one supports the branch of the sky.

**1.173.7**  In battles, O hero, let the most liberal among those who are generous be wrapped around you to embrace you. Those who rejoice over Indra together in exhilaration in the domains — and who exult even over the sun-lord with prizes.

**1.173.8**  For thus are your pressings blissful as an ocean in which the divine waters rejoice for you. The cow has been for you in all respects a source of delight — even when with wisdom you seek out the sun-lords among the people.

**1.173.9**  May we be for you as good friends, with our own power of men not to be overcome by praises — may Indra be for us one who stands in praise, swift as one who leads in the work, with songs of praise.

**1.173.10**  The rival among men, not to be overcome by praises — may Indra of the thunderbolt hand be ours. Like those who seek a friend, those who seek the lord of the city, those who have learned the proper way, worship the middle-seeking ones with sacrifices.

**1.173.11**  Indeed, whatever sacrifice enriches Indra, even the one who pours, going around with his mind — as one thirsting does not find his home at the ford, so the long path makes him helpless.

**1.173.12**  Do not abandon us here, O Indra, in battles with the gods; for there is indeed that which wards off your strength. Even the great bountiful one — for whose eager one the Maruts' praise from the oblation-bearer finds its mark.

**1.173.13**  This praise-song, O Indra, is for you from us; by means of it, O bay-horsed one, find a path for us. Turn toward us for our welfare, O god — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.174.1**  You, O Indra, are the king, and the gods protect — protect the men, O Asura, protect us. You are the true lord, O bountiful protector of ours; you are the true one, bringing wealth, the strength-giver.

**1.174.2**  O Indra, you drove away the peoples of hostile speech; you split open the seven autumnal forts for shelter. You released the blameless, wave-like waters for the young man; you subdued Vṛtra for Purukutsa.

**1.174.3**  Drive away, O Indra, the wives of the heroes, and also heaven with them now, O much-invoked one. The Rakṣas, the fire that does not dry up, the overpowering one — like a lion in the house, drive away those active in the night.

**1.174.4**  The remainders rest now in their common womb for your glory, O Indra, by the greatness of the thunderbolt. When in battle he released the rivers that had cattle, the bay horses stood boldly, winning prizes.

**1.174.5**  Carry Kutsa, O Indra, in whom you took delight, in the harness of the Syūmanyu, with the bright horses of the wind. Let the sun roll forward the wheel in the encounter; may the thunderbolt-armed one advance against the rivals.

**1.174.6**  Slaying those who have the friendship of enemies, O Indra, bay-horsed, impelled to give, those who do not offer. Those who, seeing Aryaman of the companions, are carried off by you in the rush, bearing offspring.

**1.174.7**  The wise one stumbled at the battle for the sun; he made the earth a couch for the Dāsa. The bountiful one made the three waters brilliant with gifts; in the dwelling of evil deeds he crushed him who speaks ill.

**1.174.8**  Old as they are, those powers of yours, O Indra, have come as new; strength and cloud, many, to not perish. He split apart the godless forts, not breakable; the weapon bowed not before the taunting of the ungodly.

**1.174.9**  You, O Indra, are the roarer; you have set the roaring waters free, flowing like rivers. When you carry across the ocean, O hero, bring Turvaśa and Yadu over safely.

**1.174.10**  Be you ours always in all ways, O Indra, the most free from entanglement, the protector of men. Do you, the strength-giver of all conflicts, grant us — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.175.1**  The intoxicating exhilaration of your great vessel has been drunk, O bay-horsed one, like that of a great cup. Your drop, O bull among bulls, is the racer that wins the thousandfold prize.

**1.175.2**  Let there come to us your exhilarating, bull-like, desirable intoxication — conquering, O Indra, ever-winning, crushing armies, immortal.

**1.175.3**  For you are the hero, the winner, you who impel the chariot of Manu; conquering, you burn the lawless Dasyu like a vessel with your flame.

**1.175.4**  You who are wise, you stole the sun, wielding the wheel with your might; you brought the weapon to Śuṣṇa, carrying Kutsa with the horses of the wind.

**1.175.5**  For your exhilaration is most vigorous and most glorious, and so is your wisdom — O Vṛtra-slayer, finder of wide space, most glorious, winning most horses.

**1.175.6**  As you have been for the praisers of old, O Indra, like a delight, like waters for one who does not thirst — following that I call you with the Nivid formula: may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.176.1**  Exhilarate Indra for us for the desire of better things, O drop, O bull — enter in! While you roam about raging, you find no enemy near.

**1.176.2**  Into him bestow your songs — him who is the sole one of the tribes. According to his own power the bull is cultivated, as barley is ploughed over and over.

**1.176.3**  In whose two hands lie all the wealth of the five peoples — search out him who is our enemy; smite him like a heavenly thunderbolt.

**1.176.4**  Strike down the non-presser, the equal one, who is distressingly not delightful to you. Give us his treasure; even the sun-lord praises this.

**1.176.5**  You aided him whose doubled might was continuous in the hymns — O drop, in the race you helped the prize-winner of Indra in the prizes.

**1.176.6**  As you have been for the praisers of old, O Indra, like a delight, like waters for one who does not thirst — following that I call you with the Nivid formula: may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.177.1**  Filling the peoples full, the bull of men, the king of the tribes, much-invoked Indra — praised, seeking glory with help — harnessing the two bull-like bay steeds, come hither!

**1.177.2**  Your bulls, O Indra, those bull-like stallions harnessed by prayer, drawing the bull's chariot — mount them and come hither! We invoke you at the pressed soma, O Indra.

**1.177.3**  Mount the bull-chariot, O bull; your soma is pressed, the honey-draughts are poured out. Harnessing your two bull-like bays, O bull of the peoples, come down the slope towards us!

**1.177.4**  This sacrifice is for the god, this is the oblation; these are the prayers, this is the soma, O Indra. The kusa grass is strewn — come forward, O mighty one; sit and drink; unyoke your bays here.

**1.177.5**  Come hither, O well-praised Indra, to the prayers of Mānyasya the poet. May we know the help at dawn as we praise — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.178.1**  If, O Indra, you indeed have that readiness to hear by which you have been the helper of praisers — do not burn up our desire when it is growing great; may we attain all the things you have, with the life-span.

**1.178.2**  Truly no king, O Indra, has deceived us — those who are now making sisters in the womb. Even the waters, easily flowing, have gone for him; Indra has come with friendship and with birds.

**1.178.3**  Indra, the hero, conquers in battles with men; he hears the call of the poet who implores. He brings the chariot near to the worshipper — if the raised songs should come of themselves.

**1.178.4**  Thus Indra with men, through desire for good fame, becomes a bold helper against those who are hostile. In the encounter of courses he is praised with voice; the all-achieving glory of the sacrificer is proclaimed.

**1.178.5**  Through you, O Maghavan Indra, may we overpower the enemies who think themselves great. Be you our protector; be you also for our increase — may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.179.1**  I have grown weary through many autumns, through nights and days that make the dawns old. Old age diminishes the beauty of bodies — even so now the wives should have gone to their bull-husbands.

**1.179.2**  Even those former ones who were watchers of ṛta, who declared the sacred truths together with the gods — even those came down, for they never reached the end. Now too the wives have come together with their bull-husbands.

**1.179.3**  Not in vain does the weary one whom the gods help — we two shall win all contests. Here let us win the hundred-guided race as the pair of us, united as a couple, have rushed forward together.

**1.179.4**  The desire of the roaring, pressing one has come to me — born here, from here, or from wherever. Lopāmudrā draws the bull downward; the rash one sucks the wise man as he sleeps.

**1.179.5**  This soma now drunk close by and deep in the heart I invoke. Whatever transgression we have committed, may that be forgiven by him — for mortal man has many desires.

**1.179.6**  Agastya, digging with his tools, desiring offspring, progeny and strength — the mighty seer has nourished both castes, and has gone to the gods with true prayers fulfilled.

**1.180.1**  Your realms, your well-yoked horses, your chariot — when it flies around the seas for you two — with golden rims sprinkling you, drinking the honey, you two follow the dawns.

**1.180.2**  You two go beyond the stallion when you go to the manly one whose wings spread wide, worthy of reverence. When the sister who is praised by all brings you two, O honey-drinkers, for the prize and the refreshment.

**1.180.3**  You two placed the milk in the cow Uśriyā, the ripe within the raw, the ancient one. When among the trees the truthful-dripping one calls upon you two — as a clear, unwinding one — the oblation-bearer worships.

**1.180.4**  You two have indeed chosen the honey-sweet warm drink for Atri, like waters for the flood to approach. That, O Aśvins, O heroes, seeking cattle — like chariot wheels your praises return with honey.

**1.180.5**  I would turn toward you two for your gift, O wondrous Aśvins, like a worn old man aided by Tugra's son. The great earth follows the waters of you two; worn out, your eye, O worshipful ones, has suffered from distress.

**1.180.6**  When you two set down your teams and let loose the mighty lady of intelligence with your own powers, O generous givers — then the sun-lord comes forward and backward like the wind; the good-vowed one obtains a great prize.

**1.180.7**  We too, being truly your praisers, proclaim you to be true; the chanter is well-placed. And indeed, O blameless Aśvins, you two drink; for you two bulls drink right up to the god.

**1.180.8**  Indeed you two Aśvins for days in the winning of the stream of Virudra — Agastya, praised among men as a hero among heroes, has illuminated you like a Kārādhunī with thousands.

**1.180.9**  When you two carry forward with the greatness of the chariot, when you two race like the Hotā of Manu — grant good horses to the sun-lords and wealth; may we be winners of riches, O Nāsatyas.

**1.180.10**  That chariot of yours, O Aśvins, we would invoke today anew with praises for our welfare — it whose rim is unhurt, going around heaven: may we know the refreshment, the broad space giving long life.

**1.181.1**  O most beloved, you who officiate as adhvaryus among wealth and treasure, when you lift the waters — this sacrifice has made your glory known, O you who hold riches, O guides of the people.

**1.181.2**  May your bright, milk-drinking steeds, swift as the wind, those heavenly coursers with backs unsaddled — thought-swift, vigorous bulls — bearing heavenly sovereignty, bring you Aśvins hither.

**1.181.3**  May your chariot, like a rushing river, with a yoke that glides forward, come hither for our welfare. It is swifter than thought, carrying the vigorous bull — that adorable, zealous one who presses forward first.

**1.181.4**  Born here and there, both have called out together with sinless body and with their own names. One of you is the conquering hero, patron of the bountiful sacrifice; the other, fortunate son of heaven, is glorified in the chariot race.

**1.181.5**  Your Kakuha mare, tawny in hue, has gone forward following the steeds to your dwelling-places. The bay horses of one of you are fattened with prizes; the sky-regions resound, O Aśvins, with your songs of victory.

**1.181.6**  Like a vigorous bull in autumn, your chariot has sped forth, seeking refreshment, traversing many food-offerings laden with honey. With like prizes the streams have come to us like rivers flowing upward, fattening the other.

**1.181.7**  A firm and wise hymn has been sent forth to you, O Aśvins, flowing in three streams through the sacrifice. O you who are praised, protect the supplicant — whether in the course of the journey or at rest, hear my call.

**1.181.8**  Moreover, that bright, radiant hymn of yours fills men at the triple sacrificial seat. The rain-cloud, O vigorous ones, has swelled for you both, like a cow's milk, pouring forth for the worshipper of Manu.

**1.181.9**  The full-purposed worshipper praises you two, O Aśvins, like Pūṣan, like Agni at the dawns, bearing oblations. As I call to you, singing your praises, seeking wide space — may we know the winning path and the life-giving community.

**1.182.1**  This course has now appeared — come, O wise ones, adorn the vigorous chariot, rejoice! O you who impel thought, you who are zealous, who possess the riches of Viśpalā, O sons of heaven — you of good deeds and pure vows.

**1.182.2**  For you are the most Indra-like, the most zealous, the most Marut-like, O wonder-workers, the most skilled in deeds, the best of charioteers. You drive a chariot laden full of honey; with that chariot, O Aśvins, come to the worshipper.

**1.182.3**  What do you do here, O wonder-workers? What person who offers no oblations is glorified in your presence? You shall pass beyond the niggard Paṇi; restore the life-breath to the sage, and make light for the eloquent singer.

**1.182.4**  Crush on all sides the hounds that bark and snap; strike them down, O Aśvins — you have discovered those things. Make every hymn of the praiser yield riches; both of you, O Nāsatyās, uphold my praise.

**1.182.5**  You made a raft for Tugra's son upon the rivers — a self-propelled raft with wings. With that raft, guided by divine thought, you flew well, soaring over the great waves.

**1.182.6**  The son of Tugra had been cast down into the waters, cast into darkness that offers no foothold. Four ships favored by Jaṭhala, driven by the Aśvins, carried him across.

**1.182.7**  What tree stood firm in the midst of the ocean, to which Taugra's son clung without hold? Just as the feathers of a flying bird, the Aśvins carried him upward — to Bhujyu for his glory.

**1.182.8**  May that, O heroes, O Nāsatyās, follow you — what the sages of inspired mind have uttered in praise of you. From this soma-session today, may we know the winning path and the life-giving community.

**1.183.1**  Harness that steed which is swifter than thought — the three-felied, three-wheeled chariot of the vigorous pair — by which you come near to the house of the good man; you fly with the three-fold element, like a bird with wings.

**1.183.2**  Your well-wheeled chariot rolls on toward the earth; when you stand, O wise ones, at the refreshments offered — let beauty attend beauty: may this hymn be with you, and may you Aśvins accompany Uṣas, the daughter of heaven.

**1.183.3**  Mount the well-wheeled chariot that rolls according to your ordinances, that bears the oblation. By which, O heroes, O Nāsatyās, you travel the path for nourishment, for offspring, and for the soul's own sake.

**1.183.4**  May neither wolf nor she-wolf attack you; do not turn away from us, and do not abandon us. This is the share laid up for you; this is the hymn, O wonder-workers — these are the honey-filled treasures deposited for you.

**1.183.5**  Gotama, Purumīḷha, Atri — the oblation-bearer calls on you two, O wonder-workers, for help. Like one going straight toward a pointed direction, come near to my call, O Nāsatyās.

**1.183.6**  We have crossed to the far shore of this darkness; the hymn of praise has been established for you, O Aśvins. Come here by the god-travelled paths — may we know the winning path and the life-giving community.

**1.184.1**  I, the carrier, invoke you both today and again at the dawning of Uṣas with praises. O Nāsatyās, wherever you may be, O noble sons of heaven, come to the generous giver.

**1.184.2**  O vigorous pair, do rejoice among us; strike down the stingy Paṇis in your exhilaration. Be heard by me — O heroes, discerning ones, be sought out with the ear through the utterances of my thoughts.

**1.184.3**  For glory, like Pūṣan and the arrow-maker among the gods, O Nāsatyās, bring the marriage of Sūryā. Your Kakuha mare, born of the waters, is celebrated — like the ancient yokes of mighty Varuṇa.

**1.184.4**  May that sweet gift of yours be for us; impel the hymn of the praiser who deserves honor. While the peoples rejoice, O munificent ones renowned in glory, may they rejoice in heroic strength through you.

**1.184.5**  This hymn has been made for you, O Aśvins, O bountiful ones, with songs of praise and well-wrought verses. Come, rejoicing, O Nāsatyās, to the path for offspring and for the self — for Agastya's sake.

**1.184.6**  We have crossed to the far shore of this darkness; the hymn of praise has been established for you, O Aśvins. Come here by the god-travelled paths — may we know the winning path and the life-giving community.

**1.185.1**  Which of the two is the earlier and which the later of these two? How were they born? Who among the wise truly knows? They bear all of creation within themselves by name; the two days revolve like wheels.

**1.185.2**  The two ever-moving ones carry the many and support the moving — the footed embryo held by the footless. Like a son forever in the lap of his parents — O Heaven and Earth, protect us from calamity.

**1.185.3**  I invoke the faultless, unopposed gift of Aditi, radiant and inexhaustible, worthy of reverence. May both the great ones engender that for the singer — O Heaven and Earth, protect us from calamity.

**1.185.4**  May we be near the two divine daughters, the pair of worlds that are heated without distress, filled with refreshment. Both belong to the gods, both on both types of days — O Heaven and Earth, protect us from calamity.

**1.185.5**  The two young women, sisters and kindred, come together, meeting in the lap of their father; sniffing toward the navel of the world — O Heaven and Earth, protect us from calamity.

**1.185.6**  I invoke the two vast, great, ordinance-firm dwelling-places, mothers of the gods, for their divine aid. They who sustain the immortal in good aspect — O Heaven and Earth, protect us from calamity.

**1.185.7**  The wide and broad, the many-folded ones with distant ends — I address them with reverence at this sacrifice. They who sustain good fortune, who are easy to approach — O Heaven and Earth, protect us from calamity.

**1.185.8**  Whatever sin we have committed against the gods, against a comrade or against the house-lord ever-faithful — may this prayer of ours be the atonement for them. O Heaven and Earth, protect us from calamity.

**1.185.9**  May both manly praises strengthen me; may both accompany me with protection and support. Among many a noble lord, may the gods rejoice in the liberal giver — and may we press forward with vigor, O gods.

**1.185.10**  I, of good understanding, spoke to Heaven and Earth first, so that they might hear this truth. May father and mother protect us from blame and from ill-going near at hand, with their aids.

**1.185.11**  O Heaven and Earth, O Father and Mother — may what I address to you here be true. May we be foremost among the gods with their aids — may we know the winning path and the life-giving community.

**1.186.1**  Let divine Savitṛ, the universal lord, come to us at the festival with praises and fine speech, so that you, O youthful ones, may delight in us with inspired thought at the gathering of all the world.

**1.186.2**  May all the gods come to us with all the horses — Mitra, Aryaman, and Varuṇa together in accord, so that all may prosper us, making us triumphant over the unstable one, like strength.

**1.186.3**  Together with Turvaṇi I praise Agni, your dearest guest, with hymns. May glorious Varuṇa be ours, and may the patron, praised of foes, conduct us to refreshments.

**1.186.4**  I come near to them both with reverence, desiring conquest — Dawn and Night, like an easily milked cow. On the same day I measure out the hymn, the milk flowing from their one udder in different forms.

**1.186.5**  May Ahir Budhnya bring us joy, and may the river Sindhu come like a suckling nursing her young — the river by which we impel the offspring of the waters, the vigorous steed, swift as thought, whom they carry.

**1.186.6**  May Tvaṣṭṛ too come toward us together with the patrons at the gathering, united in purpose. May Indra the Vṛtra-slayer, who fills all peoples, the most mighty of men, come to us here.

**1.186.7**  Also our thoughts, harnessed like horses, lick the young one like cows licking a calf. Hymns praise him as wives praise their husband — him, the most fragrant among men.

**1.186.8**  May the Maruts too, with swelling armies, together with the two worlds, sit down among us in one accord — they whose steeds are dappled, rolling like rivers, consuming enemies, yoked as with Mitra, like gods.

**1.186.9**  Truly now their greatness shines forth; the draft animals are harnessed with excellent harnessing. And when on a fair day their armies scatter the arrow of destruction, they sprinkle all the desert.

**1.186.10**  Send forth the Aśvins for protection; send forth Pūṣan — for they are truly self-strong. May I turn to the gods — non-hostile Viṣṇu, Vāta, Ṛbhukṣan — seeking their benevolence.

**1.186.11**  May this thought of ours for you, O adorable ones, be life-giving and settled among you — this thought of one who strives after wealth among the gods. May we know the winning path and the life-giving community.

**1.187.1**  Now I shall praise the food — great, law-sustaining, vigorous — by whose strength Trita crushed the many-jointed Vṛtra with force.

**1.187.2**  O sweet food, O honeyed food — we have chosen you. Be our protector.

**1.187.3**  Come to us, O food; approach with auspicious aids, bringing bliss. Be a friend free of hatred, charming, without duplicity.

**1.187.4**  Your savors, O food, are spread out through the realms, resting there like winds in the sky.

**1.187.5**  Yours, O food, are they who give; yours, O food, is the sweetest. The savors, swelling in taste, surge forward like those with great necks.

**1.187.6**  In you, O food, the mind of the great gods is established. With your lovely banner, one has slain Ahi with your aid.

**1.187.7**  When you, O food, went to the Vivasvat of the mountains, even there, O sweet food, come to us sufficiently for nourishment.

**1.187.8**  When we partake of the essence of the waters and plants, O Vātāpe, become fat indeed.

**1.187.9**  When we partake of you, O Soma, whether mixed with milk or mixed with barley, O Vātāpe, become fat indeed.

**1.187.10**  Be porridge, O herb; be fat in the kidneys of the charioteer. O Vātāpe, become fat indeed.

**1.187.11**  You, O food, we have refined with our words, as cows press out what is to be offered. For the gods you are a shared feast; for us too you are a shared feast.

**1.188.1**  Kindled today you reign as god among the gods, winning thousands. O Agni, wise seer and messenger, carry the oblations.

**1.188.2**  By Tanūnapāt, the sacrifice is anointed with honey as the truth-seeker proceeds; he bestows thousandfold refreshments.

**1.188.3**  You who are praised and worthy of hymns, being invoked, bring the worshipful gods hither. O Agni, you are the winner of thousands.

**1.188.4**  With might they have strewn the eastward-facing sacred grass, that has a thousand heroes — there where the Ādityas reign.

**1.188.5**  She who is the virāj, she who is the samrāj, the pervasive and the powerful, the many and the yet more numerous — from the doors the streams of ghee have flowed.

**1.188.6**  Well-decked and well-adorned, they two shine in beauty. May the two Dawns sit down here.

**1.188.7**  For the first two are the divine priestly callers, the poets of fair speech. May they sacrifice this sacrifice of ours.

**1.188.8**  Bhāratī, Iḷā, and Sarasvatī — all of you I address here. May those three spur us on to glory.

**1.188.9**  For Tvaṣṭṛ is the lord of all forms; he has assembled all cattle. Sacrifice their increase for us.

**1.188.10**  O Vanaspati, release the offering silently for the gods. Let Agni taste the oblations.

**1.188.11**  Agni goes before the gods and is anointed with the Gāyatrī metre; he shines bright in the svāhā oblations.

**1.189.1**  O Agni, lead us by the good path to wealth, O god who knows all the ways. Ward off from us the sin that lurks and misleads — we would render to you the most frequent utterance of homage.

**1.189.2**  O Agni, as one renewed, carry us across all difficulties with well-being; and the wide, broad earth be for us spacious. Be a source of peace and healing for our children and our progeny.

**1.189.3**  O Agni, ward off from us the disease; peoples without fire have afflicted us. O god, O adorable one, restore the earth to us for well-being together with all the immortals.

**1.189.4**  Protect us, O Agni, with unceasing guards; and shining bright in the beloved home — may your praiser know no fear now or in future, O youngest and most powerful.

**1.189.5**  Do not abandon us, O Agni, to the ill-intentioned foe, to the adversary or to misfortune. Do not hand us over to him who bites, who stings, who intoxicates, who harms, O powerful one.

**1.189.6**  Truly one like you, O Agni born of truth, being praised, stretches out protection for his body against all who would harm or lead astray — for you, O god, are the guardian against what comes upon us crookedly.

**1.189.7**  O Agni, knowing both classes of mortals, you seek them both at the hour of setting forth. At the time of arrival you are to be instructed by Manu — purified and burnished by those who desire, the bird of heaven.

**1.189.8**  We have spoken these set hymns in this Agni of Māna's son, the powerful one. May we win a thousand with the ṛṣis — may we know the winning path and the life-giving community.

**1.190.1**  Increase Bṛhaspati with new hymns — he who is unopposed, vigorous, sweet-tongued. He is the singer of songs, of brilliant radiance, whose new praises the gods and mortals listen to.

**1.190.2**  The timely words attend on him, as a rush of men seeking the gods has been let loose. For Bṛhaspati straightway opened the highest realms; Mātariśvan joined together in the rite.

**1.190.3**  He sends forth praise and the rising of homage and sound, like Savitṛ extending his arms. By his power, the terrible one who is to be slain in battle, like a fearsome beast, without demonic protection — yet mighty.

**1.190.4**  His praise flies through heaven and earth; the discerning bearer of the divine strikes like a racehorse. The arrows of the deer — these fly here; Bṛhaspati's magical powers against enemies flash toward the sky.

**1.190.5**  The evil Pajras who think of you as a godless one — they wickedly live off what is good. You do not grant a boon to the deceitful one, O Bṛhaspati; you punish the despiser.

**1.190.6**  Like a path to good pasture, easy to travel; like Mitra fully satisfied, hard to lead astray — those who are unopposed and look upon us have stood forth, opening what was veiled.

**1.190.7**  To whom praises converge like rivers flowing to the ocean with wheel-like banks — he, Bṛhaspati, the eager one, beholds both things from within: what is beyond and what are the waters.

**1.190.8**  Thus has Bṛhaspati, great, mightily born, mighty, vigorous, been placed as the divine bull. May he, when praised, grant us abundance of heroes and cattle — may we know the winning path and the life-giving community.

**1.191.1**  The Kaṅkata, and the one that is not Kaṅkata, and then the true Kaṅkata — saying 'two' and 'scorching,' the unseen ones have clung fast.

**1.191.2**  Coming, she slays the unseen ones; going away, she slays them too. Crushing down, she slays them; grinding, she grinds them.

**1.191.3**  Śara grasses, Kuśara grasses, Darbha grasses, and also the Sairya and Mauñja reeds — all the hostile unseen ones have together clung fast.

**1.191.4**  The cows have settled in the cowpen; the wild animals have lain down; the banners of the peoples have been lowered — the unseen ones have clung fast.

**1.191.5**  Those there have been seen at dusk, like thieves. O unseen ones, seen-by-all — you have been caught awake.

**1.191.6**  Heaven is your father, Earth your mother, Soma your brother, Aditi your sister. O unseen ones, seen-by-all — stay still and be at peace.

**1.191.7**  Those in the shoulders, those in the limbs, those needle-like, those that are called Kaṅkata — O unseen ones, whatever you are here, all together perish.

**1.191.8**  The sun rises in the east, seen by all, the slayer of the unseen. Crushing all the unseen ones and all the sorceries.

**1.191.9**  That sun has flown up, consuming all the many things; the Āditya from the mountains, seen by all, the slayer of the unseen.

**1.191.10**  I fasten the poison to the sun, to the leather flask in the house of the soma-presser. It shall not now die, nor shall we die — far from it is the yoking. The golden one has made you sweet, O honey; the bee has made you honey.

**1.191.11**  The little iyattika bird, together with the śakuntikā bird, has eaten your poison. It shall not now die, nor shall we die — far from it is the yoking. The golden one has made you sweet, O honey; the bee has made you honey.

**1.191.12**  Thrice seven viṣpuliṅkā birds have sucked up the flower of the poison. They shall not now die, nor shall we die — far from it is the yoking. The golden one has made you sweet, O honey; the bee has made you honey.

**1.191.13**  Of the nine, and of the ninety, of the festering poisons — I have seized the names of all of them. Far from it is the yoking. The golden one has made you sweet, O honey; the bee has made you honey.

**1.191.14**  Thrice seven peahens, the seven unmarried sisters — they have carried off your poison like water-carrying women carrying pots.

**1.191.15**  This little kuṣumbhaka — I split open the taka with a stone. From it the poison has flowed back, flowing away along the valleys.

**1.191.16**  The kuṣumbhaka, rolling down from the mountain, declared: 'The poison of the scorpion is without power — without power is your poison, O scorpion.'
